a date later than the current Philippine business date is rejected; no artificial lower bound is imposed on how far in the past it may fall. `expectedUpdatedAt` is required on every request, is a strict ISO-8601 UTC timestamp string with exactly three-digit millisecond precision (matching what `Date.prototype.toISOString()` always produces), is used only as a concurrency comparison token (Section 7), and is never persisted to the `Client` row or written to any `AuditLog` entry. Every editable field omitted from the request body retains its current stored value — Prisma's `update` skips any key whose value is `undefined`, and this schema relies on that convention unchanged. After combining the Client's existing `email`/`phone` with whatever the request supplies for those two keys, the resulting Client must retain at least one of `email` or `phone`; violating this returns `VALIDATION_ERROR` with the exact message "A client must retain at least one of email or phone." A request whose editable-field values, once combined with the existing record, are identical to the currently stored values is a permitted no-op: it may still succeed, but only after `expectedUpdatedAt` and authorization are both independently verified first — a no-op request carrying a stale `expectedUpdatedAt` still returns `CLIENT_CONFLICT`, never a silent success — and a genuine no-op success performs no `Client` row write and creates no `AuditLog` entry (Section 8).

  **6. Contact normalization and duplicate detection.** The exact `normalizeEmail`/`normalizePhone` algorithms D-022 §4 approved are preserved unchanged; their single authoritative implementation moves to `apps/web/src/lib/contact-normalization.ts`, with its test moved alongside it. Every existing import updates to the new path, including `features/leads/service.ts`'s static import and `features/leads/service.integration.test.ts`'s dynamic `import('./normalize')` (updated to the new path). `features/leads/normalize.ts` is removed outright once the move is complete — no re-export shim is retained, and neither algorithm is duplicated or behaviorally altered by the move. Repository ownership of duplicate-query primitives is unchanged and stays split: the existing Lead/conversion duplicate-query primitives (`findDuplicateLeadMatches`, `findDuplicateClientMatches`, `createClientFromLead`, `findClientSummaryById`) remain owned by `features/leads/repository.ts`, exactly as D-024 established; Client-edit duplicate queries — a new, self-excluding Client-vs-Client match query (excluding the Client's own id) and a new, self-excluding Client-vs-Lead match query (excluding any Lead whose `clientId` already equals the Client being edited, while still matching an unlinked Lead or a Lead linked to any other Client) — belong to `features/clients/repository.ts`, with each exclusion applied directly inside the repository query itself, never by fetching unfiltered results and excluding them afterward. This decision does not convert the D-024 conversion primitives into general Client-management primitives, and does not add a Client-scoped duplicate query to `features/leads`. During a Client `PATCH`, duplicate detection reruns whenever the `email` or `phone` key is supplied in the request, even when the resulting combined value is unchanged from the existing record — mirroring `features/leads/service.ts`'s `updateLead`'s identical `contactProvided` behavior exactly. The rerun normalizes the final combined contact values, checks other Clients (excluding the Client being edited via the new self-excluding query), and checks Leads other than the Client's own originating Lead(s) — excluding, via that same repository-level filter, any Lead whose `clientId` already equals the Client being edited, while still checking every unlinked Lead and every Lead linked to a different Client — reusing D-022's existing duplicate-reporting shape and visibility rules unchanged: an authorized match exposes only the existing allow-listed summary (`id`, `fullName`, matched channel(s), plus `status` for a Lead match); an inaccessible match collapses into `restrictedMatchDetected: true` with no identity, metadata, or count ever leaked; multiple matches remain an array, never auto-selected. Repository, service, and real-PostgreSQL integration coverage must prove that a Client's own originating Lead(s) are never returned as an accessible duplicate match and never collapsed into `restrictedMatchDetected`, while a genuinely unrelated Lead sharing a normalized email or phone with the Client is still reported normally under the existing visibility rules. Duplicate matches found during a Client `PATCH` are advisory and non-blocking — unlike D-024's conversion-time blocking behavior, an ordinary Client edit is never rejected because of a duplicate match, and duplicate detection therefore never produces its own error response during `PATCH`. This decision preserves D-024's existing rule that linking a Lead to an existing Client during conversion never overwrites any field on that Client — Client `PATCH` is a wholly separate, later, independently authorized action, and nothing in this decision alters D-024's conversion-time behavior in any way.

  **7. Optimistic concurrency and transaction behavior.** Every `PATCH` request requires `expectedUpdatedAt` (Section 5). The mutation runs inside a plain Prisma transaction (`prisma.$transaction(async (tx) => {...})`), mirroring `features/leads/service.ts`'s existing `updateLead` precedent for ordinary-field edits — not the `runSerializableWithRetry` helper `updateLeadStatus`/`convertLead` use for genuine multi-branch lifecycle-transition races, since no repository or implementation evidence shows a Client field edit needs that stronger isolation. Inside that transaction, in order: for a `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT` actor only, the active Client assignment is transaction-locally rechecked via the existing, unchanged `assignmentRepository.findActiveAssignmentForClient(tx, id)`, mirroring D-024 §6(b)'s identical transaction-local-recheck precedent — an `ADMIN_MANAGER` actor requires no such recheck, remaining unconditionally authorized once the Client's existence is confirmed; the authoritative current Client row is read, both to compute the combined-field invariant and duplicate-detection inputs (Sections 5–6) and to distinguish disappearance from staleness below; `expectedUpdatedAt` is verified against the persisted `updatedAt` even when the submitted editable-field values are unchanged (Section 5's no-op case); a genuine changed write is applied atomically by including both the Client's `id` and the expected `updatedAt` value in the update predicate (`updateMany({ where: { id, updatedAt: expectedUpdatedAt }, data })`), so the staleness comparison and the write itself are a single atomic database operation rather than a separate read-then-write pair; if that predicate matches no row, existence is re-checked inside the same transaction to distinguish a genuinely missing Client from a merely stale `expectedUpdatedAt`, preserving the same role-aware anti-enumeration split Section 9 defines rather than weakening it; on a successful write, the authoritative persisted Client row is re-read and returned; and the `CLIENT_UPDATED` audit entry (Section 8) is appended in the same transaction. The resulting outcomes are: a Client missing for an `ADMIN_MANAGER` actor returns `CLIENT_NOT_FOUND` (404, "Client not found."); a Client that is nonexistent or inaccessible for a `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT` actor returns `CLIENT_FORBIDDEN` (403, "You do not have access to this client.") — indistinguishable from one another, exactly mirroring `LeadError`'s established anti-enumeration convention; a `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT`'s assignment lost between the initial authorization check and the transaction-local recheck likewise returns `CLIENT_FORBIDDEN` (403, the same message); and an `expectedUpdatedAt` mismatch against a Client that does still exist and remains accessible returns `CLIENT_CONFLICT` (409, "This client has changed since it was last loaded. Refresh and try again."). `CLIENT_CONFLICT` never returns raw Client state or any other PII detail — code and message only, matching the established convention that only `VALIDATION_ERROR` ever carries a structured `details` array. On success, the `updatedAt` value on the returned, persisted Client becomes the UI's new concurrency baseline for its next `PATCH` request.

  **8. Audit contract.** A new `CLIENT_UPDATED` `AuditLog` action is added, using the existing, unchanged append-only `AuditLog` convention (`entityType: 'Client'`, `entityId`: the Client's `id`). Its closed snapshot contains only `changedFields` (field names only, in detection order), `hasEmail`, and `hasPhone` — mirroring `sanitizeLeadUpdateSnapshot`'s exact allow-listed shape and rationale (`features/leads/audit.ts`). `expectedUpdatedAt` is never audited, matching the established convention that a concurrency/transition token (e.g. Lead's `expectedStatus`) never appears in its own mutation's audit snapshot. Raw `fullName`, `email`, `phone`, `address`, `nationality`, `dateOfBirth`, `emergencyContact`, and `notes` values must never be written to `AuditLog`, in either `beforeState` or `afterState` — only field names and the two contact-presence flags. A no-op `PATCH` (Section 5) creates no `AuditLog` entry. The `CLIENT_UPDATED` audit insert and the Client row update occur inside the same transaction described in Section 7 — never as a separate, later write.

  **9. API and error contract.** This decision authorizes exactly three new routes: `GET /api/clients`, `GET /api/clients/[id]`, and `PATCH /api/clients/[id]`. No `POST` or `DELETE` Client resource route is authorized by this decision (Section 1). Every response uses the established `{ error: { code, message, details? } }` envelope. Controlled error codes and their HTTP status: `VALIDATION_ERROR` (400), `ROLE_NOT_PERMITTED` (403, reusing `LeadError`'s/`AssignmentError`'s existing wording and status for this identical service-layer defense-in-depth check), `CLIENT_FORBIDDEN` (403), `CLIENT_NOT_FOUND` (404), `CLIENT_CONFLICT` (409), and `INTERNAL_ERROR` (500, the existing generic unhandled-error mapping `withRole` already provides). Successful responses are: list — `200 { items, page, pageSize, total }` (Section 3); detail — `200 { client }`, `client` using Section 4's exact shape (Section 4); `PATCH` — exactly `200 { client, duplicateMatches?, restrictedMatchDetected? }`, where `client` uses Section 4's exact shape (with the fresh, post-write `updatedAt`). `duplicateMatches` (an array) and `restrictedMatchDetected` (a boolean) are present together, as a pair, only when the request supplied `email` or `phone`, exactly mirroring `UpdateLeadResult`'s existing `Partial<DuplicateMatchResult>` convention; when the request supplied neither `email` nor `phone`, both keys are omitted from the response entirely — never present as `null` or an empty placeholder. The existing `PUT /api/clients/[id]/assignment` and `DELETE /api/clients/[id]/assignment` routes are unchanged by this decision — same authorization, same request/response shape, same error codes.

  **10. Admin UI.** This decision authorizes `/admin/clients` (a paginated, searchable Client list) and `/admin/clients/[id]` (Client detail), each independently re-authorizing the actor exactly as `/admin/leads` and `/admin/leads/[id]` already do (the same three-layer defense-in-depth: `proxy.ts`'s cookie-presence check, `admin/layout.tsx`'s staff-role check, and this page's own `ADMIN_MANAGER`/`TRAVEL_CONSULTANT` narrowing). The detail page includes an `EditClientForm` that uses the server-returned Client as its authoritative baseline — including the returned `updatedAt` as the next submission's `expectedUpdatedAt` — never assuming success from submitted values alone, and that surfaces a `CLIENT_CONFLICT` response by requiring the user to refresh and retry rather than silently retrying or overwriting. The detail page also includes a read-only originating-Lead history list (Section 4) and a Client assignment panel: for `ADMIN_MANAGER`, the panel renders the existing assign/reassign/end-assignment controls calling the existing, unchanged `PUT`/`DELETE /api/clients/[id]/assignment` routes and `features/assignments/service.ts` functions directly — no second `StaffAssignment` write path is introduced; for `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT`, the panel renders the current assignment information read-only, with no interactive control of any kind. This decision does not implement the parallel, still-missing Lead assignment-management UI (Section 1). The generic `Pagination` component moves from `apps/web/src/app/admin/leads/_components/Pagination.tsx` to `apps/web/src/app/admin/_components/Pagination.tsx`, with its test moved alongside it; the existing Lead pages' imports are updated to the new path; both the Lead and the new Client pages use this one relocated implementation — no second `Pagination` implementation is created.

  **11. Testing contract.** Automated coverage is required at every existing tier before this checkpoint is considered complete: schema tests covering every field's exact limit, trimming, and clearing behavior, `.strict()`'s unknown-key rejection, `expectedUpdatedAt`'s exact ISO-8601 format, and `dateOfBirth`'s exact Gregorian-calendar validity — including a valid leap day (`2024-02-29`), an invalid non-leap-year February 29 (`2025-02-29`), and an impossible day-of-month (e.g. `2026-04-31`), each rejected without rollover or normalization — together with its Philippine-business-date future-date rejection; the request schema alone cannot know the Client's existing stored `email`/`phone` values, so the combined email/phone retention invariant is not a schema-test responsibility — its authoritative coverage instead lives in service tests and real-PostgreSQL integration tests (below); repository tests covering the assignment-scoped list query's composition into `where` (never fetch-then-filter), deterministic `createdAt`/`id` ordering, narrow field selects at both the list and detail tiers, the new self-excluding Client duplicate-match query, and the Lead-assignment-scoped originating-Leads query; service tests covering both roles' list/read/`PATCH` authorization, the no-op success path (including a stale no-op still returning `CLIENT_CONFLICT`), the combined-contact invariant, duplicate-match reporting and its visibility shaping, stale-write rejection, and transaction-local assignment-loss handling; error and audit-sanitizer tests; route tests covering every role gate, validation failures, success shapes, and the `CLIENT_NOT_FOUND`/`CLIENT_FORBIDDEN` anti-enumeration split; real-PostgreSQL integration tests guarded by the existing `heritage_v3_test`/`validateTestDatabaseUrl` safety discipline (`features/leads/service.integration.test.ts`'s established pattern), covering Admin-versus-Travel-Consultant visibility, the combined stored/submitted email-or-phone retention invariant verified against real persisted Client rows, transaction-local assignment loss, concurrent stale writes, duplicate-metadata restriction, PII-free `AuditLog` rows (asserting the serialized audit content never contains a submitted contact/profile value, mirroring the existing `service.integration.test.ts` assertion style), originating-Lead traceability, and continued compatibility with D-024's conversion behavior; and page/component tests covering list/detail/edit loading, empty, error, permission-denied, and success states, duplicate-match warnings, `CLIENT_CONFLICT` handling, and the assignment panel's role-based rendering (an `ADMIN_MANAGER` sees interactive controls; a `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT` sees a read-only display only, with no interactive control rendered at all). The relocated `contact-normalization` test (Section 6) must preserve its current assertions unchanged, including D-022 §4's exact four-format phone-equivalence case, proving the move introduces no behavioral change.

  **12. Task Board handling.** This decision does not itself modify `docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md` — it is a contract-only entry, authorizing implementation but not asserting that implementation, its tests, or its real-PostgreSQL verification already exist or pass. Any future Task Board update marking the Phase 2 "Client management backend and UI" checklist item, or the combined `canAccessLead`/`canAccessClient`-wiring checklist item, complete must be made separately, only once the implementation and its required coverage (Section 11) actually exist and pass. The combined Lead/Client staff-assignment-workflow checklist item must remain incomplete after this checkpoint if the Lead assignment-management UI (Section 1's explicit deferral) is still absent, regardless of how completely the Client half is implemented.

- **Rationale:** Reusing `canAccessClient`, the existing assignment service/routes, the standard `{ error: { code, message } }` envelope, and D-022's duplicate-detection and audit-sanitizer conventions keeps Client Management consistent with every already-reviewed Lead, Booking, and Assignment mutation rather than inventing a parallel authorization or persistence mechanism. Restricting Client creation to the existing D-024 conversion path, and deferring `Client.status`, the Lead assignment UI, and every downstream-record summary, preserves blueprint Section 5.1's non-automatic-creation rule and avoids building ahead of checkpoints (Proposal/ROS, Booking UI, Payments, Documents, Visa Cases, the Lead assignment UI) that remain separately unauthorized. Embedding the `expectedUpdatedAt` comparison directly in the update predicate, rather than forcing every Client edit through the serializable-retry helper reserved for genuine multi-branch lifecycle races, keeps ordinary Client editing at the same transactional weight as Lead's own already-reviewed `updateLead`, while still closing the concurrent-stale-write gap Lead's own ordinary-field edit never needed to address. Relocating `normalizeEmail`/`normalizePhone` to a neutral shared module, without duplicating either algorithm or leaving a re-export shim behind, and relocating the shared `Pagination` component, keep the codebase's cross-feature-reuse discipline (`.claude/rules/architecture.md`) intact now that a second feature genuinely needs both.
- **Constraint:** No `schema.prisma` change and no migration are authorized or required by this decision — `Client`, its normalized contact fields, its optional profile fields, its `updatedAt` column, `StaffAssignment`'s existing `clientId` target, and `AuditLog` are all already migrated, per migration `20260714024613_lead_client_assignment_foundation` (`Client`/`StaffAssignment`) and migration `20260713062622_staff_role_management_foundation` (`AuditLog`). No existing API path, request shape, or authorization rule is renamed or altered: `/api/leads/**`, `/api/clients/[id]/assignment`, and every other existing route are reused unchanged. This decision does not resolve `Client.status` (D-021's open item (b), still open), does not implement Proposal/ROS, Booking UI, Payments, Documents, Visa Cases, the client portal, or the Lead assignment-management UI, and does not itself write any application code, schema, or migration.
- **Deferred:** Direct Client creation and `POST /api/clients`; Client deletion and `DELETE /api/clients/[id]`; `Client.status` and any status migration, filter, badge, or lifecycle workflow; the Lead assignment-management UI; client-portal invitation/activation UI; Proposal/ROS, Booking, Payment, Document, VisaCase, and Conversation/support-history summaries or counts on the Client detail view; a general, cross-feature Client search API; and all schema, migration, environment, deployment, and infrastructure work.
- **Effect:** Authorizes implementation of the Phase 2 "Client management backend and UI" checklist item, and the Client half of the combined `canAccessLead`/`canAccessClient`-wiring checklist item, in `docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md`. Does not itself mark either item, or any other Task Board item, complete — that update remains a later, separate step once the implementation and its required automated and real-PostgreSQL integration coverage (Section 11) actually exist and pass. Does not implement Proposal/ROS, Booking UI, the admin-dashboard overview, the Lead assignment-management UI, or any other not-yet-implemented Phase 2 checkpoint.

## D-026 — Lead Assignment Management UI authorization and converted-lead scope contract

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Date accepted:** August 3, 2026
- **Decision:** The Lead Assignment Management UI checkpoint (blueprint Sections 4, 4.2, 4.3, 6.4; D-009; D-022; D-023; D-025; `docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md` Phase 2's combined "Wire existing Lead/Client staff-assignment functionality ... into actual Lead/Client management workflows" item) is approved as follows. This entry is self-contained — it is the authoritative contract for implementation, not a pointer to a separate report.

  **1. Scope.** This checkpoint authorizes exactly: a new `LeadAssignmentPanel` component (`apps/web/src/app/admin/leads/[id]/_components/LeadAssignmentPanel.tsx`) and its required unit tests; its wiring into `/admin/leads/[id]` (`apps/web/src/app/admin/leads/[id]/page.tsx`) in place of that page's existing static "Assigned Consultant" `<dt>`/`<dd>` pair; and the corresponding updates to that page's own test file. It explicitly does not authorize, and defers: any change to `PUT`/`DELETE /api/leads/[id]/assignment`, `GET /api/assignments/travel-consultants`, or any file under `features/assignments/**` (`schemas.ts`, `service.ts`, `repository.ts`, `http.ts`, `authorization.ts`, `audit.ts`); any `schema.prisma` change or migration; any change to `ClientAssignmentPanel.tsx`, `clients.module.css`, or any Client Management route, service, repository, schema, or UI; and any Lead-status-based restriction on assignment controls (Section 3). The existing assignment APIs, the shared `features/assignments` backend, the Prisma schema and its migrations, `ClientAssignmentPanel`, and Client Management behavior are all reused exactly as already implemented and verified under D-025, and remain unmodified by this decision.

  **2. Authorization — reused unchanged.** This checkpoint introduces no new authorization rule. `PUT`/`DELETE /api/leads/[id]/assignment` remain `withRole(['ADMIN_MANAGER'])`-only, exactly as D-009's role boundary and blueprint Section 4.2 ("Manage staff accounts' operational role assignments and lead/client/booking assignments") already establish and as the existing route tests already confirm (`SYSTEM_ADMINISTRATOR` and `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT` both rejected with 403). `GET /api/assignments/travel-consultants` remains `ADMIN_MANAGER`-only at both the route and the service-layer defense-in-depth check (`assertAssignmentActor`), reused unchanged from D-023 §6. `/admin/leads/[id]` remains reachable by `ADMIN_MANAGER` and `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT` per D-023 §2's existing `ALLOWED_ROLES` gate, unchanged by this decision. Within that page, `LeadAssignmentPanel` renders full interactive assign/reassign/end-assignment controls only for an actor whose role is `ADMIN_MANAGER`; every other role reaching the page (in practice only `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT`) renders a read-only assignment summary with no button, select, or reason input of any kind, and issues no eligible-consultant fetch — the identical division `ClientAssignmentPanel` already implements for Client, per D-025 §2/§10.

  **3. Converted-lead assignment-availability resolution (approved product decision).** D-026 preserves the existing Lead-assignment backend behavior exactly as implemented: `setLeadAssignment`/`endLeadAssignment` (`features/assignments/service.ts`) perform no `Lead.status` check of any kind — the only existence check is `repository.findLeadById`'s plain `{ id: true }` lookup — and this decision does not add one. Accordingly, `ADMIN_MANAGER`'s assignment controls (assign, reassign, and end assignment) remain available and fully interactive in `LeadAssignmentPanel` for every Lead that actor can access, regardless of `Lead.status`, including `CONVERTED_TO_CLIENT`. This checkpoint deliberately does not introduce a Lead-status-based UI lock analogous to `EditLeadForm`'s/`StatusTransitionPanel`'s existing `CONVERTED_TO_CLIENT` lock (the real, server-enforced `LEAD_LOCKED` rule in `features/leads/service.ts`'s `updateLead`) — the asymmetry between assignment (never locked, at either layer) and ordinary-field editing/status transition (locked, at both layers) identified during this checkpoint's contract-audit stage is knowingly preserved, not resolved, here. This is recorded as a deliberate compatibility and scope decision for this checkpoint only — it is not a permanent prohibition on changing the rule later. Any future decision to restrict assignment changes on a converted Lead must be raised and approved as its own, separate decisions-log entry, and — if approved — must be enforced at both the backend (`features/assignments/service.ts`) and the UI (`LeadAssignmentPanel`) layers together; a UI-only lock with no backend enforcement is explicitly prohibited, consistent with this project's standing rule that client-side validation is never authoritative and a control must never be disabled for a rule the server does not itself enforce.

  **4. Component approach.** This checkpoint implements a new, separate `LeadAssignmentPanel.tsx`, modeled directly on the existing `ClientAssignmentPanel.tsx` (`apps/web/src/app/admin/clients/[id]/_components/ClientAssignmentPanel.tsx`) — not a shared, cross-feature, target-parameterized component. This follows the codebase's own established precedent of keeping a separate per-feature copy of near-identical, target-specific UI (the existing separate `EditLeadForm`/`EditClientForm` pair; `clients.module.css`'s own comment explaining why `.clientForm`/`.formAlert`/`.fieldError`/`.formActions`/`.formSuccessAlert` are deliberately not shared with `leads.module.css`'s identical classes) rather than extracting a generic `AssignmentPanel<TargetKind>` abstraction the first time a second consumer appears (`.claude/rules/architecture.md`'s "Avoid Premature Abstraction"). `LeadAssignmentPanel` reuses `leads.module.css`'s existing `.statusPanel`, `.leadForm`, `.formAlert`, `.fieldError`, `.formActions`, `.formSuccessAlert`, and `.filterField` classes unchanged — each already identical in every declared property to `clients.module.css`'s `.assignmentPanel`/`.clientForm` counterparts — so this checkpoint introduces no new CSS.

  **5. Behavioral contract.** `LeadAssignmentPanel` reproduces `ClientAssignmentPanel`'s exact behavior, retargeted from Client to Lead: a first assignment (no existing active assignment) sends no `reason` key and requires none; a reassignment (an existing active assignment with a different assignee) requires a non-blank `reason`, enforced both client-side (blocking submission) and by the existing server-side `REASON_REQUIRED`/`VALIDATION_ERROR` responses; selecting the already-assigned consultant in the reassignment control is treated as a no-op and disables submission client-side, mirroring `setAssignment`'s own server-side idempotent same-assignee no-op; ending an assignment always requires a non-blank `reason` and is idempotent (`endLeadAssignment` returns `{ assignment: null }` with no error when nothing is currently active); a `pendingAction` of `'assign' | 'end' | null` (never a single shared boolean) disables only the active form's own controls, never borrowing the inactive action's progress label; and every controlled server error (`REASON_REQUIRED`, the `VALIDATION_ERROR` reason-path detail, `ASSIGNEE_NOT_FOUND`, `ASSIGNEE_INACTIVE`, `ASSIGNEE_INELIGIBLE_ROLE`, `ASSIGNMENT_CONFLICT`, `LEAD_NOT_FOUND`) and every malformed-response case (unparseable JSON, a non-2xx body that is not a valid `{ error: { code, message } }` envelope, a 2xx body that fails structural or target validation) are handled identically to `ClientAssignmentPanel`, never surfacing a raw exception or a partially-applied UI update.

  **6. Response validation and Lead-targeting.** Every mutation response is validated before being trusted, exactly mirroring `ClientAssignmentPanel`'s `isAssignmentRecordLike`/`isSetAssignmentSuccess`/`isEndAssignmentSuccess` guards with `leadId` in place of `clientId`: a `PUT` success is trusted only when the returned `assignment` is structurally valid, has `endedAt === null`, and has `leadId` equal to the Lead id this panel was rendered for — never a different Lead, a Client, or a Booking; a `DELETE` success is trusted only when `assignment` is `null` (the idempotent no-active-assignment case) or is a structurally valid record with `endedAt !== null` and `leadId` equal to the same expected Lead id. This reuses `AssignmentRecord`'s existing shape (`features/assignments/repository.ts`) unchanged — exactly one of `leadId`/`clientId`/`bookingId` is ever non-null per the database's own three-way XOR constraint, so checking `leadId` alone is sufficient.

  **7. Eligible-consultant pagination.** `LeadAssignmentPanel` reuses `GET /api/assignments/travel-consultants` exactly as `ClientAssignmentPanel` already does — this read is target-agnostic and requires no Lead-specific parameter. For an `ADMIN_MANAGER` actor only, on mount, the panel requests page 1 at `pageSize=100`, inspects the response's real `total`, and continues requesting subsequent pages until every eligible Travel Consultant has been aggregated; an empty page received before `total` is reached, a malformed page, or a failed page all discard whatever was aggregated so far and surface the same controlled loading error a first-page failure would, rather than committing a silently partial roster. No further pagination control is offered — the fully aggregated roster populates one plain `<select>`, identical to the Client panel.

  **8. State synchronization.** `LeadAssignmentPanel` reproduces `ClientAssignmentPanel`'s three synchronization guards, keyed on `leadId` in place of `clientId`: a `leadId` prop change (navigating to a different Lead without this component remounting) resets every piece of transient state (selection, both reason fields, both error states, both success flags, `pendingAction`); a same-Lead change to the authoritative `currentAssignment` prop (typically `router.refresh()` resolving after a mutation) re-syncs only the displayed assignment, never in-progress form state; and a successful mutation resolves the optimistically displayed assignee's name from the already-loaded eligible list (never invented from the raw selection alone), then calls `router.refresh()` so the page's own `getLeadById` read supplies the fully authoritative name and assignment state, while also clearing the other action's now-stale success/error/reason state.

  **9. Accessibility.** Every control in `LeadAssignmentPanel` has an accessible label (the consultant `<select>`, both reason `<textarea>` fields, and both submit buttons), every error/success region uses `role="alert"`/`role="status"` as `ClientAssignmentPanel` already does, and both reason fields carry `aria-invalid`/`aria-describedby` wiring identical to the Client panel's existing implementation — consistent with `.claude/rules/frontend.md`'s accessible-names and keyboard-operability requirements, and requiring no new pattern beyond what `ClientAssignmentPanel` already established and this checkpoint reuses.

  **10. Page wiring.** `/admin/leads/[id]/page.tsx` renders `<LeadAssignmentPanel leadId={leadId} role={user.role} currentAssignment={lead.assignment} />` in place of its current static "Assigned Consultant" `<dt>`/`<dd>` pair inside `styles.detailFields`, positioned immediately after that `dl` and before the "Edit Lead" heading — mirroring `/admin/clients/[id]/page.tsx`'s identical `dl` → assignment panel → edit-form ordering (D-025 §10). `user.role` is the same authenticated actor's role this page already resolves via `getCurrentUser()`; `lead.assignment` is the same field the existing, unchanged `getLeadById` read (`features/leads/service.ts`, backed by `findLeadByIdForRead`/`LeadRecordWithAssignment`) already returns. This decision introduces no new server-side data fetch, service call, or repository call — the page continues calling only `getLeadById`, `getLeadStatusHistory`, and (for a `QUALIFIED` Lead) `getConversionOptions`, exactly as before.

  **11. Testing contract.** Automated component-test coverage for `LeadAssignmentPanel` must cover the same categories `ClientAssignmentPanel.test.tsx` already establishes, retargeted to Lead: first assignment with no reason field or key; reassignment requiring and submitting a reason, and blocked client-side when the reason is blank; a no-op reassignment selection never submitted; ending an assignment requiring and submitting a reason, idempotent on an already-inactive assignment; eligible-consultant loading, empty, and error states; mutation failure handling (server error message, malformed success envelope, network failure); pending-request button isolation between the two independent actions; `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT` read-only rendering with no interactive control and no eligible-list fetch; prop synchronization on both a `leadId` change and a same-Lead `currentAssignment` change, including non-clobbering of a just-confirmed optimistic update by a still-stale prop; mutation response validation rejecting a non-null `endedAt` on a `PUT` success, a null `endedAt` on a `DELETE` success, and a response whose `leadId` targets a different Lead; cross-operation success/error-state clearing; and eligible-consultant pagination aggregation across multiple pages, including a malformed or failed later page. `admin/leads/[id]/page.test.tsx` must add a global `fetch` stub in its `beforeEach` — mirroring `admin/clients/[id]/page.test.tsx`'s existing precedent — since the real, unmocked `LeadAssignmentPanel` now fetches the eligible-consultant list for an `ADMIN_MANAGER` actor; must confirm `LeadAssignmentPanel` receives the exact `leadId`, `role`, and `lead.assignment` the mocked `getLeadById` returns, for both `ADMIN_MANAGER` (interactive, eligible list fetched) and `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT` (read-only, no fetch); and every pre-existing test in that file (status transitions, the conversion panel, status-history pagination, the `CONVERTED_TO_CLIENT` locked-editing state) must continue passing unchanged in substance. No route, service, repository, or real-PostgreSQL integration test is required by this checkpoint, since no file under `features/assignments/**` or any API route changes.

  **12. Task Board handling.** This decision does not itself modify `docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md` — it is a contract-only entry, authorizing implementation but not asserting that the implementation or its required coverage (Section 11) already exists or passes. The combined "Wire existing Lead/Client staff-assignment functionality ... into actual Lead/Client management workflows" checklist item must remain unchecked until `LeadAssignmentPanel` is implemented, wired into `/admin/leads/[id]`, and its required test coverage exists and passes — that update remains a later, separate step, made only once that evidence exists, consistent with D-025 §12's identical handling of the Client half of this same combined item.

- **Rationale:** Reusing the existing, unmodified `PUT`/`DELETE /api/leads/[id]/assignment`, `GET /api/assignments/travel-consultants`, and `features/assignments/**` backend, and modeling `LeadAssignmentPanel` directly on the already-reviewed `ClientAssignmentPanel`, keeps Lead assignment management consistent with the Client assignment management D-025 already authorized and shipped, rather than inventing a parallel mechanism or a shared abstraction ahead of genuine need. Preserving the existing, permissive assignment behavior for a `CONVERTED_TO_CLIENT` Lead — rather than inventing a new backend or UI-only restriction during what is otherwise a UI-only checkpoint — avoids conflating this checkpoint's narrow scope with a separate, unauthorized change to Lead lifecycle or assignment business rules; explicitly recording this as a deliberate, revisitable scope decision (rather than silently leaving it undocumented) keeps the asymmetry with `EditLeadForm`/`StatusTransitionPanel` visible for a future, properly-scoped decision rather than allowing it to be mistaken for an oversight.
- **Constraint:** No `schema.prisma` change and no migration are authorized or required by this decision. No existing API path, request shape, or authorization rule is renamed or altered: `PUT`/`DELETE /api/leads/[id]/assignment`, `GET /api/assignments/travel-consultants`, and every file under `features/assignments/**` are reused entirely unchanged. `ClientAssignmentPanel.tsx`, `clients.module.css`, and every Client Management route, service, repository, schema, and UI already authorized and implemented under D-025 remain unmodified. This decision does not introduce a Lead-status-based lock on assignment controls (Section 3) and does not resolve the blueprint Section 16.2 open "Lead assignment rule (manual vs. an automated rule such as round robin)" item, which concerns how an assignee is chosen and remains separate from, and unaffected by, this checkpoint.
- **Deferred:** Any Lead-status-based restriction on assignment controls, including for `CONVERTED_TO_CLIENT` (Section 3) — reserved for its own future, separately approved decision enforced at both the backend and UI layers; the blueprint Section 16.2 manual-vs-automated Lead assignment rule; any shared, cross-feature `AssignmentPanel` abstraction; and any change to `features/assignments/**`, `schema.prisma`, migrations, `ClientAssignmentPanel`, or Client Management.
- **Effect:** Authorizes implementation of `LeadAssignmentPanel` and its wiring into `/admin/leads/[id]`, closing the Lead half of the Phase 2 combined "Wire existing Lead/Client staff-assignment functionality ... into actual Lead/Client management workflows" checklist item once implemented and verified per Section 11. Does not itself implement `LeadAssignmentPanel`, modify any application code, or mark that Task Board item — or any other Task Board item — complete; that update remains a later, separate step, made only once the implementation and its required test coverage actually exist and pass.

## D-027 — Proposal / ROS Management authorization, versioning, publishing, and external-response contract

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Date accepted:** August 3, 2026
- **Decision:** The Proposal / ROS Management checkpoint (blueprint Sections 4.2, 4.3, 5, 8, 9, 9.1, 13.5, 14.4; D-002; D-006; D-009; D-014; D-019; D-021; `docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md` Phase 2's "Proposal / ROS authoring, versioning, and management UI" and "Recording externally received Accept/Decline/Request Changes responses" items) is approved as follows. This entry is self-contained — it is the authoritative contract for implementation, not a pointer to a separate report.

  **1. Content field and migration.** `ProposalVersion` gains one new column, `content String?` — nullable at the database level solely for safe-migration compatibility with any pre-existing row this repository's own code has never written (no application writer, service, route, UI, test, or seed script has ever existed for `Proposal`/`ProposalVersion`/`ProposalAcceptance` prior to this checkpoint, per the Proposal / ROS Schema Foundation checkpoint's own file-header note in `apps/web/prisma/schema.prisma`); this nullability is never a permission for this checkpoint's own code to write a null or blank value. Every `ProposalVersion` this checkpoint's service layer creates — both the first version of a new Proposal and every later revision — requires `content` to be present: a trimmed, non-blank, plain-text string of at most exactly 20,000 characters (`z.string().trim().min(1, 'content is required').max(20000, 'content must be at most 20,000 characters')`). Trimming removes only leading/trailing whitespace; every internal character, including newlines, is preserved unchanged. `content` is plain text only: it is never interpreted as HTML or any other markup by any part of this checkpoint, and the admin UI renders it as a plain-text node (e.g., CSS `white-space: pre-wrap`, never `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` or any HTML-interpreting renderer), so a literal `<script>`-looking or markup-looking string a Travel Consultant types is always displayed as text, never executed or rendered as markup. The migration additionally adds the raw PostgreSQL CHECK constraint `ALTER TABLE "proposal_version" ADD CONSTRAINT "proposal_version_content_nonblank" CHECK ("content" IS NULL OR btrim("content") <> '')` — required by this contract, not optional — permitting `NULL` (any pre-existing row) while forbidding an all-whitespace string from ever being written by any future code path, including one that bypasses the service layer, mirroring this schema's established precedent of backing a service-level invariant with a database CHECK wherever Prisma's schema language can express it (`booking_travel_dates_order`, `proposal_acceptance_response_path`). No itinerary structure, day structure, inclusions/exclusions structure, line-item or quotation representation, pricing, tax, discount, total, or currency field is introduced by this decision — all remain explicitly deferred to their own future, separately approved decisions. Backfilling any legacy row whose `content` is `NULL`, and the follow-up migration that would then add a `NOT NULL` constraint, are both explicitly deferred to their own future, separate decision — this checkpoint neither performs nor requires either, mirroring D-019's identical precedent for `Booking.totalAmount`/`currencyCode` (nullable now, service-layer invariant enforced now, hard constraint deferred).

  **2. Scope.** This checkpoint authorizes: the `content` migration above; a new `features/proposals` module (schemas, errors, repository, service, audit, http); five route-handler files exposing six HTTP endpoints (Section 6 of this decision's route table, immediately below); the `/admin/proposals` list and `/admin/proposals/[id]` detail/version-history/publish/response-recording pages; a new `CreateProposalPanel` on the existing `/admin/clients/[id]` page; a role-gated "Proposals" link in `apps/web/src/app/admin/layout.tsx`; and the required test coverage (Section 10). It explicitly does not authorize, and defers: structured itinerary/day content and any TourPackage linkage; inclusions/exclusions structure; quotation/line-item representation; pricing, tax, discount, total, or currency on a Proposal; the `content` column's backfill or `NOT NULL` follow-up; portal-based (client-authenticated) acceptance (Phase 3, once the client portal exists); and Proposal deletion or archival (neither is implied anywhere in the blueprint). Existing already-shipped features receive only the specific, additive integrations Section 8 authorizes — the new `CreateProposalPanel`/Proposal-summary wiring added to the existing `/admin/clients/[id]` page, and the new role-gated "Proposals" link added to the existing `apps/web/src/app/admin/layout.tsx` navigation — and nothing else: existing Client schemas, repository, service, HTTP/API behavior, editing, and assignment management, and every other unrelated existing UI behavior on both pages, remain unchanged. `Booking`, `StaffAssignment`, `features/assignments/**`, `canAccessClient`, `ClientAssignmentPanel`, and `LeadAssignmentPanel` receive no change of any kind from this decision.

  **3. Role and operation matrix.** `ADMIN_MANAGER` may list every Proposal and read any Proposal's full detail and version history, and may record a Section 9.1 externally received response (Accept, Decline, or Request Changes) for any Client's current client-visible `ProposalVersion` — but may not create a Proposal, create a revision, or publish a version, consistent with blueprint Section 4.2's capability list, which grants Admin/Manager "full visibility across... proposals" and "record externally received proposal responses under the controlled fallback in Section 9.1" but never names proposal authoring, unlike Section 4.3's explicit, Travel-Consultant-only "prepare and revise proposals / ROS drafts and versions." `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT` may list and read only Proposals belonging to a Client they hold an active assignment for, and may create a Proposal, create a revision, publish a version, and record a Section 9.1 external response only for a Client they hold an active assignment for. Every other role (`SYSTEM_ADMINISTRATOR`, `FINANCE_ACCOUNTING`, `VISA_DOCUMENTATION`, `CLIENT`) is denied every operation this checkpoint introduces. Enforcement is layered, mirroring every prior Lead/Client/Booking/Assignment checkpoint's own established discipline: `GET /api/proposals`, `GET /api/proposals/[id]`, and `POST /api/proposals/versions/[id]/response` are gated by `withRole(['ADMIN_MANAGER', 'TRAVEL_CONSULTANT'])`; `POST /api/proposals`, `POST /api/proposals/[id]/versions`, and `POST /api/proposals/versions/[id]/publish` are gated by `withRole(['TRAVEL_CONSULTANT'])` only — `ADMIN_MANAGER` is rejected by the route guard itself for these three routes, before any handler body runs. Service-layer authorization remains mandatory as defense in depth, independent of and in addition to each route's own gate: view/read access and Section 9.1 response-recording access both reuse the existing, unchanged `canAccessClient` (`features/assignments/authorization.ts`) exactly as already implemented — its own `ADMIN_MANAGER`-unconditional / `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT`-active-assignment split already matches this operation's rule precisely; create/revise/publish access is governed by a new, feature-local `assertProposalAuthorAccess`, which — because the route guard already excludes `ADMIN_MANAGER` for these three operations — checks only that the actor is `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT` and holds an active assignment for the target Client (via `assignmentRepository.findActiveAssignmentForClient`, the same underlying primitive `canAccessClient` itself calls, reused directly rather than duplicated). Every `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT` mutation (create, revise, publish, record-response) additionally performs an authoritative transaction-local active-assignment recheck — `assignmentRepository.findActiveAssignmentForClient(tx, clientId)`, re-run inside the same `runSerializableWithRetry` transaction as the write itself, before that write occurs — mirroring D-024 Section 6(b)'s and D-025 Section 7's identical, already-shipped transaction-local-recheck precedent exactly, since the actor's assignment could change between the pre-transaction check and the write. `canAccessClient` and every existing file under `features/assignments/**` remain entirely unmodified by this decision — reused exactly as already implemented and verified.

  **4. Lifecycle and Section 9.1 responses.** This checkpoint introduces no new stored lifecycle field: it reuses `ProposalVersion.clientVisibleAt` and `ProposalVersion.supersededAt` exactly as already migrated by the Proposal / ROS Schema Foundation checkpoint, and continues to derive "accepted/locked" from the existence of a `ProposalAcceptance` row with `responseType = ACCEPT` rather than a stored flag — no Proposal-level status enum and no separate stored "locked" boolean or timestamp is added. This checkpoint authorizes Section 9.1's externally-recorded-response mechanism (Accept, Decline, or Request Changes, entered by staff on the client's behalf) as part of this same checkpoint, not as a separate follow-up: the record captures the response type, the client's stated response timestamp, the acting staff account, the response method (e.g., phone, email, in person), and a supporting evidence reference, exactly as `ProposalAcceptance`'s already-migrated external-attribution path requires. Portal-based (client-authenticated) responses remain deferred to Phase 3, when the client portal and `ClientProfile`-based session attribution actually exist; `ProposalAcceptance.respondingClientProfileId`/`respondingSessionIdAtResponse` remain schema-only until then. `DECLINE` and `REQUEST_CHANGES` are immutable historical responses recorded against the specific `ProposalVersion` they answer — neither terminates the Proposal, sets any Proposal-level status, nor prevents a Travel Consultant from creating and publishing a later revision regardless of an earlier version's response. A later revision may also supersede a version that was itself accepted (per blueprint Section 9's own "any subsequent change requires a new revision to be created and, if needed, re-accepted" text), without altering, unlinking, or otherwise affecting any `Booking` already created from that specific accepted historical `ProposalVersion` — `Booking.proposalVersionId`'s existing `onDelete: Restrict` foreign key to that exact version row is untouched by any later Proposal-level supersession. Exactly one `ProposalVersion` may receive at most one response, ever — enforced by `ProposalAcceptance.proposalVersionId`'s existing `@unique` constraint, not merely a service-layer policy; Section 7 below defines the resulting non-idempotent behavior.

  **5. Publish concurrency contract.** Every `POST /api/proposals/versions/[id]/publish` request supplies a required key, `expectedCurrentVersionId: string (uuid) | null` — the current client-visible version the actor observed for this Proposal before deciding to publish, or explicitly `null` when the actor observed no current version at all. This key is never optional or omittable, avoiding the omitted-versus-null ambiguity D-025 Section 3 already disambiguated for an analogous case. Inside one `runSerializableWithRetry` transaction, in this exact order: (a) the target `ProposalVersion` is fetched, scoped to what the actor may see; since publish is Travel-Consultant-only (Section 3 — this route is `withRole(['TRAVEL_CONSULTANT'])`, and `ADMIN_MANAGER` never reaches it), a nonexistent target and a target that exists but belongs to a Client the actor holds no active assignment for are indistinguishable and both produce the identical `PROPOSAL_VERSION_FORBIDDEN` (403) — never `PROPOSAL_VERSION_NOT_FOUND` (404) — consistent with the established anti-enumeration rule this codebase already applies wherever a lookup is scoped to a single non-administrative role; (b) the `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT` transaction-local active-assignment recheck (Section 3) runs; (c) if the target is already the current client-visible version (`clientVisibleAt IS NOT NULL AND supersededAt IS NULL`), this is a pure idempotent no-op — the version is returned unchanged, with no additional write and no new audit entry, checked before any other comparison below, mirroring `setLeadAssignment`'s/`setClientAssignment`'s own identical same-outcome-runs-first precedent; (d) if the target is already superseded (`supersededAt IS NOT NULL`), the request is rejected with `PROPOSAL_VERSION_SUPERSEDED` (409) — a superseded version can never be resurrected as current by a later publish call; (e) otherwise, the Proposal's authoritative current version (if any) is read inside the transaction and its id (or `null`, if none exists) is compared against the request's `expectedCurrentVersionId`; a mismatch returns `PROPOSAL_CONFLICT` (409, "The current version has changed since you last loaded this proposal. Refresh and try again.") rather than silently superseding a version the actor never observed; (f) on a match, the prior current version (if one exists) has its `supersededAt` set, the target version's `clientVisibleAt` is set, and the `PROPOSAL_VERSION_PUBLISHED` audit entry is written — all atomically, in the same transaction. The existing partial unique index `proposal_version_current_client_visible_key` (already migrated by the Proposal / ROS Schema Foundation checkpoint) remains the final database-level backstop if two publish requests somehow both pass step (e) concurrently; any residual conflict this backstop raises, and any residual SERIALIZABLE conflict (P2034) that survives `runSerializableWithRetry`'s own retry bound, is caught and translated to the identical controlled `PROPOSAL_CONFLICT` (409) response — never exposed to the route layer as a raw Prisma/PostgreSQL error, mirroring `features/bookings/service.ts`'s `isOtherKnownConflict` handling exactly.

  **6. Revision allocation, replay, and creation idempotency; API route table.** The next `versionNumber` for a new revision is computed as `MAX(versionNumber) + 1` for the Proposal, read inside the same `runSerializableWithRetry` transaction as the write, relying on SERIALIZABLE isolation to prevent two concurrent transactions from computing and committing the same next number. As a defense-in-depth backstop mirroring `features/bookings/service.ts`'s `createBooking`'s own `bookingReference`-collision retry loop exactly, a residual unique-constraint conflict on `ProposalVersion`'s existing `@@unique([proposalId, versionNumber])` index is caught by an outer bounded retry loop — up to three attempts — that opens a fresh transaction and recomputes `MAX(versionNumber) + 1` again, never leaking a raw Prisma/PostgreSQL error to the route layer and never silently overwriting an existing version. Creating a Proposal (with its first version) and creating a revision are both **not** idempotent: the schema deliberately permits multiple Proposals per Client and multiple coexisting unpublished drafts per Proposal, so neither operation has a natural key to replay against — every successful request creates exactly one new row, matching this codebase's existing Lead-creation precedent (duplicate detection flags a match for staff review; it never blocks, merges, or dedupes the write itself). A caller is responsible for not double-submitting either request, the same as every other create endpoint in this codebase. This checkpoint authorizes exactly five route-handler files, exposing exactly six HTTP endpoints between them (`/api/proposals/route.ts` alone exposes both `POST` and `GET`): `POST /api/proposals` (`{ clientId: uuid, content: string }` → `201 { proposal, version }`); `GET /api/proposals` (paginated, actor-scoped list → `200 { items, page, pageSize, total }`); `GET /api/proposals/[id]` (→ `200 { proposal, versions }`, each version including `content`, `clientVisibleAt`, `supersededAt`, `createdByUserId`, `createdAt`, and its `acceptance` if any); `POST /api/proposals/[id]/versions` (`{ content: string }` → `201 { version }`); `POST /api/proposals/versions/[id]/publish` (`{ expectedCurrentVersionId: uuid | null }` → `200 { version }`); and `POST /api/proposals/versions/[id]/response` (`{ responseType: 'ACCEPT'|'DECLINE'|'REQUEST_CHANGES', respondedAt: ISO-8601 string, responseMethod: string (trimmed, 1–100 characters), evidenceReference: string (trimmed, 1–500 characters) }` → `200 { acceptance }`). Every request body is `.strict()` — an unrecognized key fails validation with `VALIDATION_ERROR` (400) rather than being silently stripped, matching `createBookingSchema`'s established discipline. The full controlled-error-code-to-status map is: `ROLE_NOT_PERMITTED` 403; `CLIENT_NOT_FOUND` 404 / `CLIENT_FORBIDDEN` 403 (via `canAccessClient`); `PROPOSAL_NOT_FOUND` 404 / `PROPOSAL_FORBIDDEN` 403; `PROPOSAL_VERSION_NOT_FOUND` 404 / `PROPOSAL_VERSION_FORBIDDEN` 403; `PROPOSAL_VERSION_SUPERSEDED` 409; `PROPOSAL_VERSION_NOT_CURRENT` 409; `PROPOSAL_RESPONSE_ALREADY_RECORDED` 409; `PROPOSAL_CONFLICT` 409; `VALIDATION_ERROR` 400.

  **7. External-response recording — one-time, non-idempotent.** Recording a Section 9.1 external response is not idempotent. Because `ProposalAcceptance.proposalVersionId` is `@unique`, at most one response may ever exist for a given `ProposalVersion`. Inside the transaction, immediately after the identical view/response-recording authorization (Section 3) and the transaction-local `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT` recheck, the checks run in this exact, fixed order: first, whether a `ProposalAcceptance` already exists for this version — if one does, the request is rejected with `PROPOSAL_RESPONSE_ALREADY_RECORDED` (409) unconditionally, regardless of whether the newly submitted `responseType`, `respondedAt`, `responseMethod`, or `evidenceReference` match the existing row or differ from it, and **regardless of whether this version has since been superseded** — an already-responded version's response is immutable history and is never re-evaluated against the version's current lifecycle state; no fuzzy or partial-match replay is performed. Only when no `ProposalAcceptance` exists yet for this version does the request proceed to the second check: whether the target is the Proposal's current client-visible version at write time; if it is not, the request is rejected with `PROPOSAL_VERSION_NOT_CURRENT` (409). This ordering — existing-response check before current-version check — ensures a version that already carries a recorded response, even one long since superseded by a later revision, always reports `PROPOSAL_RESPONSE_ALREADY_RECORDED` rather than the misleading `PROPOSAL_VERSION_NOT_CURRENT`. Whether an earlier recording attempt actually succeeded is answered by reading the Proposal/version detail (`GET /api/proposals/[id]`), never by retrying the `POST`. On `ACCEPT` specifically, this same write is simultaneously the version's lock — derived per Section 4, with no separate stored flag.

  **8. UI scope and entry points.** The sole entry point for creating the first Proposal for a Client is a new `CreateProposalPanel` component rendered on the existing `/admin/clients/[id]` detail page — reusing that page's own already-established authorization context (`canAccessClient` already gates the page; a `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT` can only ever be viewing a Client they are assigned to) so that no new Client-search or Client-picker mechanism is introduced anywhere by this decision. Creating a revision of an existing Proposal is a distinct capability that belongs on `/admin/proposals/[id]`, not on the Client detail page: no `CreateProposalRevisionPanel` or equivalent component is added under `apps/web/src/app/admin/clients/[id]/_components/`. `/admin/proposals` provides a paginated, actor-scoped Proposal list; `/admin/proposals/[id]` provides the Proposal's full detail, complete version history, the revision-creation form, the publish action, and the Section 9.1 response-recording form — all four of these workflows live on this one detail page. `apps/web/src/app/admin/layout.tsx` gains a role-gated "Proposals" navigation link, visible to the same `['ADMIN_MANAGER', 'TRAVEL_CONSULTANT']` role set already used for the existing "Clients" link. No Proposal title or reference field is introduced anywhere by this decision: the blueprint never names one for Proposal (unlike Booking's explicit `bookingReference`, blueprint Section 13.4), and multiple Proposals belonging to the same Client are distinguished in every list or detail view using only the linked Client's identity, `Proposal.createdAt`, and each version's own existing identifiers and derived state — never an invented label. A legacy `ProposalVersion` row whose `content` is `NULL` (Section 1) remains fully readable through every list/detail/history view and displays an explicit "Content unavailable" state in place of the missing text, rather than an empty space, a rendering error, or a fabricated placeholder. Every rendering of a `ProposalVersion`'s lifecycle state (draft / current client-visible / superseded) and its response state (no response / Accept / Decline / Request Changes), where both apply to the same version, presents the two as separate, clearly labeled indicators rather than collapsing them into one combined label — so that a state such as "accepted, and later superseded by a newer version" remains unambiguous to the viewing staff member.

  **9. Audit contract.** Four new `AuditLog` actions are added: `PROPOSAL_CREATED`, `PROPOSAL_VERSION_CREATED`, `PROPOSAL_VERSION_PUBLISHED`, and `PROPOSAL_RESPONSE_RECORDED` — entity type `Proposal` for the first, `ProposalVersion` for the middle two, and `ProposalVersion` again for the last (identifying the version the response was recorded against). Every sanitizer is an explicit allow-list, matching `sanitizeBookingSnapshot`'s/`sanitizeAssignmentSnapshot`'s established convention, and never includes a `ProposalVersion`'s `content` field, in either `beforeState` or `afterState`, under any circumstance — an audit snapshot records only ids, version numbers, and lifecycle/response state transitions, never the authored business content. `evidenceReference` (Section 9.1) remains, at the schema and API layer alike, a bounded pointer or identifier only — never a full copy of correspondence, unrelated personal information, or an identity document — consistent with blueprint Section 9.1's own text and `.claude/rules/database-security.md`'s data-minimization principle; this checkpoint does not relax that limit anywhere, including inside audit snapshots.

  **10. Testing contract.** Automated coverage is required at every existing tier before this checkpoint is considered complete: schema tests covering `content`'s exact trim/nonblank/20,000-character-maximum behavior and the `.strict()` rejection of any unrecognized request key on every new endpoint; repository tests covering assignment-scoped list/read composed directly into the query's `where` clause (never fetch-then-filter) and the atomic `MAX(versionNumber) + 1` allocation; service tests covering both roles' authorization exactly as Section 3 defines it (including that `ADMIN_MANAGER` is rejected for create/revise/publish specifically), the transaction-local active-assignment recheck, the publish idempotent-no-op and `PROPOSAL_CONFLICT`/`PROPOSAL_VERSION_SUPERSEDED` paths (Section 5's indistinguishable-nonexistent-vs-inaccessible `PROPOSAL_VERSION_FORBIDDEN` outcome for the Travel-Consultant-only publish route specifically), concurrent-revision-creation retry behavior, and `PROPOSAL_RESPONSE_ALREADY_RECORDED` on any second response-recording attempt — including, specifically, a version that already carries a recorded response and has since been superseded by a later revision, proving it still returns `PROPOSAL_RESPONSE_ALREADY_RECORDED` rather than `PROPOSAL_VERSION_NOT_CURRENT` (Section 7's fixed check-ordering); error and audit-sanitizer tests proving no snapshot ever contains `content`; route tests covering every role gate (including the `withRole(['TRAVEL_CONSULTANT'])`-only routes correctly rejecting `ADMIN_MANAGER`), validation failures, and the anti-enumeration `NOT_FOUND`/`FORBIDDEN` split; component and page tests covering a legacy `NULL`-content version's "Content unavailable" display, the separate lifecycle-state/response-state rendering (Section 8), `CreateProposalPanel`'s presence on the Client detail page, and the absence of any revision-creation control there; and real-PostgreSQL integration tests, guarded by this feature's own copy of the established `heritage_v3_test`/`validateTestDatabaseUrl` safety discipline, covering Admin-versus-Travel-Consultant visibility, a genuinely concurrent publish exercising the `expectedCurrentVersionId` mismatch path under real parallel transactions, concurrent revision-number allocation under real concurrency, PII-free `AuditLog` rows, and — specifically — a regression test proving that an `ACCEPT` `ProposalAcceptance` written by this checkpoint's code remains correctly visible to `features/bookings/repository.ts`'s existing, unmodified `findEligibleProposalVersionForActor`/`hasAcceptedAcceptance` check, since Booking's own creation eligibility already depends on this exact table.

  **11. Task Board handling.** This decision authorizes future implementation only; it does not itself implement any application code, schema change, or migration, and does not modify `docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md`. Implementation occurs on a new branch created only after this entry is accepted and merged into `main` — never on this contract-only branch. Neither the Phase 2 "Proposal / ROS authoring, versioning, and management UI" checklist item nor the "Recording externally received Accept/Decline/Request Changes responses" checklist item may be marked complete by this entry; that update remains a later, separate closure checkpoint, made only once the implementation described above, and its required test coverage (Section 10), actually exist and pass — mirroring D-024 Section 12's, D-025 Section 12's, and D-026 Section 12's identical, already-established precedent exactly.

- **Rationale:** Reusing the existing, unmodified `canAccessClient`, `assignmentRepository.findActiveAssignmentForClient`, `runSerializableWithRetry`, the standard `{ error: { code, message } }` envelope, and this schema's own already-migrated concurrency backstop (the partial unique index) keeps Proposal / ROS management consistent with every already-reviewed Lead, Client, Booking, and Assignment checkpoint, rather than inventing a parallel authorization, concurrency, or persistence mechanism. Respecting blueprint Section 4.2's and Section 4.3's literal, asymmetric capability lists — Travel-Consultant-only authoring, Admin/Manager visibility plus Section 9.1 recording only — rather than defaulting to this codebase's usual Admin/Manager-unconditional-access pattern, avoids silently expanding a role's authority beyond what the blueprint specifically wrote for it. Requiring `expectedCurrentVersionId` on every publish request closes the lost-update gap the existing partial unique index alone cannot close (that index prevents two simultaneously current versions, but not a stale actor unknowingly superseding a version they never observed), using the same optimistic-concurrency shape D-014 and D-025 Section 7 already established for Booking and Client. Treating Decline/Request-Changes as immutable, non-terminal historical facts — never inventing a Proposal-level status — preserves the schema's own deliberate design (accepted/locked is derived, never stored) and blueprint Section 9's explicit allowance for a later revision "if needed, re-accepted" even after an earlier acceptance. Placing first-Proposal creation on the Client detail page, and keeping revision creation on the Proposal detail page instead of duplicating a creation panel under Client detail, avoids introducing a second, redundant creation surface and the unrestricted Client-search mechanism a standalone `/admin/proposals/new` page would otherwise require.
- **Constraint:** `Booking`, `StaffAssignment`, `features/assignments/**` (including `canAccessClient` itself), `ClientAssignmentPanel`, and `LeadAssignmentPanel` receive no change of any kind from this decision. Existing Client Management code (`features/clients/**`, `/api/clients/**`, and the existing Client-detail editing and assignment-management UI) and the existing admin navigation are modified only by the two specific, additive integrations Section 8 authorizes — `CreateProposalPanel`/Proposal-summary wiring on `/admin/clients/[id]`, and the role-gated "Proposals" link in `apps/web/src/app/admin/layout.tsx` — every other existing Client schema, repository, service, HTTP/API behavior, editing capability, assignment-management capability, and unrelated UI behavior on both pages remains unchanged. The `content` migration is additive only (Section 1); no existing `ProposalVersion`, `ProposalAcceptance`, or `Proposal` column, index, or constraint is altered or removed. No `NOT NULL` constraint is added to `content` by this decision. This decision does not resolve the blueprint Section 16.2/D-002 currency-strategy open item, does not extend D-019's Booking-scoped currency refinement to Proposal, and does not implement any structured itinerary, pricing, or quotation content.
- **Deferred:** Structured itinerary/day content and any TourPackage linkage; inclusions/exclusions structure; quotation/line-item representation; pricing, tax, discount, total, and currency on a Proposal; the `content` column's backfill and its future `NOT NULL` follow-up migration; portal-based (client-authenticated) acceptance, deferred to Phase 3; Proposal deletion or archival; any shared, cross-feature authoring-panel abstraction; and any change to `docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md`.
- **Effect:** Authorizes implementation of the Phase 2 "Proposal / ROS authoring, versioning, and management UI" checklist item and the "Recording externally received Accept/Decline/Request Changes responses" checklist item together, as one combined checkpoint, on a future implementation branch created after this entry reaches `main`. Does not itself implement either item, and does not mark either — or any other Task Board item — complete; that update remains a later, separate step, made only once the implementation and its required test coverage (Section 10) actually exist and pass.

## D-028 — Booking Management UI scope, authorization, and workflow contract

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Date accepted:** August 6, 2026
- **Decision:** The Booking Management UI checkpoint (blueprint Sections 4.2, 4.3, 5, 13.4, 14.5; D-009; D-013; D-014; D-015; D-019; D-021; D-027; `docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md` Phase 2's "Booking management UI using the existing Booking API (creation, list, detail, status transitions, assignment)" item) is approved as follows. This entry is self-contained — it is the authoritative contract for implementation, not a pointer to a separate report.

  **1. Scope.** This checkpoint authorizes an admin Booking Management UI built exclusively against the existing `features/bookings/**` and `features/assignments/**` service/repository/API contract exactly as already implemented and tested (D-013; D-014; D-015): `POST /api/bookings`, `GET /api/bookings`, `GET /api/bookings/[id]`, `PUT /api/bookings/[id]/status`, and `PUT /api/bookings/[id]/assignment`. It authorizes no Prisma schema change, no migration, no seed-script change, no new dependency, and no expansion of any Booking API request body, query parameter, or response shape — `createBookingSchema`, `listBookingsQuerySchema`, `updateBookingStatusSchema`, and `features/assignments/schemas.ts`'s `setAssignmentSchema` are all reused unmodified. No new list-filter field is introduced. No new status-history read function or HTTP endpoint is introduced. No standalone booking-edit workflow (for `totalAmount`, `currencyCode`, `tourPackageName`, `destination`, travel dates, traveler count, included/excluded services, special requests, or either notes field) is introduced — every nullable Booking field this checkpoint's UI displays remains read-only, exactly as already returned by `GET /api/bookings/[id]`.

  **2. Authorized roles.** `ADMIN_MANAGER` receives unrestricted list, detail, creation, and status-transition access across every Booking, plus the sole ability to assign or reassign a Booking's Travel Consultant — mirroring `PUT /api/bookings/[id]/assignment`'s existing `withRole(['ADMIN_MANAGER'])` route gate exactly. `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT` receives list, detail, creation, and status-transition access scoped exclusively through the existing active-Client-assignment mechanism (`features/bookings/repository.ts`'s `clientAssignmentFilter`, reused unmodified) and receives a **read-only** presentation of a Booking's current assignment — no assignment-mutation control of any kind, matching the backend's own unconditional 403 for this role on the assignment route. `FINANCE_ACCOUNTING`, `VISA_DOCUMENTATION`, `CLIENT`, and `SYSTEM_ADMINISTRATOR` remain excluded from every Booking Management UI surface this checkpoint introduces, exactly as the existing backend already excludes them from every Booking endpoint (D-014's role list; confirmed unchanged for the assignment route by D-015). This checkpoint preserves, and introduces no UI-layer exception to, the existing 401 (unauthenticated)/403 (forbidden role, or an inaccessible-to-this-Travel-Consultant Booking)/404 (genuinely missing, Admin/Manager only) split and its existence-leak protection: the UI never renders, infers, or exposes through timing, copy, or navigation state any signal that would let an unassigned Travel Consultant distinguish a Booking that does not exist from one that exists but is not theirs.

  **3. Routes and navigation.** `/admin/bookings` is a Server Component, filter-less, paginated list, gated to `['ADMIN_MANAGER', 'TRAVEL_CONSULTANT']` at the page layer, mirroring `/admin/proposals`'s existing three-layer authentication/authorization discipline (proxy, `admin/layout.tsx`, and the page's own independent re-check) exactly. `/admin/bookings/[id]` is a Server Component detail page under the same role gate. `apps/web/src/app/admin/layout.tsx` gains a role-gated "Bookings" navigation link, visible only to `['ADMIN_MANAGER', 'TRAVEL_CONSULTANT']` through a dedicated role-set constant declared in that file — matching the "Clients"/"Proposals" gated-`Link` pattern, not the always-rendered "Leads" pattern.

  **4. List behavior.** `/admin/bookings` reuses `listBookingsQuerySchema`'s existing `page`/`pageSize` validation (default 1/20, max pageSize 100) unmodified, and the existing shared `apps/web/src/app/admin/_components/Pagination.tsx` component unmodified. No filter field (status, Client, assigned consultant, or date range) is introduced, because the current Booking list contract supports pagination only — adding one would require a `listBookingsQuerySchema` change this checkpoint does not authorize. The list renders both a responsive `<table>` and a mobile-card layout from the same fetched page of items, toggled by CSS media query only, mirroring `ProposalTable`'s existing dual-render pattern (the closest existing precedent, since Proposal is likewise filter-less). The page independently, server-side renders: an empty state ("No bookings yet") when `total === 0`; a validation-error state (mirroring the existing `role="alert"` plus corrective-link pattern) for a malformed `page`/`pageSize`; and the existing in-place "Access denied" state for an authenticated actor outside `['ADMIN_MANAGER', 'TRAVEL_CONSULTANT']` — none of these three states depends on client-side loading state, since the page is a Server Component that resolves its data before rendering.

  **5. Detail behavior.** `/admin/bookings/[id]` displays the specified user-relevant fields from the existing `BookingRecord` response `GET /api/bookings/[id]` already returns (`BookingRecord`'s exact field set and its backing `BOOKING_SELECT`, `features/bookings/repository.ts:58-100`), read-only: `bookingReference`, `status`, `clientId` (rendered as a link to `/admin/clients/[clientId]`), `proposalVersionId` (rendered as reference text only — this checkpoint does not invent a proposal-version detail route or construct a link to one, since none exists), the nullable operational/travel fields (`tourPackageName`, `destination`, `travelStartDate`/`travelEndDate`, `travelerCount`, `includedServices`, `excludedServices`, `specialRequests`, `internalNotes`, `clientVisibleNotes`) exactly as returned, with an explicit placeholder for any field that is `null` — never a fabricated value — and `createdAt`/`updatedAt` as read-only booking metadata, since both are likewise part of the confirmed `BookingRecord` response. The raw `id` field need not be rendered as a separate visual field, since it is already this page's own route identity (`/admin/bookings/[id]`). **`totalAmount` and `currencyCode` are not part of the current `BookingRecord` response** — `BOOKING_SELECT` (`repository.ts:82-100`) never includes either column, despite both existing in the database and their backing migration (`20260720060555_payment_schema_foundation`) being confirmed present, successfully applied, and not rolled back in both `heritage_v3_dev` and `heritage_v3_test` by this checkpoint's Stage 1 read-only verification — a database column existing is not the same as the feature's read DTO returning it, and this entry authorizes no field outside the existing response. Displaying `totalAmount`/`currencyCode` would require a `BOOKING_SELECT`/`BookingRecord` expansion this checkpoint does not authorize (Section 1). No edit control exists for any field this section authorizes — populating or editing any Booking field remains an explicitly deferred, separate future decision (Section 10). No status-history display is authorized by this entry.

  **6. Creation flow.** The sole Booking-creation entry point is a new `CreateBookingButton` embedded in the existing Proposal detail / version-history flow (`/admin/proposals/[id]`, inside or adjacent to `ProposalVersionHistory`), rendered only for a `ProposalVersion` that (a) already carries an externally recorded accepted response — a `ProposalAcceptance` row whose `responseType` equals the `ProposalResponseType.ACCEPT` enum value (`apps/web/prisma/schema.prisma:1396-1399`), matching `features/bookings/repository.ts`'s existing `findEligibleProposalVersionForActor`'s `hasAcceptedAcceptance` eligibility predicate (`found.acceptance?.responseType === 'ACCEPT'`, `repository.ts:236`) exactly, and identical at the API layer, where `features/proposals/schemas.ts`'s `recordProposalResponseSchema` accepts `responseType` via `z.nativeEnum(ProposalResponseType)` unmodified (D-027 Sections 4/7) — and (b) is accessible to the current authorized actor under that page's own existing authorization — no new Proposal- or Client-picker of any kind is introduced. On submit, it issues `POST /api/bookings` with exactly `{ proposalVersionId }`, reusing `createBookingSchema` unmodified. The response is validated to accept both a `201` (newly created) and a `200` (idempotent replay) success shape — mirroring `createBooking`'s own documented idempotent-replay behavior (Section 1; D-013's retry precedent) — never assuming success from the request alone. On a validated success, the component navigates to `/admin/bookings/{returnedBookingId}` (`router.push`, then `router.refresh()`, mirroring `CreateProposalPanel`'s established navigate-then-refresh convention) and sets a permanent post-success latch (mirroring `justCreated`/`justPublished`/`justRecorded`) so a stray resubmission during the navigation window can never issue a second, redundant `POST /api/bookings`. No `/admin/bookings/new` page or route is introduced.

  **7. Status transitions.** `/admin/bookings/[id]` renders a `BookingStatusTransitionPanel` offering only the target statuses `features/bookings/transitions.ts`'s existing `isTransitionAllowed(currentStatus, x)` permits for the Booking's server-rendered current status — computed and filtered exactly as `ProposalVersionHistory` already computes per-version publish eligibility server-side before passing it as a prop. `CANCELLED` is presented as an ordinary member of that same allowed-transitions set whenever `isTransitionAllowed` includes it, never as a separate control or endpoint — matching the existing backend, which folds cancellation into `PUT /api/bookings/[id]/status` and exposes no dedicated cancel route. Every submission sends exactly `{ expectedStatus, newStatus }` to `PUT /api/bookings/[id]/status`, where `expectedStatus` is the status value the panel most recently rendered — reusing `updateBookingStatusSchema` unmodified. A `409 BOOKING_CONFLICT` response is handled with an explicit stale-data message and a "Refresh booking" affordance that calls `router.refresh()` only, never automatically resubmitting — mirroring `PublishProposalVersionButton`'s existing `conflict`-flag pattern exactly.
