Every other controlled response (`BOOKING_FORBIDDEN`, `BOOKING_NOT_FOUND`, `INVALID_STATUS_TRANSITION`, a role-forbidden/unauthenticated response, or a generic failure) and the accessible success/error/`role="alert"`/`role="status"` presentation reuse the established Proposal/Lead-panel conventions unmodified. Unlike `CreateBookingButton` (Section 6), a status transition is a repeatable, in-place operation, not a one-shot creation — so this panel's double-submit guard is not a permanent latch: it is a synchronous `submitting` gate (blocking a concurrent duplicate submission while a request is in flight) plus a post-success duplicate guard scoped only to the specific `expectedStatus` value the panel most recently submitted. That guard remains active only while the page still server-renders that same, now-stale status; it resets the moment `router.refresh()` supplies a different server-rendered `currentStatus` reflecting the transition that just succeeded. A validated transition must never permanently prevent the Booking's next valid transition.

  **8. Assignment.** `/admin/bookings/[id]`'s Server Component calls the existing, already-implemented `features/assignments/repository.ts`'s `findActiveAssignmentForBooking` directly to read and display the Booking's current Travel Consultant assignment. This assignment lookup must run only **after** the page's own actor-scoped Booking detail lookup (the same lookup this page uses to authorize the request and render every Section 5 field) has already succeeded — never in parallel with it, and never before it. If that actor-scoped lookup fails (a genuinely missing Booking, or one an unassigned Travel Consultant may not access), `findActiveAssignmentForBooking` must not be called at all for that request: `findActiveAssignmentForBooking` performs no actor-scoping of its own, so calling it before or alongside the Booking authorization check would risk a data or timing signal for an inaccessible or nonexistent Booking that Section 2's existence-leak and timing discipline forbids. This sequencing is itself a same-process, owning-feature function call (assignments owns Booking-assignment data; `features/bookings`/its UI calling it introduces no cross-feature boundary violation per `.claude/rules/architecture.md`), not a new HTTP round-trip, and requires no new route, schema, or migration. No `GET /api/bookings/[id]/assignment` endpoint is introduced. `BookingAssignmentPanel` presents this data: for `ADMIN_MANAGER`, an interactive assign/reassign control, mirroring `ClientAssignmentPanel`/`LeadAssignmentPanel` exactly — eligible Travel Consultants fetched from the existing, unmodified `GET /api/assignments/travel-consultants`; a `reason` required only when replacing an already-active assignment (never on a first assignment); a client-side no-op guard blocking a reassignment to the already-assigned consultant, while the backend's own existing enforcement (`REASON_REQUIRED`, idempotent same-assignee no-op) remains the authoritative safeguard regardless of the client-side guard. For `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT`, the panel renders the identical read-only presentation `ClientAssignmentPanel`/`LeadAssignmentPanel` already use for a non-`ADMIN_MANAGER` viewer — no mutation control of any kind, consistent with Section 2.

  **9. Components.** This checkpoint authorizes exactly these new component names, matching this codebase's existing per-feature naming convention, unless implementation discovers an actual naming collision: `BookingTable`, `BookingStatusTransitionPanel`, `BookingAssignmentPanel`, `CreateBookingButton`. No `BookingStatusHistory` component (or equivalent) is authorized by this entry.

  **10. Explicit deferrals and Task Board handling.** The following are explicitly out of scope for this checkpoint, not silently resolved by it: Booking list filters of any kind; Booking status-history presentation (server- or client-rendered); any Booking visibility or capability for `FINANCE_ACCOUNTING` or `VISA_DOCUMENTATION`, consistent with blueprint Sections 4.4/4.5's eventual assignment-based intent remaining unimplemented until Payment/VisaCase features exist; a standalone booking-creation page or route; any edit capability for travel, financial, or notes fields; any new Booking API endpoint or response-shape expansion; and backfilling a real-PostgreSQL `features/bookings/service.integration.test.ts` — this last item is recorded here as an explicit, open follow-up risk (the Booking backend's SERIALIZABLE-concurrency and atomicity guarantees remain verified only against a mocked Prisma client, unlike every Lead/Client/Proposal/Staff/Assignment feature, each of which already has a real-Postgres integration tier), not as a gap this checkpoint treats as resolved or waives. This decision authorizes future implementation only; it does not itself implement any application code and does not modify `docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md` — the Phase 2 "Booking management UI using the existing Booking API..." checklist item remains unchecked until implementation, and its required test coverage, actually exist and pass, mirroring D-024 Section 12's, D-025 Section 12's, D-026 Section 12's, and D-027 Section 11's identical, already-established precedent exactly.

- **Rationale:** Building exclusively against the already-implemented, already-tested Booking and Booking-assignment backend (D-013; D-014; D-015) — rather than expanding its request/query/response contract — keeps this checkpoint additive and reviewable in isolation, consistent with every prior Phase 2 UI checkpoint's discipline of reusing existing service-layer primitives unmodified wherever they already suffice. Reading current assignment via `findActiveAssignmentForBooking` directly from the Server Component, rather than adding a new HTTP endpoint, avoids introducing API surface area purely to satisfy a UI read the owning feature can already serve in-process. Embedding Booking creation on the Proposal detail page, rather than a standalone page with its own picker, mirrors D-027 Section 8's identical reasoning for `CreateProposalPanel` — the precondition (an accepted `ProposalVersion`) is already established context on that page, so no new search/picker mechanism is needed. Excluding Finance/Accounting and Visa Documentation Staff from any Booking visibility, despite blueprint Sections 4.4/4.5's eventual intent, avoids granting UI-layer access that no backend scoping mechanism yet bounds — no Payment or VisaCase assignment concept exists yet to determine which Bookings either role should see, consistent with D-009(a)'s Finance-visibility principle remaining unimplemented for Booking as of this checkpoint. Recording the missing Booking integration-test tier explicitly, rather than silently building the UI as though backend coverage were complete, preserves this repository's `validation-deployment.md` discipline of never claiming a check passed that was not actually run.
- **Constraint:** `features/bookings/**`, `features/assignments/**` (including `findActiveAssignmentForBooking` itself), every existing Booking/assignment API route, `apps/web/prisma/schema.prisma`, and every existing migration receive no change of any kind from this decision. `docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md` is not modified by this entry. No component, page, or panel this checkpoint authorizes may exceed the following distinct boundaries: a mutation payload any component submits may contain only fields `createBookingSchema`, `updateBookingStatusSchema`, or `setAssignmentSchema` already authorize (`listBookingsQuerySchema` governs the list page's own query parameters, not a submitted payload); Booking list/detail data may display only fields the existing `BookingRecord` response shape (`features/bookings/repository.ts:58-100`) already returns, per Section 5's corrected enumeration — which excludes `totalAmount`/`currencyCode`; assignment presentation may display only the existing return shapes of `findActiveAssignmentForBooking` and `GET /api/assignments/travel-consultants`, called exactly as Section 8 describes; and `CreateBookingButton` may consume only the `proposalVersionId` identity and the accepted-response eligibility information (Section 6) already available to the Proposal detail flow it is embedded in. No expansion of any of these existing API response shapes is authorized by this decision.
- **Deferred:** Booking list filters; Booking status-history presentation; any editing capability for nullable Booking fields; Finance/Accounting and Visa Documentation Staff Booking access; a standalone booking-creation page; any new Booking API endpoint, request field, or response expansion; and a real-PostgreSQL `features/bookings/service.integration.test.ts` backfill (recorded as an open follow-up risk, not resolved).
- **Effect:** Authorizes implementation of the Phase 2 "Booking management UI using the existing Booking API (creation, list, detail, status transitions, assignment)" checklist item. Does not itself implement any UI code, and does not mark that — or any other — Task Board item complete; that update remains a later, separate closure checkpoint, made only once the implementation described above, and its required test coverage, actually exist and pass.

## D-029 — Admin Dashboard Overview

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Date accepted:** August 7, 2026
- **Decision:** The Admin Dashboard Overview checkpoint (blueprint Sections 2.2, 13.1; D-021's dependency-ordered Phase 2 sequence, which names "dashboard-overview work" as the checkpoint following Booking UI; `docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md`'s Phase 2 "Admin dashboard overview" item) is approved as follows. This entry is self-contained — it is the authoritative contract for implementation, not a pointer to a separate report. Correction Pass 1 (recorded within this same entry) reconciles this decision with D-023 Section 3's already-accepted but never-implemented post-login routing requirement, corrects the dashboard's query accounting to exactly five service calls, and makes every widget's content, links, and empty states fully deterministic.

  **1. Implementation boundary.** This checkpoint authorizes: a single new read-only Server Component route, `/admin` (`apps/web/src/app/admin/page.tsx`, plus its sibling `apps/web/src/app/admin/dashboard.module.css`), that aggregates already-existing, already-authorized Lead/Client/Proposal/Booking summary data by calling the existing `features/leads/service.ts`'s `listLeads`, `features/clients/service.ts`'s `listClients`, `features/proposals/service.ts`'s `listProposals`, and `features/bookings/service.ts`'s `listBookings` functions directly, exactly as `admin/leads/page.tsx`, `admin/clients/page.tsx`, `admin/proposals/page.tsx`, and `admin/bookings/page.tsx` already call their own feature's service; a new role-gated navigation link in `apps/web/src/app/admin/layout.tsx`; and, per Correction 1 (Section 2 below), a role-aware redirect added to `apps/web/src/app/dashboard/page.tsx`. `apps/web/src/app/login/page.tsx` is explicitly not modified (Section 2). It authorizes no Prisma schema change, no migration, no seed-script change, no new dependency, no mutation endpoint, and no change to any existing Lead, Client, Proposal, Booking, or Assignment mutation behavior. `listLeadsQuerySchema`, `listClientsQuerySchema`, `listProposalsQuerySchema`, and `listBookingsQuerySchema` are all reused unmodified — no new query parameter, filter field, or response field is added to any of them or to any API route.

  **2. Route behavior and navigation relationship.** Today, `apps/web/src/app/admin/` contains only `layout.tsx`/`layout.test.tsx` and the `bookings/`, `clients/`, `leads/`, `proposals/`, `_components/` subdirectories — no `page.tsx` exists at the bare `/admin` segment, so a request that reaches it today (after passing `proxy.ts`'s cookie-presence check and `admin/layout.tsx`'s own `STAFF_ROLES` check) renders Next.js's default not-found output, since a layout alone never renders route content. This checkpoint authorizes a new `apps/web/src/app/admin/page.tsx`, making `/admin` itself the Dashboard Overview and the operational landing page for `ADMIN_MANAGER` and `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT` — the natural landing page of the admin namespace, consistent with blueprint Section 2.2 listing "Dashboard overview" first among admin areas. `apps/web/src/app/admin/layout.tsx` additionally gains a role-gated "Dashboard" navigation link (placed first among the nav items, before "Leads," pointing to `/admin`), gated by a new dedicated role-set constant declared in that file — mirroring the file's own established `CLIENT_MANAGEMENT_ROLES`/`PROPOSAL_MANAGEMENT_ROLES`/`BOOKING_MANAGEMENT_ROLES` per-feature-constant convention (`layout.tsx:16,23,30`) exactly, not the always-rendered "Leads" pattern. No existing nav link on this page is removed or renamed.

  **Correction 1 — reconciling D-023 Section 3's post-login routing.** D-023 Section 3 already accepted that, "After successful login, `ADMIN_MANAGER` and `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT` users are routed to `/admin/leads`; every other role retains the existing `/dashboard` destination" — but this requirement was never actually implemented: `apps/web/src/app/login/page.tsx` calls `router.push('/dashboard')` unconditionally for every role, and `apps/web/src/app/dashboard/page.tsx` renders its Phase-1 verification content for every authenticated role with no role-aware redirect of any kind. This checkpoint closes that pre-existing D-023 implementation gap while updating the destination to the Dashboard Overview this decision newly makes available, superseding only D-023 Section 3's now-obsolete `/admin/leads` target: `/admin/leads` → `/admin`. Concretely, `apps/web/src/app/dashboard/page.tsx` becomes the authoritative role-aware Server Component redirect: unauthenticated continues to `redirect('/login')` exactly as today (unchanged); `ADMIN_MANAGER` or `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT` now calls `redirect('/admin')`; every other authenticated role (`SYSTEM_ADMINISTRATOR`, `FINANCE_ACCOUNTING`, `VISA_DOCUMENTATION`, `CLIENT`) retains the existing Phase-1 verification content exactly as rendered today, unchanged. `redirect()` must be called outside any `try`/`catch` — Next.js's `redirect()` works by throwing a control-flow signal that a surrounding `catch` would otherwise intercept and treat as an application error. `apps/web/src/app/login/page.tsx`'s own `router.push('/dashboard')` is explicitly not changed by this decision: every login continues to land on `/dashboard` first, which then performs the role-aware fork described above — `/dashboard` becomes the sole routing point, not `/login`. This checkpoint additionally authorizes the corresponding test coverage for `apps/web/src/app/dashboard/page.test.tsx` (Section 11).

  **3. Authorized roles.** Exactly `ADMIN_MANAGER` and `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT` may view `/admin` — mirroring D-022 through D-028's identical, unbroken `ALLOWED_ROLES = ['ADMIN_MANAGER', 'TRAVEL_CONSULTANT']` Layer-3 gate, independently confirmed present, in this exact form, on `admin/leads/page.tsx`, `admin/clients/page.tsx`, `admin/proposals/page.tsx`, and `admin/bookings/page.tsx`. `SYSTEM_ADMINISTRATOR`, `FINANCE_ACCOUNTING`, `VISA_DOCUMENTATION`, and `CLIENT` receive the identical in-place "Access denied" state every other Phase 2 admin page already renders for these roles — never a redirect — consistent with blueprint Section 4.1's explicit boundary that System Administrator access "does not automatically grant unrestricted access to operational client data," and with the fact that `listLeads`/`listClients`/`listProposals`/`listBookings` each already throw `ROLE_NOT_PERMITTED` (via `assertLeadActor`/`assertClientActor`/`assertProposalViewerActor`/`assertBookingActor`) for any other role, matching this same two-role boundary at the service layer regardless of what the page itself gates. `CLIENT` is additionally excluded one layer earlier by `admin/layout.tsx`'s own `STAFF_ROLES` gate, before this page is ever reached.

  **4. Role-specific visibility and assignment scoping.** `ADMIN_MANAGER` sees unrestricted, platform-wide counts and recent-item lists across all four features. `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT` sees only the Leads, Clients, Proposals, and Bookings actively assigned to them — enforced the same way every existing Phase 2 list page already enforces it: `listLeads`/`listClients`/`listProposals`/`listBookings` compose `leadAssignmentFilter`/`clientAssignmentFilter`/`proposalClientAssignmentFilter`/(Bookings' own) `clientAssignmentFilter` directly into each underlying Prisma `where` clause (`features/leads/repository.ts:36-47`, `features/clients/repository.ts:36-47`, `features/proposals/repository.ts:161`, `features/bookings/repository.ts`'s identical pattern) — database-level scoping, never a post-fetch filter and never a per-row `canAccessLead`/`canAccessClient` loop over dashboard results, which would deviate from `.claude/rules/admin-dashboard.md`'s Visibility Scoping rule exactly as both repositories' own doc comments already warn against for their respective list functions. This checkpoint introduces no new authorization mechanism of any kind — it calls the four existing, already-scoped service functions with no actor parameter of its own.

  **5. Dashboard sections/widgets.** The page's top-level heading is exactly `Dashboard overview`. Beneath it, exactly four sections, one per existing Phase 2 operational feature — Leads, Clients, Proposals, Bookings — no fifth section, no combined/cross-feature widget, and no widget backed by Payments, Documents, Support, Visa Cases, Notifications, or a general administrative-activity feed (Section 12). Each section contains, in this order: (a) its exact metric label and numeric value — always rendered, including exactly `0`, never hidden or omitted; (b) its exact recent-list heading; (c) its bounded recent-item list (at most 5 entries) or, when that specific call's own `items.length === 0`, its exact empty-state message (Section 6); a recent list's emptiness is controlled only by that call's own `items.length`, never by an unrelated total or a differently-filtered call's result (see the Leads New-inquiries/Recent-Leads distinction in Section 6); (d) exactly one "View all …" link to that feature's existing list page (Section 6).

  **6. Exact definition and source of every count, list, and link.** Exactly five service calls in total (never more; see Section 8 for the full database-operation accounting). Every recent-item list renders only existing DTO fields, using the existing admin list/detail formatting conventions for human-readable status and date presentation (each feature's own status-label map — e.g. `admin/leads/_components/leadStatusLabels.ts`, each Booking page/component's own local `BOOKING_STATUS_LABELS` copy — and each existing list/detail page's `toLocaleDateString('en-PH')`/`<time dateTime>` convention) — no additional contact, notes, assignment, proposal-content, or internal Booking field is ever exposed on the overview.
  - **Leads — "New inquiries" metric:** `listLeads(actor, { status: LeadStatus.NEW, page: 1, pageSize: 1 })`. `.total` is the blueprint's own named metric (Section 13.1), directly computable because `listLeadsQuerySchema` already accepts an optional `status` filter (`features/leads/schemas.ts:109-115`) and `LeadStatus.NEW` is the initial status every Lead is created with (`features/leads/repository.ts:233-259`). Always renders its numeric value, including `0`. No other Lead metric is added.
  - **Leads — "Recent Leads" list:** a separate call, `listLeads(actor, { page: 1, pageSize: 5 })`. `.items` — up to 5 leads, unfiltered by status, in the function's own fixed `createdAt desc, id desc` order (`features/leads/repository.ts:203`). This is a second, distinct call from the New-inquiries metric above — the two are never combined into one call and never share one result — because New-inquiries is deliberately status-filtered while this list is not: a `0` New-inquiries count must never be read as, or cause, an empty Recent Leads list, since older non-`NEW` leads may still populate it. Each entry's detail link is the Lead's `fullName`, targeting `/admin/leads/{lead.id}`; each entry additionally shows its human-readable `status` and `createdAt`. Empty state (when this call's own `items.length === 0`): "No leads yet. Leads created by staff will appear here." (the exact existing string at `admin/leads/page.tsx:143`). "View all leads" links to `/admin/leads`.
  - **Clients:** exactly one call, `listClients(actor, { page: 1, pageSize: 5 })`, reused for both this feature's metric and its list — never a second Client call. `.total` is "Total Clients" (always rendered, including `0`) — not "Active clients" (blueprint Section 13.1's phrasing): `Client` has no `status` field, and one is explicitly and deliberately not defined anywhere in the schema (`prisma/schema.prisma:1007-1011`'s and `:435-436`'s doc comments; corroborated by `features/clients/schemas.ts:156-159`, "No `status` filter exists... `Client.status` is not authorized") — inventing a status-based count here would fabricate a field this repository explicitly leaves an open, unresolved decision (D-021 Section 5(b)). `.items` is "Recent Clients" — up to 5, `createdAt desc, id desc` (`features/clients/repository.ts:161`); each entry's detail link is the Client's `fullName`, targeting `/admin/clients/{client.id}`, plus its `createdAt`. Empty state: "No clients yet. Clients created through lead conversion will appear here." (the exact existing string at `admin/clients/page.tsx:137`). "View all clients" links to `/admin/clients`.
  - **Proposals:** exactly one call, `listProposals(actor, { page: 1, pageSize: 5 })`, reused for both. `.total` is "Total Proposals" (always rendered, including `0`). `.items` is "Recent Proposals" — up to 5, `createdAt desc, id desc` (`features/proposals/repository.ts:167`). Each item is exactly `PROPOSAL_LIST_SELECT`'s shape — `id`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt`, `client: { id, fullName }` (`features/proposals/repository.ts:133-138`) — which deliberately excludes every `ProposalVersion` and `ProposalAcceptance` field and carries no proposal-level reference/title of its own; each entry's detail link text is therefore "Proposal for {client.fullName}", targeting `/admin/proposals/{proposal.id}`, plus its `createdAt`. Blueprint Section 13.1's "Pending quotation requests" is therefore explicitly not built: computing "Proposals awaiting response" would require either a new query shape (expanding `PROPOSAL_LIST_SELECT` or adding a new repository function) or fetching every Proposal's full detail in a loop, both outside this checkpoint's reuse-only boundary (Section 12). Empty state: "No proposals yet." (the exact existing string at `admin/proposals/page.tsx:102`). "View all proposals" links to `/admin/proposals`.
  - **Bookings:** exactly one call, `listBookings(actor, { page: 1, pageSize: 5 })`, reused for both. `.total` is "Total Bookings" (always rendered, including `0`). `.items` is "Recent Bookings" — up to 5, `createdAt desc, id desc` (`listBookingsForActor`); each entry's detail link is the Booking's `bookingReference`, targeting `/admin/bookings/{booking.id}`, plus its human-readable `status` and `createdAt`. Blueprint Section 13.1's "Upcoming departures" is explicitly not built: `listBookingsQuerySchema` (`features/bookings/schemas.ts:54-57`) accepts only `page`/`pageSize` — no status filter and no date-based filter or sort of any kind — and `listBookingsForActor`'s `orderBy` is fixed to `createdAt desc, id desc`, never `travelStartDate`; sourcing a genuine "soonest upcoming departure" list would require a new repository function/ordering this checkpoint does not authorize. Empty state: "No bookings yet." (the exact existing string at `admin/bookings/page.tsx:102`). "View all bookings" links to `/admin/bookings`.
  - No widget is backed by Payments, Documents, Support, Visa Cases, or Notifications (Section 12). No widget is backed by `AuditLog` or a general "recent administrative activity"/"important alerts" feed: the only `AuditLog` read anywhere in the codebase is `features/leads/repository.ts:626-639`'s `findLeadConversionAudit`, a single-Lead-scoped lookup for one specific audit entry, not a general list/query function — and blueprint Section 13.1's "Recent administrative activity" is exactly the `ActivityLog` concept D-021 Section 4 already left "unmodeled... an open question for a future decision, not resolved here." This checkpoint does not resolve it either.

  **7. Ordering, limits, and empty-state behavior.** Every recent-item list uses only the ordering its underlying call already fixes (`createdAt desc, id desc`) — this checkpoint never independently sorts, re-sorts, or filters results client- or server-side beyond the `pageSize`/`status` parameters Section 6 names. Every list is capped at 5 items via `pageSize: 5` passed straight into the existing, already-bounded (`min(1)`/`max(100)`) schema — never fetched unbounded and sliced in application code. Every metric always renders its numeric value, including exactly `0`. A recent list's own empty state is controlled solely by that specific call's own `items.length === 0` — never by a different feature's total, a different call's total, or any other proxy; this matters specifically for Leads, where the New-inquiries metric and the Recent-Leads list come from two independently-filtered calls (Section 6) and must never be conflated.

  **8. Data-access architecture.** No new feature module is introduced — no `features/dashboard/**` directory of any kind. Unlike Lead/Client/Proposal/Booking, the Dashboard Overview owns no domain entity of its own; it is a pure read-composition over four already-owned, already-authorized feature services, so a dedicated repository/service layer here would be exactly the kind of premature abstraction `.claude/rules/architecture.md` already warns against ("Do not introduce a shared abstraction... until at least two concrete features need it"). `apps/web/src/app/admin/page.tsx` (a Server Component) calls `listLeads`, `listClients`, `listProposals`, and `listBookings` directly — never a repository, and never an internal `fetch` to any API route, exactly mirroring every existing Phase 2 list page's own established pattern. No new API route is introduced. The dashboard issues exactly five service calls (Section 6: two `listLeads` calls, one `listClients` call, one `listProposals` call, one `listBookings` call) inside one outer `Promise.all`, mirroring the concurrency discipline each individual repository function already applies internally between its own `findMany`/`count` pair. The resulting database-operation count is ten, not five: each existing repository list function performs one `findMany` plus one `count` through its own internal `Promise.all`, so five service calls produce five `findMany` operations (each `take`-limited by its call's own `pageSize`) and five `count` operations (each actor-scoped by the same `where` as its paired `findMany`, but carrying no `LIMIT` of its own and therefore never described as "bounded"). No claim is made that any of these ten operations uses an applicable database index — independently proving each query path's actual index usage is outside this checkpoint's scope, so that claim is not made. The authenticated user (`AuthenticatedUser`, from `getCurrentUser()`) is passed as the `actor` argument to every one of the five calls, exactly as every existing Phase 2 list page already does — this checkpoint introduces no separate actor type, no new authorization mechanism, and no actor transformation of any kind. Authorization is enforced at the same four layers every other Phase 2 surface already uses: `proxy.ts`'s Layer 1 cookie-presence check, `admin/layout.tsx`'s Layer 2 `STAFF_ROLES` database-backed check, `admin/page.tsx`'s own Layer 3 `['ADMIN_MANAGER', 'TRAVEL_CONSULTANT']` check, and each called service function's own Layer 4 `assertLeadActor`/`assertClientActor`/`assertProposalViewerActor`/`assertBookingActor` defense-in-depth check.

  **9. Failure handling.** An unauthenticated request redirects to `/login` (identical to every existing Phase 2 page). A forbidden role renders the in-place "Access denied" state (Section 3) — never a redirect, never a partial dashboard. Because the five service calls (Section 6/8) are combined via a single `Promise.all`, any one of them rejecting (including an unexpected, non-`ROLE_NOT_PERMITTED` service failure) rejects the whole `Promise.all` and is intentionally not caught by `admin/page.tsx` — it bubbles to this route segment's nearest error boundary, exactly mirroring `admin/bookings/page.tsx`'s and `admin/proposals/page.tsx`'s own explicit "any unexpected error... is intentionally not caught here" convention for their single data source, extended here to five. No per-widget partial-degradation UI (e.g., three sections rendering while a fourth shows its own inline error) is introduced — that would be new failure-handling behavior with no existing precedent anywhere in this codebase, and is therefore out of scope for a checkpoint whose entire premise is reusing already-established patterns unmodified. No dashboard widget is ever permitted to render a fabricated, cached, or partially-stale count in place of a genuine failure.

  **10. Responsive and accessibility requirements.** A new `apps/web/src/app/admin/dashboard.module.css` (sibling to the new `page.tsx`) provides mobile-first styling, mirroring the established per-feature-CSS-module convention (`bookings.module.css`, `proposals.module.css`, `clients.module.css`) — each feature keeps its own copy rather than sharing one cross-feature module. Each of the four sections is a semantic `<section>` with its own heading; each count/metric uses a labelled `<dl>/<dt>/<dd>` pair, mirroring the `detailFields`/`detailField` convention already identical across the Booking, Proposal, and Client detail pages; each recent-item list is a semantic list (`<ul>` or `<ol>`) whose entries link to that record's own existing detail page. `/admin/page.tsx` is a pure Server Component with no `'use client'` directive anywhere in this checkpoint's scope — it has no form, button, or other mutation control, so no client-side interactivity is needed; all navigation (recent-item links, "View all" links, the new "Dashboard" nav link) is plain, keyboard-operable anchors, mirroring `Pagination.tsx`'s identical link-only convention.

  **11. Automated-test contract.**
  - `/admin/page.test.tsx`, at minimum: unauthenticated redirects to `/login`; each of the four excluded roles (`SYSTEM_ADMINISTRATOR`, `FINANCE_ACCOUNTING`, `VISA_DOCUMENTATION`, `CLIENT`) renders "Access denied" and calls none of the five service invocations; for `ADMIN_MANAGER`, exactly five service invocations occur with the exact actor and query arguments Section 6 specifies; for `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT`, the identical exact five invocations with that actor; the Clients/Proposals/Bookings sections' total and items are each asserted to come from the same single mocked service result per feature (never two separate mock calls for one feature); a scenario with a `0` Leads New-inquiries count and a non-empty Recent Leads list renders both correctly, proving the zero metric never hides the non-empty list; each recent-item's exact visible fields and exact detail-page link (Section 6); each exact "View all …" link; all four exact empty-state messages (Section 6), each triggered only by its own call's `items.length === 0`; a rejection from any one of the five service calls causes the page itself to reject (`rejects.toThrow`), with no partial dashboard ever rendered — mirroring `admin/bookings/page.test.tsx`'s existing "rethrows an unexpected error" test shape.
  - `admin/layout.test.tsx`, at minimum: the "Dashboard" navigation link is visible only for `ADMIN_MANAGER` and `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT` (an `it.each` role-gating test mirroring the file's existing per-link pattern exactly, covering both the two authorized roles and the excluded roles); it targets exactly `/admin`; it is the first item in the navigation list, before "Leads."
  - `apps/web/src/app/dashboard/page.test.tsx`, at minimum: unauthenticated redirects to `/login`; `ADMIN_MANAGER` redirects to `/admin`; `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT` redirects to `/admin`; `SYSTEM_ADMINISTRATOR`, `FINANCE_ACCOUNTING`, `VISA_DOCUMENTATION`, and `CLIENT` each remain on the existing Phase-1 verification content and never redirect to `/admin`.
  - Scope of what these mocked tests do and do not prove: every `/admin/page.test.tsx` test above mocks `listLeads`/`listClients`/`listProposals`/`listBookings` directly, so it proves the exact authenticated actor is passed to each already-scoped service and that the page renders only the DTO data those services return — it does not, and cannot, independently re-prove Prisma/database-level assignment scoping while those services are mocked. The existing service- and repository-level (including real-PostgreSQL integration, where already present) tests for `features/leads/**`, `features/clients/**`, `features/proposals/**`, and `features/bookings/**` remain the sole authoritative coverage for actual database-level assignment scoping; this checkpoint does not modify, duplicate, or weaken any of them.

  **12. Explicit constraints and deferrals.** The following are explicitly out of scope for this checkpoint, not silently resolved by it: any widget backed by Payments, Documents, Support & Messages, Visa Cases, or Notifications (none of these features has an implemented repository/service read layer beyond Stage-A schema per D-017 through D-020); a general "recent administrative activity" or "important alerts" feed of any kind (the `ActivityLog` concept D-021 Section 4 already left unmodeled); a Booking-status-filtered metric of any kind (no status filter exists in `listBookingsQuerySchema`, and adding one is not authorized here); an "Upcoming departures" (travel-date-ordered) Booking list; a "Proposals awaiting response"/"Pending quotation requests" list (the existing list DTO excludes acceptance data); any `Client.status`-based metric (no such field exists); any change to `apps/web/src/app/login/page.tsx`'s own redirect target — it continues calling `router.push('/dashboard')` unconditionally for every role (Section 2); any new query parameter, filter field, or response field on any of the four existing list schemas or their API routes; and any dashboard content or visibility for `SYSTEM_ADMINISTRATOR`, `FINANCE_ACCOUNTING`, or `VISA_DOCUMENTATION` (their own future operational surfaces, once Payment/VisaCase/System-Settings features exist, remain a separate, later decision).

  **13. Task Board handling.** This decision authorizes future implementation only; it does not itself implement any application code and does not modify `docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md` — the Phase 2 "Admin dashboard overview" checklist item remains unchecked until implementation, and its required test coverage, actually exist and pass, mirroring D-022 through D-028's identical, already-established precedent exactly.

- **Rationale:** Composing the dashboard entirely from the four already-implemented, already-tested Phase 2 list services — rather than introducing a dedicated dashboard repository/service layer or expanding any existing query schema — keeps this checkpoint additive, reviewable in isolation, and free of any new authorization surface to get wrong, consistent with every prior Phase 2 UI checkpoint's discipline of reusing existing service-layer primitives unmodified wherever they already suffice. Deferring every blueprint Section 13.1 item this repository cannot yet honestly compute (Payments, Documents, Support, Visa, Notifications, ActivityLog, Upcoming departures, Pending quotation requests, Active-clients-by-status) rather than approximating or fabricating them preserves `.claude/rules/frontend.md`'s content-integrity discipline and this repository's established practice of never inventing a status, field, or metric the underlying data model does not actually define. Placing the new "Dashboard" nav link first, gated identically to the existing feature links, keeps `admin/layout.tsx`'s navigation internally consistent rather than introducing a second gating convention. Using a single `Promise.all` with fail-together semantics, rather than inventing per-widget partial-degradation handling, avoids introducing an entirely new failure-handling pattern this codebase has never needed before (every prior page has had exactly one data source), and keeps this checkpoint's behavior predictable and easy to reason about under `.claude/rules/backend.md`'s "Consistent Error Responses" spirit. Closing D-023 Section 3's post-login routing gap here, rather than leaving it open indefinitely, reflects that the missing piece — a real destination for `ADMIN_MANAGER`/`TRAVEL_CONSULTANT` beyond `/admin/leads` — only becomes available once this checkpoint's `/admin` Dashboard Overview exists; updating the destination from D-023's original `/admin/leads` to the now-available `/admin` is a narrow, evidence-driven correction to an already-accepted requirement, not a new product decision. Placing the redirect in `dashboard/page.tsx` rather than changing `login/page.tsx` keeps the actual authentication/sign-in flow completely untouched, consistent with this checkpoint's broader no-mutation, no-existing-behavior-expansion discipline — `/dashboard` was always the generic post-login landing page every role already passes through, so it is the natural, minimal place for a role-aware fork to live. Reusing each feature's single list call for both its metric and its recent list (rather than one call per purpose) avoids doubling the dashboard's query count for Clients, Proposals, and Bookings with no benefit, since the same actor-scoped `where` already produces both `total` and `items` in one round trip.
- **Constraint:** `features/leads/**`, `features/clients/**`, `features/proposals/**`, `features/bookings/**`, `features/assignments/**`, every existing Lead/Client/Proposal/Booking/Assignment API route, `apps/web/prisma/schema.prisma`, every existing migration, and `apps/web/src/app/login/page.tsx` receive no change of any kind from this decision. `docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md` is not modified by this entry. No component or page this checkpoint authorizes may exceed the following boundary: `/admin/page.tsx` may call only `listLeads`, `listClients`, `listProposals`, and `listBookings`, exactly five times total (Section 6/8), each with only `page`/`pageSize` (plus, for Leads' New-inquiries call only, the already-existing `status` filter) — no other parameter, no mutation call, and no direct repository or database access of any kind. `apps/web/src/app/dashboard/page.tsx`'s change is limited exactly to the role-aware `redirect('/admin')` branch described in Section 2 — no other change to its existing unauthenticated/other-role behavior is authorized. Displayed data may include only the fields each function's existing response shape already returns (Section 6) — no expansion of any of these existing response shapes is authorized by this decision.
- **Deferred:** Payments/Documents/Support/Visa/Notification-backed widgets; a general administrative-activity or alerts feed; a Booking-status-filtered metric; an "Upcoming departures" Booking list; a "Proposals awaiting response" list; any `Client.status`-based metric; any change to `login/page.tsx`'s own redirect target; any new query parameter, filter, or response field on the four existing list schemas/routes; and any dashboard visibility for `SYSTEM_ADMINISTRATOR`, `FINANCE_ACCOUNTING`, or `VISA_DOCUMENTATION`.
- **Effect:** Authorizes implementation of the Phase 2 "Admin dashboard overview" checklist item, and closes D-023 Section 3's previously-unimplemented post-login routing requirement by retargeting it from `/admin/leads` to `/admin`. Does not itself implement any UI code, and does not mark that — or any other — Task Board item complete; that update remains a later, separate closure checkpoint, made only once the implementation described above, and its required test coverage, actually exist and pass.

## D-030 — Phase 2 AuditLog Coverage Confirmation and Minimal Assignment/Booking Integration-Test Closure

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Date accepted:** August 10, 2026
- **Decision:** The AuditLog coverage checkpoint (`docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md` Phase 2's "`AuditLog` coverage for new Phase 2 sensitive mutations" item; blueprint Section 14.9; `.claude/rules/backend.md`'s Auditability rule; `.claude/rules/database-security.md`'s Audit Records rule; D-022 through D-029) is approved as follows, on the basis of an exhaustive, evidence-based discovery pass (recorded in this session as "D-030 Stage 0 — Phase 2 AuditLog Coverage Discovery and Contract Recommendation," Correction Pass 4, the sole governing discovery result for this entry). This entry is self-contained — it is the authoritative contract for implementation, not a pointer to a separate report.

  **1. Discovery conclusion.** All 16 Phase 2 mutation operations in scope for this checkpoint — `createLead`, `updateLead`, `updateLeadStatus`, `setLeadAssignment`, `endLeadAssignment`, `convertLead`, `updateClient`, `setClientAssignment`, `endClientAssignment`, `createProposal`, `createProposalRevision`, `publishProposalVersion`, `recordProposalResponse`, `createBooking`, `updateBookingStatus`, and `setBookingAssignment` — were exhaustively traced from entry point through their successful database transaction and their existing tests. All 16 already write correct, atomic, actor-attributed, PII-free `AuditLog` records: every audit write executes inside the same `prisma.$transaction`/`runSerializableWithRetry` callback and receives the identical `Prisma.TransactionClient` as its corresponding business mutation, so the business write and its audit row(s) commit or roll back together; every audit snapshot is an explicit, hand-written allow-list containing no raw PII, free text, `ProposalVersion.content`, or concurrency/transition token; every idempotent no-op and every rejected mutation writes neither a business row nor an audit row. No application, service, repository, sanitizer, schema, or migration defect was found anywhere in this surface — a full sweep of every `.create`/`.update`/`.updateMany`/`.delete`/`.deleteMany` call across `features/{leads,clients,bookings,proposals,assignments}/repository.ts` confirmed every business write maps to an already-traced, correctly atomic audit call site, with no orphaned mutation found.

  Of the `AuditLog.action` constants these 16 operations rely on, 21 are defined across the five owning `audit.ts` files (`leads/audit.ts`: 5; `clients/audit.ts`: 1; `assignments/audit.ts`: 9; `proposals/audit.ts`: 4; `bookings/audit.ts`: 2), of which 20 are reachable by at least one of the 16 operations and exactly one — `BOOKING_ASSIGNMENT_ENDED` — is defined-only and unreachable, because no `endBookingAssignment` capability exists anywhere in `features/assignments/service.ts` (a deliberate, already-documented non-capability, not a gap).

  9 of the 16 operations (`createLead`, `updateLead`, `updateLeadStatus`, `convertLead`, `updateClient`, `createProposal`, `createProposalRevision`, `publishProposalVersion`, `recordProposalResponse`) have direct same-function real-PostgreSQL coverage: an existing integration test calls that exact function against a real `heritage_v3_test` database and asserts the exact persisted `action`/`entityType`/`entityId`/`actorId`. The remaining 7 operations (`setLeadAssignment`, `endLeadAssignment`, `setClientAssignment`, `endClientAssignment`, `createBooking`, `updateBookingStatus`, `setBookingAssignment`), covering 10 distinct audit-action strings (`LEAD_ASSIGNMENT_CREATED`, `LEAD_ASSIGNMENT_REPLACED`, `LEAD_ASSIGNMENT_ENDED`, `CLIENT_ASSIGNMENT_CREATED`, `CLIENT_ASSIGNMENT_REPLACED`, `CLIENT_ASSIGNMENT_ENDED`, `BOOKING_ASSIGNMENT_CREATED`, `BOOKING_ASSIGNMENT_REPLACED`, `BOOKING_CREATED`, `BOOKING_STATUS_CHANGED`), lack direct same-function real-database verification, though every one of the 7 operations' implementations remains code-trace-verified correct and covered by mocked, operation-level tests. The evidence for these 10 actions splits into three tiers: two actions (`LEAD_ASSIGNMENT_CREATED`, `CLIENT_ASSIGNMENT_CREATED`) have mocked coverage of their own dedicated assignment function plus indirect real-database evidence through an unrelated code path (`createLead`'s embedded Travel-Consultant self-assignment and `convertLead`'s embedded inherited-assignment branch, respectively, each calling the assignments repository directly rather than `setLeadAssignment`/`setClientAssignment`); seven actions have action-specific mocked evidence only, with no real-database evidence of any kind, direct or indirect; and one action, `CLIENT_ASSIGNMENT_REPLACED`, has no action-specific automated-test coverage at either the mocked or the real-database tier, while the shared replacement-branch logic it relies on (`features/assignments/service.ts`'s generic `setAssignment` function) remains code-traced and is exercised and passing through its Lead-target and Booking-target test cases.

  **2. Exact authorized implementation scope.** This decision authorizes exactly two test-file changes, and no other file of any kind:
  - **Extend** `apps/web/src/features/assignments/service.integration.test.ts` to add direct real-PostgreSQL mutation/audit coverage for: `setLeadAssignment` (`LEAD_ASSIGNMENT_CREATED`, `LEAD_ASSIGNMENT_REPLACED`); `endLeadAssignment` (`LEAD_ASSIGNMENT_ENDED`); `setClientAssignment` (`CLIENT_ASSIGNMENT_CREATED`, `CLIENT_ASSIGNMENT_REPLACED`); `endClientAssignment` (`CLIENT_ASSIGNMENT_ENDED`); and `setBookingAssignment` (`BOOKING_ASSIGNMENT_CREATED`, `BOOKING_ASSIGNMENT_REPLACED`) — 8 real-database assertions in total, each verifying the exact persisted `action`, `entityType`, `entityId`, and `actorId` against the real database.
  - **Add** `apps/web/src/features/bookings/service.integration.test.ts`, guarded by this feature's own copy of the established `heritage_v3_test`/`validateTestDatabaseUrl` safety discipline (mirroring `features/leads/service.integration.test.ts`'s and `features/assignments/service.integration.test.ts`'s existing precedent exactly), scoped only to direct real-PostgreSQL verification of `BOOKING_CREATED` and `BOOKING_STATUS_CHANGED`: persisted action, entity type, entity id, actor attribution, atomicity with the corresponding business write, and PII-free sanitized snapshot state.

  No application code, service, repository, sanitizer, schema, or migration file is authorized to change by this decision. `CLIENT_ASSIGNMENT_REPLACED`'s gap is closed exclusively by the real-database case added to `assignments/service.integration.test.ts` above — no corresponding case is authorized or required in the mocked `assignments/service.test.ts`.

  **3. Booking fixture contract.** The verified minimum fixture chain for the `setBookingAssignment` real-database case above is, in order: (a) a raw real `Client` fixture row, inserted directly via `prisma.client.create(...)`, mirroring the already-established `createClientFixture` precedent in `features/clients/service.integration.test.ts` (no application-level Client-creation path exists outside Lead-to-Client conversion, so a raw fixture insert is the established pattern for test setup); (b) the real `createProposal` service call, producing a `Proposal` and its first `ProposalVersion`; (c) the real `publishProposalVersion` service call; (d) the real `recordProposalResponse` service call with `responseType: 'ACCEPT'`; and (e) the real `createBooking` service call. Publication (step c) is required — not optional — because `recordProposalResponse` (`features/proposals/service.ts`) accepts a response only against the Proposal's current client-visible version (`clientVisibleAt` set, `supersededAt` null); a version fresh from `createProposal` alone starts with `clientVisibleAt: null` and is rejected with `PROPOSAL_VERSION_NOT_CURRENT`, confirmed directly by `features/proposals/service.integration.test.ts`'s own dedicated test of that exact rejection. `createProposalRevision` is not required: nothing in the verified chain depends on a second `ProposalVersion` — the first version, once published, is sufficient for both `recordProposalResponse` and `createBooking`.

  **4. Required invariants.** Every audit write uses the same transaction client and the same transaction boundary as its business mutation. The business write and every applicable audit row it produces commit or roll back together. Audit-write ordering inside a transaction is not itself the source of this atomicity — several of the 13 audit-writing implementation functions write more than one audit row per invocation, and an earlier write may precede further transactional work or a later write within the same callback; atomicity comes from the shared transaction boundary and `tx` client, never from write position. Rejected and idempotent no-op mutations produce no `AuditLog` row. Audit snapshots remain explicit, hand-written allow-lists and exclude PII, free text, `ProposalVersion.content`, and concurrency/transition tokens (`expectedStatus`, `expectedUpdatedAt`, `expectedCurrentVersionId`, and equivalents). Assignment changes (`setAssignment`/`endAssignment`) record both the prior and the new assignee state in `beforeState`/`afterState`. Lifecycle transitions (`updateLeadStatus`, `updateBookingStatus`) record both the prior and the new status. `AuditLog` remains append-only — no role, and no code path introduced or touched by this decision, may update or delete an existing `AuditLog` row.

  **5. Explicit out of scope.** This decision does not authorize, and this checkpoint does not perform: any production implementation change to any service, repository, route, UI component, or schema file; any Prisma schema or migration change; any change to an existing action name, entity-type constant, or sanitizer function; any change to authorization, role, or assignment-boundary logic; the full Booking business/concurrency real-PostgreSQL integration-test backfill beyond the two audit-specific assertions in Section 2 (the broader backfill remains D-028 Section 10's already-recorded, still-open follow-up risk, and remains the separate, later Phase 2 "Automated test coverage for all of the above" checklist item's responsibility, not this one's); adding a `CLIENT_ASSIGNMENT_REPLACED` case to the mocked `assignments/service.test.ts` (an optional future defense-in-depth improvement, deliberately not bundled into this decision's two-file scope); a general `AuditLog` reader/browser UI (already deferred by D-023 Section 12 and D-029 Section 12); marking any Task Board item other than the single "`AuditLog` coverage for new Phase 2 sensitive mutations" line; and any edit to `docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md` at this stage.

  **6. Verification and completion gate.** The Phase 2 "`AuditLog` coverage for new Phase 2 sensitive mutations" Task Board line may be closed only after: both authorized integration-test files (Section 2) exist; all 10 affected actions named in Section 1 have direct real-database verification through them; both files pass against `heritage_v3_test` using the existing `TEST_DATABASE_URL`/`validateTestDatabaseUrl` safety guards and the `SELECT current_database()` runtime re-check already established by every sibling integration suite; the full existing automated test suite continues to pass; lint and typecheck pass; and the implementation-stage diff contains exactly the two files authorized in Section 2 — no other file. Task Board closure itself occurs later, as a separate, later stage and its own separate commit, exactly mirroring D-022 through D-029's identical, already-established precedent of never marking a Task Board item complete from within the decision that authorizes the underlying work.

  **7. No migration required.** No database migration is required or authorized by this decision. `AuditLog` (`apps/web/prisma/schema.prisma:727-743`, migrated once, in Phase 1's `20260713062622_staff_role_management_foundation`, and never altered since) already fully supports every mechanism described above — every action name, entity type, actor attribution, and before/after snapshot shape this checkpoint relies on already exists and is already in use.

- **Rationale:** Reusing each feature's own existing, already-established integration-test file and safety discipline — rather than inventing a new cross-feature test module or a shared audit-testing abstraction — keeps this checkpoint additive and consistent with the codebase's deliberate per-feature `audit.ts`/`insertAuditLog` convention (`.claude/rules/architecture.md`'s premature-abstraction rule; D-030 Stage 0 discovery, Section 4.3). Scoping the two authorized files exactly to the 10 actions the discovery pass actually found unverified — rather than a broader Booking or assignment test rewrite — keeps this decision minimal and reviewable, and avoids silently absorbing the separate, later "Role and assignment boundary verification," "Automated test coverage for all of the above," and "End-to-end verification of the full lead → client → proposal → booking flow" Task Board items into this one. Requiring the `CLIENT_ASSIGNMENT_REPLACED` case specifically in the real-database file, rather than accepting a cheaper mocked-only addition, closes the one action in the entire Phase 2 surface with no action-specific automated-test coverage at any tier, consistent with `.claude/rules/admin-dashboard.md`'s Audit Trails requirement. Requiring the full `createProposal` → `publishProposalVersion` → `recordProposalResponse` → `createBooking` chain for the Booking fixture, rather than assuming an unpublished first version suffices, reflects the discovery pass's own direct verification of `recordProposalResponse`'s actual precondition against its implementation, its repository invariants, and an existing, directly on-point real-database integration test — not an inference from `createBooking`'s eligibility query alone.
- **Constraint:** No `schema.prisma` change and no migration are authorized or required by this decision (Section 7). No existing action name, entity-type constant, sanitizer function, service, repository, route, or UI component is renamed, altered, or otherwise changed by this decision — every one of the 13 audit-writing implementation functions and 20 reachable action strings identified by the governing discovery pass remains exactly as already implemented. `docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md` is not modified by this entry. The two files authorized in Section 2 are the only files this decision authorizes changing; no other test file, including `assignments/service.test.ts`, may be modified under this decision.
- **Deferred:** The full Booking business/concurrency real-PostgreSQL integration-test backfill beyond the two named audit assertions (remains D-028 Section 10's open follow-up and the later "Automated test coverage for all of the above" item's responsibility); a mocked `CLIENT_ASSIGNMENT_REPLACED` case in `assignments/service.test.ts`; a general `AuditLog` reader/browser UI; role and assignment boundary verification across every new Lead/Client/Proposal/Booking surface (its own, separate Task Board item); and end-to-end verification of the full lead → client → proposal → booking flow in a real PostgreSQL-backed environment (its own, separate Task Board item).
- **Effect:** Authorizes implementation of exactly the two test-file changes in Section 2, toward closing the Phase 2 "`AuditLog` coverage for new Phase 2 sensitive mutations" checklist item. Does not itself implement either file, and does not mark that — or any other — Task Board item complete; that update remains a later, separate closure checkpoint, made only once both files exist and their required verification (Section 6) actually passes.

## D-031 — Phase 2 Role and Assignment Boundary Verification: Concurrency-Recheck and Defense-in-Depth Contract

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Date accepted:** August 12, 2026
- **Decision:** The role-and-assignment-boundary-verification checkpoint (`docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md` Phase 2's "Role and assignment boundary verification across every new Lead/Client/Proposal/Booking surface" item; blueprint Sections 4, 5, 6.4, 6.7, 9, 9.1; `.claude/rules/backend.md`'s layered-authorization and idempotency rules; `.claude/rules/admin-dashboard.md`'s per-record re-authorization rule; D-022 through D-030) is approved as follows, on the basis of an exhaustive, evidence-based discovery pass and its four subsequent correction passes (recorded in this session as "D-031 Stage 0 — Phase 2 Role and Assignment Boundary Verification Discovery," Correction Passes 1 through 4, the sole governing discovery result for this entry). This entry is self-contained — it is the authoritative contract for implementation, not a pointer to a separate report.

  **1. Purpose and boundary.** This decision governs exactly the implementation and test work needed to complete the Task Board's "Role and assignment boundary verification across every new Lead/Client/Proposal/Booking surface" item. It is a narrow boundary-hardening decision. It does not redefine the application's existing role model, ownership model, assignment policy, or any accepted D-022 through D-030 behavior — every role/assignment rule this discovery examined was found to already match its governing decision exactly (Section 2 below), with the sole exceptions recorded as defects in Sections 3 and 4.

  **2. Accepted surface inventory.** The discovery pass enumerated exactly 38 canonical Lead/Client/Proposal/Booking-related surfaces, independently classified each into one of five categories, and reconciled the total exactly: **Category A (implementation correct and boundary directly tested) = 28; Category B (implementation correct, boundary-test evidence incomplete) = 6; Category C (implementation or policy defect) = 4; Category D (accepted-contract ambiguity) = 0; Category E (not applicable / out of scope) = 0. 28 + 6 + 4 + 0 + 0 = 38.** This classification is accepted as-is; no surface is reclassified, renumbered, or reinterpreted by this decision.
  - **Category A (28):** `ASGN-01` (shared `canAccessLead`/`canAccessClient` authorization primitives); `ASGN-02` (`GET /api/assignments/travel-consultants`); `LEAD-01` (`POST /api/leads`); `LEAD-02` (`GET /api/leads` list + `/admin/leads`); `LEAD-03` (`GET /api/leads/[id]` detail); `LEAD-06` (`GET /api/leads/[id]/status-history`); `LEAD-07` (`POST /api/leads/[id]/conversion` + conversion-options read); `LEAD-09` (`/admin/leads/new`); `CLIENT-01` (`GET /api/clients` list + `/admin/clients`); `CLIENT-02` (`GET /api/clients/[id]` detail); `CLIENT-05` (`CreateProposalPanel` entry point); `PROPOSAL-01` through `PROPOSAL-07` (Proposal list, detail, create, revision, publish, external-response recording, and the Booking-creation entry point on the Proposal detail page); `BOOKING-05` (`PUT /api/bookings/[id]/assignment`); `CROSS-01` through `CROSS-09` (`proxy.ts`; `admin/layout.tsx`; `admin/page.tsx`; `dashboard/page.tsx`; `login/page.tsx`; `admin/_components/Pagination.tsx`; `GET /api/me`; `GET /api/health`; `/api/auth/[...all]`).
  - **Category B (6):** `LEAD-08` (`PUT`/`DELETE /api/leads/[id]/assignment` + `LeadAssignmentPanel`); `CLIENT-04` (`PUT`/`DELETE /api/clients/[id]/assignment` + `ClientAssignmentPanel`); `BOOKING-01` (`GET /api/bookings` list); `BOOKING-02` (`GET /api/bookings/[id]` detail); `BOOKING-03` (`POST /api/bookings` create); `BOOKING-04` (`PUT /api/bookings/[id]/status`).
  - **Category C (4):** `ASGN-03` (the shared assignment-mutation engine `setAssignment`/`endAssignment` in `features/assignments/service.ts`); `LEAD-04` (`PATCH /api/leads/[id]` → `updateLead`); `LEAD-05` (`PUT /api/leads/[id]/status` → `updateLeadStatus`); `CLIENT-03` (`PATCH /api/clients/[id]` → `updateClient`).
  - **Category D:** none. **Category E:** none.

  **3. F-01 — Medium implementation defect: incomplete transaction-local assignment-recheck protection in `updateLead`, `updateLeadStatus`, and `updateClient`.** Affects `LEAD-04`, `LEAD-05`, and `CLIENT-03`. The discovery pass found that the assignment-based authorization check performed for a `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT` actor, and the protected business mutation it is meant to gate, are not guaranteed today to operate against one consistent, serializable transactional view of the target's `StaffAssignment` state. Concretely: `updateLead` (`features/leads/service.ts`) and `updateClient` (`features/clients/service.ts`) each run their write inside a plain `prisma.$transaction`, whose isolation level is whatever the connected PostgreSQL database happens to be configured with rather than an isolation level this codebase's application layer itself enforces; `updateLead` and `updateLeadStatus` (`features/leads/service.ts`) perform no transaction-local recheck of the acting consultant's Lead assignment at all — `updateLeadStatus` rechecks only `Lead.status`/`expectedStatus`, never the assignment; `updateClient` already performs a transaction-local recheck of the Client assignment, and that recheck is real-database proven to close the race window between the pre-transaction authorization check and the moment the recheck itself executes, but is not proven — and, under its current plain-transaction mechanism, is not actually closed — against a second race window in which the assignment changes after that recheck succeeds but before the mutation's business write commits.

  Stage 2 implementation must, for all three functions:
  - Use the repository's existing `runSerializableWithRetry` mechanism (`apps/web/src/lib/serializable-transaction.ts`) as the transaction wrapper. `updateLeadStatus` already uses this mechanism and requires no isolation-level change. `updateLead` and `updateClient` must each be converted from their current plain `prisma.$transaction` to `runSerializableWithRetry`.
  - Perform the applicable assignment-authorization recheck — `assignmentRepository.findActiveAssignmentForLead(tx, id)` for `updateLead`/`updateLeadStatus`, `assignmentRepository.findActiveAssignmentForClient(tx, id)` for `updateClient` (both already-existing, already-used-elsewhere repository functions, already accepting a `Prisma.TransactionClient`) — for a `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT` actor only, as the first operation inside the transaction, keeping the recheck strictly transaction-local. `updateClient`'s existing recheck is retained, not replaced, and now runs under the stronger isolation level.
  - Preserve every existing role and assignment policy this discovery verified elsewhere in the codebase without alteration — `ADMIN_MANAGER` continues to require no recheck of any kind, remaining unconditionally authorized once the target's existence is confirmed.
  - Prevent an assignment change occurring during the mutation from allowing a now-stale authorization decision to protect a write that should no longer be permitted.

  **Required error behavior**, distinguishing two outcome paths precisely:
  - A retry attempt whose own transaction-local recheck observes that the acting consultant's assignment access has been lost or changed must return the existing controlled `LEAD_FORBIDDEN` (Lead) or `CLIENT_FORBIDDEN` (Client) result — both already-defined, already-used error codes, no new code introduced. That controlled forbidden result is a plain application error, not a Prisma-coded conflict, and must not itself be retried by `runSerializableWithRetry`'s retry logic.
  - If a retryable `P2034` serialization conflict recurs on every one of `runSerializableWithRetry`'s bounded attempts, the exhausted conflict must be mapped to the existing controlled `LEAD_CONFLICT` (Lead) or `CLIENT_CONFLICT` (Client) result — again, both already-defined, already-used codes reused for this new code path, never invented. This exhaustion outcome must never be represented as `LEAD_FORBIDDEN`/`CLIENT_FORBIDDEN` (an exhausted, ambiguous retry sequence never actually confirms the specific denial a forbidden result asserts), and no raw Prisma or PostgreSQL error of any kind may escape to the route layer or caller.
  - Neither outcome path may produce an `AuditLog` row for the rejected business mutation. A concurrent `ADMIN_MANAGER` reassignment that legitimately triggers this race by committing during it separately, correctly writes its own `LEAD_ASSIGNMENT_CREATED`/`LEAD_ASSIGNMENT_REPLACED`/`CLIENT_ASSIGNMENT_CREATED`/`CLIENT_ASSIGNMENT_REPLACED`-family audit entry (already covered by D-030) for that successful, separate mutation; this legitimate audit entry must never be read, in any test or in any implementation reasoning, as evidence of an audit entry for the rejected, unauthorized business mutation, which correctly produces none.

  **Mechanism note, required to keep the tests below executable.** `runSerializableWithRetry` (`apps/web/src/lib/serializable-transaction.ts`) re-invokes its entire callback — including the transaction-local recheck — fresh, inside a brand-new transaction, on every one of its bounded attempts (three total: an initial attempt plus up to two retries), and retries only a Prisma-coded `P2034` conflict; any other thrown error, including a domain error such as `LEAD_FORBIDDEN`, propagates immediately and ends the attempt loop (confirmed directly against this file's own implementation and its existing test suite, `lib/serializable-transaction.test.ts`). Consequently, a single real, concurrently-committing `ADMIN_MANAGER` reassignment does not, and cannot be relied on to, deterministically exhaust all bounded attempts: it can cause `P2034` on at most the one attempt genuinely racing against it, and the very next attempt's own fresh transaction-local recheck then observes the reassignment's already-committed effect and correctly throws the controlled forbidden result — which is a plain domain error, not a `P2034`, and is therefore not retried further. Genuine bounded-retry exhaustion (every attempt independently receiving `P2034`) is not an outcome one committed concurrent reassignment can be relied on to produce, and this decision does not require or claim otherwise.

  **Required tests, for each of `updateLead`, `updateLeadStatus`, and `updateClient` individually** (a shared test helper may be used only if each resulting case remains individually attributable to its specific function):
  1. **Assignment loss followed by a successful retry**, proven at two tiers: (i) a deterministic mocked unit test in which the first simulated transaction attempt receives a `P2034`-shaped conflict and the second attempt's mocked transaction-local recheck reports the assignment lost or reassigned, asserting the function returns the controlled forbidden result and that this result is not itself retried; and (ii) a genuine real-PostgreSQL concurrency test against `heritage_v3_test` that (a) begins the assignment-scoped mutation as a real, in-flight transaction; (b) allows its transaction-local assignment recheck to execute and succeed; (c) deterministically pauses the mutation after that recheck but before its business write or commit; (d) runs and commits exactly one real, concurrent `ADMIN_MANAGER` reassignment against the same target; and (e) resumes the paused mutation and asserts it resolves to the controlled forbidden result via PostgreSQL's own serialization-conflict detection and `runSerializableWithRetry`'s retry — never a silent success and never a raw database error.
  2. **Genuine bounded-retry exhaustion**, proven by a deterministic mocked unit test only, mirroring this codebase's own already-established precedent for the identical mechanism (`features/leads/service.test.ts`'s existing `convertLead` test "maps a serialization conflict that survives every retry to `LEAD_CONFLICT`, never a raw Prisma error," and `lib/serializable-transaction.test.ts`'s own "gives up and rethrows the raw error after exhausting retries on repeated conflicts" test): the underlying transaction call is stubbed to reject with a `P2034`-shaped error on every one of `runSerializableWithRetry`'s bounded attempts, deterministically, independent of any real database state, and the test asserts the function maps the exhausted conflict to the existing controlled `LEAD_CONFLICT`/`CLIENT_CONFLICT` result — never a forbidden result, and never a raw `PrismaClientKnownRequestError` reaching the route layer. This decision does not require a real-PostgreSQL bounded-exhaustion test: per the mechanism note above, the single, concurrently-committing `ADMIN_MANAGER` reassignment authorized for item 1's real-PostgreSQL sequence cannot by itself prove bounded exhaustion, since it resolves on the very next attempt's fresh recheck rather than recurring on every attempt — so, under this contract, bounded exhaustion is proven deterministically at the mocked-unit tier only, while genuine real-PostgreSQL coverage remains required for the single-conflict-then-fresh-retry-to-forbidden path (item 1). This is not a claim that every conceivable real-PostgreSQL test harness is incapable of producing a conflict on every bounded attempt — only that the single-reassignment sequence this decision authorizes cannot, and this decision neither requires nor authorizes a more elaborate real-database orchestration to attempt it.
  3. Confirmation that the mutation's business write never appears in final persisted state when the mutation is rejected under either outcome in items 1–2 above, and that no audit row is written for the rejected attempt (Section 3, "Required error behavior," third bullet).
  4. Confirmation that every currently-passing test for the existing anti-enumeration behavior (`*_NOT_FOUND` for `ADMIN_MANAGER`, `*_FORBIDDEN` for `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT`, on a genuinely missing versus inaccessible target) and the existing `{error: {code, message}}` route envelope convention continues to pass, unmodified in assertion, after this remedy is implemented.

  **4. F-02 — Low defense-in-depth implementation defect: no service-layer role assertion in the shared assignment-mutation engine.** Affects `ASGN-03` — `setAssignment`/`endAssignment` and their five public wrappers in `apps/web/src/features/assignments/service.ts`. Every other Lead/Client/Proposal/Booking service function in this codebase independently re-asserts the actor's role in its own service layer, in addition to its route's `withRole` guard, as defense in depth. `setAssignment`/`endAssignment` do not: the existing `assertAssignmentActor` function is called only by the unrelated `listEligibleTravelConsultants` read, and its hardcoded message ("This role is not permitted to view eligible Travel Consultants.") would be factually incorrect if reused verbatim to reject a mutation attempt. No live authorization bypass exists today, because every current caller of this engine is independently gated by an already-tested `withRole(['ADMIN_MANAGER'])` route guard — this is a defense-in-depth gap, not a demonstrated boundary violation.

  Stage 2 implementation must add a dedicated, mutation-specific service-layer role assertion — either a new, separate assertion function, or a generalized, context-aware version of the existing `assertAssignmentActor` that leaves its current read-path message and tests completely unmodified — requiring exactly `ADMIN_MANAGER`, for exactly these five existing public wrappers:
  - `setLeadAssignment`
  - `setClientAssignment`
  - `setBookingAssignment`
  - `endLeadAssignment`
  - `endClientAssignment`

  Every role other than `ADMIN_MANAGER` — `SYSTEM_ADMINISTRATOR`, `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT`, `CLIENT`, `FINANCE_ACCOUNTING`, `VISA_DOCUMENTATION` — must be rejected by this new assertion before any assignment repository call. Required tests: a mocked unit test per wrapper proving rejection of each of the five non-`ADMIN_MANAGER` roles before any repository access. `endBookingAssignment` does not currently exist as a capability anywhere in this codebase and is **not** authorized, created, or tested by this decision; it remains deliberately out of scope, exactly as already recorded by D-030 Section 1.

  **5. F-03 and F-04 — required missing route-test evidence, Lead and Client assignment routes.** `F-03` (Lead): `apps/web/src/app/api/leads/[id]/assignment/route.test.ts` currently exercises rejection of only `SYSTEM_ADMINISTRATOR` and `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT` on `PUT`, and only `SYSTEM_ADMINISTRATOR` on `DELETE` — `CLIENT`, `FINANCE_ACCOUNTING`, and `VISA_DOCUMENTATION` rejection is not explicitly exercised in this file. `F-04` (Client): `apps/web/src/app/api/clients/[id]/assignment/route.test.ts` currently exercises rejection of only `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT` on `PUT`, with no explicit `DELETE` role-rejection test found in the file. Both findings are in scope for this decision and required, not optional.

  Stage 2 implementation must add exhaustive, parameterized (`it.each`-style) rejected-role coverage for both `PUT` and `DELETE` in both route-test files, mirroring the already-exhaustive pattern already present in `apps/web/src/app/api/bookings/[id]/assignment/route.test.ts`. Each operation (`PUT` and `DELETE`, in each of the two files) must assert rejection of all five non-`ADMIN_MANAGER` roles: `SYSTEM_ADMINISTRATOR`, `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT`, `CLIENT`, `FINANCE_ACCOUNTING`, `VISA_DOCUMENTATION`. These tests verify and preserve the already-established, unmodified `ADMIN_MANAGER`-only authorization contract for both routes; they authorize no role beyond `ADMIN_MANAGER` and alter no existing request, response, or error-code behavior.

  **6. Deferred and informational findings — not converted into Stage 2 implementation work.**
  - **F-05** — Booking real-PostgreSQL role/assignment-boundary automated coverage remains confirmed still limited to the two audit-specific assertions D-030 already authorized (`BOOKING_CREATED`, `BOOKING_STATUS_CHANGED`); broader boundary behavior (the `BOOKING_FORBIDDEN`/`BOOKING_NOT_FOUND` split, Client-assignment list/detail scoping, idempotent-replay behavior) under real Postgres remains unproven beyond the mocked-service and route tiers, which are themselves precise and exhaustive at those tiers. This finding is recorded here, in this decision's text, as confirmed and still open — that recording is documentation only. It is not authorized as Stage 2 implementation work under this decision, and remains exactly what D-028 Section 10 and D-030 Section 5 already recorded it as: the separate, later Phase 2 "Automated test coverage for all of the above" checklist item's responsibility.
  - **F-06** — `BOOKING_ASSIGNMENT_ENDED` is currently unreachable because no `endBookingAssignment` capability exists anywhere in this codebase. This is informational only, consistent with D-030 Section 1's identical prior finding. This decision must not, and does not, create that capability.
  - **F-07** — two real-database concurrency-proof tests (`assignments/service.integration.test.ts`'s Booking-assignment-fixture-chain case; `proposals/service.integration.test.ts`'s dual-in-flight-revision `pg_blocking_pids()` proof) failed with `TransactionWriteConflict` when all six `*.service.integration.test.ts` files were run together, and passed when those same two files were re-run together in isolation from the other four. The cause of the two failures under concurrent execution was not established in the governing discovery pass and is not established by this decision. This finding is recorded as informational only and is explicitly **not** characterized as a proven product defect, a proven infrastructure defect, a proven test defect, or a confirmed-harmless issue — its cause remains undetermined. It is not a Stage 2 action item under this decision.

  **7. Explicit policy exclusions.** This decision does not, and no Stage 2 implementation under it may:
  - Change D-026 Section 3's intentional, already-accepted behavior permitting Lead assignment set/reassign/end operations for a Lead whose status is `CONVERTED_TO_CLIENT`.
  - Reopen, reinterpret, or otherwise revisit any other accepted Blueprint Section 16 open decision, or any accepted D-001 through D-030 decision, beyond the narrow F-01/F-02/F-03/F-04 remedies this entry itself authorizes.
  - Change any existing role allowlist on any route or service function, except for the F-02 addition of service-layer defense-in-depth enforcement of the already-established, unchanged `ADMIN_MANAGER`-only assignment-mutation boundary.
  - Change `apps/web/prisma/schema.prisma`, or author, apply, or require any migration.
  - Add or remove any API route.
  - Implement any UI/component change of any kind.
  - Add `endBookingAssignment` or any other new capability not explicitly named above.
  - Perform any refactor unrelated to the F-01/F-02/F-03/F-04 remedies this entry authorizes.
  - Backfill the separately tracked, later Phase 2 "Automated test coverage for all of the above" checklist item.
  - Perform, or claim completion of, the separately tracked, later Phase 2 "End-to-end verification of the full lead → client → proposal → booking flow in a real PostgreSQL-backed development/test environment" checklist item.

  **8. Stage 2 authorized file boundary.** Stage 2 implementation is authorized in exactly these ten files, and no other tracked file, unless a separately reviewed contract correction to this decision explicitly expands this boundary:
  1. `apps/web/src/features/leads/service.ts`
  2. `apps/web/src/features/leads/service.test.ts`
  3. `apps/web/src/features/leads/service.integration.test.ts`
  4. `apps/web/src/features/clients/service.ts`
  5. `apps/web/src/features/clients/service.test.ts`
  6. `apps/web/src/features/clients/service.integration.test.ts`
  7. `apps/web/src/features/assignments/service.ts`
  8. `apps/web/src/features/assignments/service.test.ts`
  9. `apps/web/src/app/api/leads/[id]/assignment/route.test.ts`
  10. `apps/web/src/app/api/clients/[id]/assignment/route.test.ts`

  **9. Stage 2 validation contract.** Implementation acceptance requires, at minimum: mocked unit verification of the transaction/retry behavior of `updateLead`, `updateLeadStatus`, and `updateClient` — both the assignment-loss-followed-by-a-successful-retry path and the genuine bounded-retry-exhaustion path (Section 3's mechanism note and required tests); genuine real-PostgreSQL concurrency coverage for all three of those functions against `heritage_v3_test`, proving the assignment-loss-followed-by-a-successful-retry path specifically, via Section 3's required deterministic-pause-and-single-concurrent-commit sequence (genuine bounded-retry exhaustion is proven only by the mocked tier, per Section 3's mechanism note — it is not a real-PostgreSQL requirement); unit tests proving all five assignment wrappers named in Section 4 reject every non-`ADMIN_MANAGER` role before any repository access; exhaustive `it.each` rejected-role matrices for both `PUT` and `DELETE` in both the Lead and the Client assignment route-test files (Section 5); a passing run of the complete `apps/web` automated test suite; isolated execution of the affected real-database integration suites (`features/leads/service.integration.test.ts`, `features/clients/service.integration.test.ts`, `features/assignments/service.integration.test.ts`), each run as its own separate command; a passing ESLint run; a passing TypeScript type-check; a read-only formatting check (never a formatter's `--write` option); and a final diff review confirming the implementation-stage diff contains only the ten files authorized in Section 8.
