This decision (Stage 1) records this validation contract only — no application code, test, or database work has been performed under this decision, and no item in this validation contract is claimed to have passed as of this decision's acceptance.

- **Rationale:** Reusing `runSerializableWithRetry`, `findActiveAssignmentForLead`/`findActiveAssignmentForClient`, and the already-defined `LEAD_FORBIDDEN`/`CLIENT_FORBIDDEN`/`LEAD_CONFLICT`/`CLIENT_CONFLICT` error codes — rather than inventing a new concurrency primitive or new error codes — keeps this checkpoint consistent with every already-reviewed transaction-local-recheck precedent in this codebase (D-024 Section 6 for Lead conversion, D-025 Section 7 for Client update, D-027 Sections 3/5/7 for Proposal mutations) and with `.claude/rules/architecture.md`'s premature-abstraction rule. Requiring genuine bounded-retry exhaustion to be proven by a deterministic mocked test under this contract reflects `runSerializableWithRetry`'s own actual mechanism (Section 3's mechanism note): the single, concurrently-committing reassignment this decision authorizes for the real-PostgreSQL sequence resolves on the very next attempt's fresh transaction-local recheck rather than recurring on every bounded attempt, so that authorized single-reassignment sequence cannot by itself deterministically prove exhaustion — this decision does not require, and does not authorize, a more elaborate real-database orchestration to attempt it, and makes no broader claim that every conceivable PostgreSQL test harness is incapable of producing a conflict on every attempt. Genuine real-PostgreSQL coverage remains required for the single-conflict-then-fresh-retry-to-forbidden path the authorized sequence does prove. This mirrors the identical, already-established distinction this codebase already draws between `convertLead`'s own mocked exhaustion test and its separate, genuine real-database race tests, rather than inventing a real-database exhaustion requirement beyond what this contract's authorized sequence can establish. Requiring a dedicated or context-aware assertion for F-02, rather than reusing `assertAssignmentActor` unchanged, avoids surfacing an incorrect error message to a rejected caller while leaving the already-tested eligible-consultant read path untouched. Scoping F-03/F-04 to exhaustive rejected-role parameterization, mirroring the Booking assignment route test's own already-established pattern, closes a real coverage gap without altering any authorization behavior. Explicitly declining to convert F-05 into implementation work, and explicitly declining to characterize F-07's undetermined cause, preserves this repository's `validation-deployment.md` discipline of never claiming a check passed, or a cause confirmed, that was not actually established — and avoids silently absorbing the separate, later "Automated test coverage for all of the above" and "End-to-end verification of the full lead → client → proposal → booking flow" Task Board items into this one, exactly mirroring D-030's own identical discipline for the same two later items.
- **Constraint:** No `schema.prisma` change and no migration are authorized or required by this decision. No existing action name, error code, entity-type constant, route, or UI component is renamed or altered by this decision. None of the four domain error codes this remedy relies on — `LEAD_FORBIDDEN`, `LEAD_CONFLICT`, `CLIENT_FORBIDDEN`, `CLIENT_CONFLICT` — is newly defined; each already exists and is already reachable somewhere in this codebase today. What Section 3 authorizes is new *reachability* of an existing code from a new or extended transactional path, function by function: `updateLead` already returns the existing `LEAD_FORBIDDEN` code from its existing pre-transaction `canAccessLead` check (`features/leads/service.ts`); Stage 2 adds a transaction-local recheck that lets that same existing code also arise from a fresh retry attempt that observes lost assignment access, and separately adds a catch-and-map path — currently absent from `updateLead`, which performs no `P2034` handling of any kind today — so an exhausted retry maps to the existing `LEAD_CONFLICT` code rather than leaking a raw Prisma error. `updateLeadStatus` already returns the existing `LEAD_FORBIDDEN` code from its pre-transaction `canAccessLead` check and already contains a catch branch that maps an exhausted `P2034` to the existing `LEAD_CONFLICT` code. Separately, its existing `expectedStatus` mismatch path already throws a `LeadError` carrying `LEAD_CONFLICT`, which the catch block rethrows unchanged. Stage 2 adds only the missing transaction-local assignment recheck; a lost-access result flows through the existing `LeadError` path, while genuine bounded-retry exhaustion flows through the existing `isKnownConflict` mapping. `updateClient` already has a working transaction-local assignment recheck that already returns the existing `CLIENT_FORBIDDEN` code (D-025 §7, already real-database proven for the race window between the pre-transaction check and the recheck, `features/clients/service.ts`); Stage 2 retains that existing recheck exactly and moves it, with the rest of the transaction, from a plain `prisma.$transaction` to `runSerializableWithRetry`/`Serializable` isolation — extending that same existing `CLIENT_FORBIDDEN` outcome's protection to the additional race window between the recheck and the write's own commit, without introducing a new outcome — and separately adds a catch-and-map path — currently absent from `updateClient`, which performs no `P2034` handling of any kind today (its existing `CLIENT_CONFLICT` path is an unrelated, application-level `expectedUpdatedAt`-staleness check, not a caught Prisma conflict) — so an exhausted retry maps to the existing `CLIENT_CONFLICT` code rather than leaking a raw Prisma error. No existing role allowlist is expanded on any route or service function. `docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md` is not modified by this entry. The ten files authorized in Section 8 are the only files this decision authorizes changing under Stage 2; no other application, test, schema, migration, route, or UI file may be modified under this decision.
- **Deferred:** The full Booking business/concurrency real-PostgreSQL integration-test backfill beyond the two already-authorized D-030 audit assertions (F-05, remains the later "Automated test coverage for all of the above" item's responsibility); `endBookingAssignment` and the `BOOKING_ASSIGNMENT_ENDED` action it would make reachable (F-06); any determination of F-07's undetermined cause; end-to-end verification of the full lead → client → proposal → booking flow in a real PostgreSQL-backed environment (its own, separate Task Board item); and any reopening of D-026 Section 3 or any other accepted Blueprint Section 16 or Decisions Log item not named in Section 7.
- **Effect:** Authorizes Stage 2 implementation of exactly the F-01, F-02, F-03, and F-04 remedies described above, in exactly the ten files listed in Section 8, toward closing the Phase 2 "Role and assignment boundary verification across every new Lead/Client/Proposal/Booking surface" checklist item. Does not itself implement any code or test file, and does not mark that — or any other — Task Board item complete; that update remains a later, separate closure checkpoint, made only once the Stage 2 implementation described above, and its required validation (Section 9), actually exist and pass.

## D-032 — Phase 2 Automated Test Coverage Verification Contract

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Date accepted:** August 14, 2026
- **Decision:** The "Automated test coverage for all of the above" checkpoint (`docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md` Phase 2's own-named item; `.claude/rules/validation-deployment.md`'s validation-evidence discipline; D-022 through D-031) is approved as follows, on the basis of an exhaustive, evidence-based discovery pass and its three subsequent correction passes (recorded in this session as "D-032 Stage 0 — Phase 2 Automated Test Coverage Verification Discovery and Contract Recommendation," Correction Passes 1 through 3, the sole governing discovery result for this entry). This entry is self-contained — it is the authoritative contract for implementation, not a pointer to a separate report.

  **1. Purpose and boundary.** This decision governs exactly the implementation work needed to close the Task Board's "Automated test coverage for all of the above" item. It does not complete, partially complete, or in any way alter the separately tracked "End-to-end verification of the full lead → client → proposal → booking flow in a real PostgreSQL-backed development/test environment" item — no fixture chain, cross-feature dependency, or partial workflow exercised by any test this decision authorizes is ever offered, here or in any later stage, as evidence toward that separate item. This decision introduces no new production behavior, no new or changed role policy, no database schema change, no migration, no CI service, no numeric coverage threshold, and no end-to-end test framework. It is a narrow, additive, test-only checkpoint.

  **2. Accepted evidence inventory.** The governing discovery pass enumerated exactly 45 canonical entries — the 38-surface inventory D-031 already accepted, reused unchanged by identifier, plus 7 new systemic findings this checkpoint's own broader scope surfaced — independently classified each into one of seven categories, and reconciled the total exactly: **Category A (sufficient automated evidence already exists) = 28; Category B (required behavioral evidence is incomplete) = 3; Category C (required real-PostgreSQL evidence is incomplete) = 4; Category D (coverage tooling or CI-enforcement gap) = 2; Category E (test-reliability or isolation concern) = 1; Category F (optional improvement, not required to close this checkpoint) = 6; Category G (separately tracked end-to-end work, out of scope) = 1. 28 + 3 + 4 + 2 + 1 + 6 + 1 = 45.** This classification is accepted as-is; no surface is reclassified, omitted, duplicated, or invented by this decision.
  - **Category A (28) — sufficient automated evidence already exists at whichever tier (mocked, route, component, or real-PostgreSQL) can actually prove the behavior in question:** `ASGN-01` (`canAccessLead`/`canAccessClient` primitives); `ASGN-02` (`GET /api/assignments/travel-consultants`); `ASGN-03` (assignment-mutation engine orchestration logic, D-031 F-02); `LEAD-01` (Lead creation); `LEAD-02` (Lead list); `LEAD-03` (Lead retrieval); `LEAD-04` (`updateLead`, D-031 F-01); `LEAD-05` (`updateLeadStatus`, D-031 F-01); `LEAD-06` (Lead status-history read); `LEAD-08` (Lead assignment route + `LeadAssignmentPanel`, D-031 F-03); `LEAD-09` (manual Lead-creation UI); `CLIENT-01` (Client list); `CLIENT-02` (Client retrieval); `CLIENT-03` (`updateClient`, D-031 F-01); `CLIENT-04` (Client assignment route + `ClientAssignmentPanel`, D-031 F-04); `CLIENT-05` (`CreateProposalPanel` entry point); `PROPOSAL-01` through `PROPOSAL-07` (Proposal list, detail, create, revision, publish, external-response recording, and the Booking-creation entry point); `BOOKING-05` (`PUT /api/bookings/[id]/assignment`); `CROSS-02` (`admin/layout.tsx`); `CROSS-03` (`admin/page.tsx`); `CROSS-04` (`dashboard/page.tsx`); `CROSS-06` (`admin/_components/Pagination.tsx`).
  - **Category B (3) — a specific expected behavior has no automated test proving it, at any tier:** `LEAD-07` (`POST /api/leads/[id]/conversion` + conversion-options read — two of D-024 §11's four required anti-enumeration/link-availability distinctions are unproven, Section 5 below); `CROSS-01` (`apps/web/src/proxy.ts` — no test file exists); `CROSS-05` (`apps/web/src/app/login/page.tsx` — no test file exists).
  - **Category C (4) — the required Booking authorization, scoping, anti-enumeration, idempotency, and concurrency behaviors lack real-PostgreSQL evidence:** `BOOKING-01` (`listBookings`); `BOOKING-02` (`getBookingById`); `BOOKING-03` (`createBooking`); `BOOKING-04` (`updateBookingStatus`) — mocked service/route coverage for all four already exists and continues to run normally under `pnpm test`; existing real-PostgreSQL Booking coverage (`features/bookings/service.integration.test.ts`) proves only the two D-030 §2 audit-atomicity assertions (`BOOKING_CREATED`, `BOOKING_STATUS_CHANGED`), using only an `ADMIN_MANAGER` actor for every `createBooking`/`updateBookingStatus` call, and never calling `listBookings` or `getBookingById` at all.
  - **Category D (2) — a structural condition in how coverage is measured or enforced, independent of any single surface's own test content:** `COV-01` (`features/assignments/repository.ts` exports exactly 12 async functions — `findLeadById`, `findClientById`, `findBookingById`, `findAssigneeCandidateById`, `findActiveAssignmentForLead`, `findActiveAssignmentForClient`, `findActiveAssignmentForBooking`, `findClientProfileOwnership`, `createAssignment`, `endAssignmentById`, `insertAuditLog`, `listEligibleTravelConsultants` — of which `features/assignments/repository.test.ts` directly tests only **2** (`findClientProfileOwnership`, `listEligibleTravelConsultants`); the remaining **10** — `findLeadById`, `findClientById`, `findBookingById`, `findAssigneeCandidateById`, `findActiveAssignmentForLead`, `findActiveAssignmentForClient`, `findActiveAssignmentForBooking`, `createAssignment`, `endAssignmentById`, `insertAuditLog` — have 0% executed-code coverage under `pnpm test`, confirmed by a fresh coverage run performed during discovery; `assignments/service.test.ts` mocks the entire `./repository` module, so the real bodies of these ten functions are never executed by any mocked-tier test, and their only verification is the real-PostgreSQL tier, which `COV-03` establishes CI never runs); `COV-03` (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s `Test` step never sets `TEST_DATABASE_URL` and never provisions a PostgreSQL service, so all six `*.service.integration.test.ts` files are unconditionally skipped on every PR and every push to `main` — a real, confirmed finding, but explicitly non-blocking for this checkpoint's closure, Section 10 below).
  - **Category E (1) — an existing, otherwise-adequate test is not reliably reproducible under certain execution conditions:** `COV-04` (a genuine `DriverAdapterError: TransactionWriteConflict` was freshly reproduced during discovery. Exact run accounting: three default-parallelism invocations ran all six real-PostgreSQL integration files together — one failed, two passed; one separate default-parallelism invocation ran only the Assignments and Proposals integration files together and failed in Proposals; five consecutive invocations of the complete six-file set using Vitest's documented `--no-file-parallelism` option all passed. This supports `--no-file-parallelism` as a deterministic mitigation but does not establish the flakiness's root cause, which remains unproven and is not characterized as a product, infrastructure, or test defect by this decision).
  - **Category F (6) — additional coverage that would be beneficial but is not necessary to honestly close this checkpoint:** `CROSS-07` (`GET /api/me`); `CROSS-08` (`GET /api/health`); `CROSS-09` (`/api/auth/[...all]`) — all three are thin, low-complexity pass-throughs with no test file and negligible risk; `COV-02` (`@vitest/coverage-v8` is an installed devDependency with no wired script or config — the ad hoc invocation used throughout discovery already produced a report covering every file that specific run's own dependency graph imported, with zero configuration changes, so a permanent script adds only discoverability and scope hygiene, not new closure evidence); `DOC-01` (`CLAUDE.md` §1 still describes Lead, Client, Proposal/ROS, and real admin-dashboard workflow implementation as incomplete Phase 2 work, which is stale relative to D-022 through D-030); `DOC-02` (`.claude/rules/validation-deployment.md`'s "Current State" section still states no package manager, test runner, linter, or build system exists, which is stale relative to the `pnpm`/`vitest`/`eslint`/`tsc` tooling in continuous use throughout this codebase) — both `DOC-01` and `DOC-02` are informational drift only, immaterial to this checkpoint's own conclusions (every finding in this decision rests on direct source and command verification, never on the stale narrative text), and neither is edited by this decision.
  - **Category G (1) — belongs to the separately tracked end-to-end item, not this one:** `E2E-01` (a symbolic entry representing the Task Board's own separate "End-to-end verification of the full lead → client → proposal → booking flow in a real PostgreSQL-backed development/test environment" item — included in this inventory only so the total reconciles and the exclusion in Section 1 is arithmetically explicit; no fixture chain reused by any test this decision authorizes is offered as evidence toward it).

  **3. Exact implementation boundary.** Stage 2 implementation is authorized in exactly these six files, and no other tracked file, unless a separately reviewed contract correction to this decision explicitly expands this boundary:
  1. `apps/web/src/features/bookings/service.integration.test.ts`
  2. `apps/web/src/features/leads/service.integration.test.ts`
  3. `apps/web/src/features/assignments/repository.test.ts`
  4. `apps/web/src/app/login/page.test.tsx`
  5. `apps/web/src/proxy.test.ts`
  6. `apps/web/package.json`

  Exactly two of these six files are real-PostgreSQL integration-test files — `bookings/service.integration.test.ts` and `leads/service.integration.test.ts` — each extended, not newly created. The remaining four are mocked-unit (`assignments/repository.test.ts`), jsdom-component (`login/page.test.tsx`), unit-tier (`proxy.test.ts`, new), and package configuration (`package.json`, one new script). Stage 2 implementation must not change any production code, `schema.prisma` file, migration, `docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md` or any other documentation file, `.github/workflows/*.yml` CI workflow, `apps/web/vitest.config.ts`, or any coverage-tooling configuration.

  **4. Booking integration requirements — F-Booking, Category C remedy.** Affects `BOOKING-01` through `BOOKING-04`. Stage 2 implementation must extend `bookings/service.integration.test.ts` with all eight of the following assertion groups, reusing the existing `tcActor` fixture (already present in this file for Proposal-authoring/fixture setup, per Section 2's `BOOKING-03`/`BOOKING-04` finding) plus one new `otherTcActor` fixture (a second, deliberately-unassigned `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT`, mirroring the identical pattern already established in `leads/service.integration.test.ts`):
  1. **`createBooking` anti-enumeration.** `otherTcActor` calling `createBooking` against a real, existing-but-inaccessible `ProposalVersion` and against a genuinely nonexistent `ProposalVersion` id both reject with the identical `PROPOSAL_VERSION_FORBIDDEN` result.
  2. **`getBookingById`/`updateBookingStatus` anti-enumeration.** `otherTcActor` against a real, existing-but-inaccessible Booking and against a genuinely nonexistent Booking id both reject with the identical `BOOKING_FORBIDDEN` result, for both functions.
  3. **`listBookings` scoping.** `tcActor`, while actively assigned to a Client, sees exactly that Client's Bookings in both `.items` and `.total`; a Booking belonging to a Client `tcActor` is not assigned to is absent from both; `adminActor` sees every Booking (the required comparison case); after ending `tcActor`'s Client assignment, a repeat `listBookings` call no longer includes that Booking in either `.items` or `.total`.
  4. **Positive consultant authorization.** `tcActor`, while actively assigned, successfully creates a Booking, appears in their own `listBookings` result, successfully retrieves it via `getBookingById`, and successfully performs the allowed `DRAFT → PENDING_CONFIRMATION` transition via `updateBookingStatus` — all four against real Postgres.
  5. **Rejected-operation atomicity.** For every anti-enumeration rejection in items 1–2, assert zero new `Booking`, `BookingStatusHistory`, and `AuditLog` rows via before/after count deltas scoped to the specific fixture/actor/id under test — never an unscoped global count.
  6. **`createBooking` idempotency.** A repeated call for the same `proposalVersionId` returns `created:false` and the original Booking's id, and exactly one `Booking` row, exactly one initial `BookingStatusHistory` row, and exactly one `BOOKING_CREATED` audit row exist for it.
  7. **`updateBookingStatus` idempotency.** A same-status replay — including one submitted with a stale `expectedStatus` — returns the Booking unchanged and writes no additional `BookingStatusHistory` or audit row.
  8. **Genuine divergent-target concurrency.** Create one real `DRAFT` Booking. Open an independent, deliberately held-open real Prisma "blocker" transaction and capture its own PostgreSQL backend PID (`SELECT pg_backend_pid()`); inside it, execute `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` against the specific Booking row, taking a genuine row-level lock, then signal that the blocker is ready. Only **after** the blocker is confirmed to hold the row lock, install a test-only interception of `prisma.$transaction` — first capturing and preserving the exact, original, unmodified method reference — that, for exactly the next two invocations only, wraps each real callback solely to capture that transaction's own `pg_backend_pid()` without altering its real behavior in any other way (mirroring `proposals/service.integration.test.ts`'s already-established `createPidCapturingTransactionWrapper` pattern). Launch two real, unmodified `updateBookingStatus` calls concurrently — both with `expectedStatus: 'DRAFT'`, one targeting `PENDING_CONFIRMATION` and the other targeting `CANCELLED`. Using the existing established `pg_blocking_pids()`/`pg_stat_activity` polling helper, prove that both captured service-operation backend PIDs are genuinely blocked specifically on the external blocker's own PID before releasing it. Use an explicit, extended-but-bounded Prisma transaction timeout and Vitest test timeout for this test, following the existing Proposal publish-race precedent (`proposals/service.integration.test.ts`'s own `timeout: 15000`-style override). Restore the exact original `prisma.$transaction` reference, release and settle the blocker transaction, and settle both service-call promises — all unconditionally, in `finally` blocks, so no open transaction, held lock, intercepted method, or unhandled rejection can survive the test regardless of outcome. Resolve both outcomes with `Promise.allSettled`. Assert: exactly one promise is `fulfilled` and exactly one is `rejected` with a `reason` that is `instanceof BookingError` with `.code === 'BOOKING_CONFLICT'` — never a raw `DriverAdapterError`, `Prisma.PrismaClientKnownRequestError`, or any other unmapped PostgreSQL error escaping; the final persisted `Booking.status` equals the fulfilled result's own requested target, read from its resolved value and never assumed; exactly one new `BookingStatusHistory` row exists with `previousStatus: 'DRAFT'`; exactly one new `BOOKING_STATUS_CHANGED` audit row exists; the rejected target's status was never persisted as a committed transition (a count-by-`newStatus` on the losing target equals zero). This test does not claim to prove, and this decision does not claim, the root cause of the `COV-04` multi-file flakiness — it is a distinct assertion about genuine two-target conflict resolution, unrelated to that open concern. **Same-target concurrent idempotency remains explicitly separate from this divergent-target conflict case**: two calls requesting the identical resulting status may legitimately resolve as one real write plus one successful idempotent no-op (item 7 above), and this is not an outcome item 8's assertions require or test for.

  **5. Lead integration requirements — F-Lead, Category B remedy.** Affects `LEAD-07`. Stage 2 implementation must extend `leads/service.integration.test.ts` with exactly these two real-PostgreSQL tests, and must not add any test to `leads/service.test.ts` for this purpose:
  - A `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT` actor calling `convertLead` with a syntactically valid `randomUUID()` that was never inserted as a `Lead` row receives `LEAD_FORBIDDEN` — the identical code the existing real, inaccessible-but-existing-Lead test already proves (`service.integration.test.ts`'s "rejects an unassigned TRAVEL_CONSULTANT with LEAD_FORBIDDEN..." test) — preserving the same existence-hiding behavior for a genuinely nonexistent Lead as for an inaccessible one.
  - An `ADMIN_MANAGER` actor converting a genuine, real, `QUALIFIED` Lead fixture while supplying a syntactically valid `randomUUID()` that was never inserted as a `Client` row receives `CLIENT_LINK_NOT_AVAILABLE`, and produces no conversion-related write or audit entry: the specific Lead fixture's `status` remains `QUALIFIED` and `clientId` remains `null`; no `AuditLog` row with `action: 'LEAD_CONVERTED_TO_CLIENT'` and `entityId` equal to that Lead's id exists; no `AuditLog` row with `action: 'CLIENT_CREATED'` and an `afterState.sourceLeadId` equal to that Lead's id exists; that Lead's `LeadStatusHistory` row count is unchanged from before the attempt.

  These two distinctions belong only in `leads/service.integration.test.ts`, and no mocked `leads/service.test.ts` substitute is authorized, because `convertLead` calls the global `canAccessLead` check before its transaction ever opens, and `canAccessLead`'s own mechanism for a `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT` actor (an active-`StaffAssignment` lookup) returns an identical denial whether the target Lead is genuinely nonexistent or merely exists-but-unassigned — there is no distinct code branch a mock could exercise to prove this specific distinction; only a real database, where the two underlying facts genuinely differ, can prove it. The existing mocked `canAccessLead`-denial test already proves `convertLead`'s own service-layer mapping from a denied authorization check to `LEAD_FORBIDDEN`; that mapping is not retested by this remedy.

  **6. Assignments repository requirements — F-Repo, Category D remedy.** Affects `COV-01`. Stage 2 implementation must extend `assignments/repository.test.ts` with one direct unit test per each of the ten currently-untested exported functions, mirroring the existing hand-built-mocked-Prisma-client pattern already established in `leads/repository.test.ts`/`clients/repository.test.ts`:
  - `findActiveAssignmentForLead`, `findActiveAssignmentForClient`, `findActiveAssignmentForBooking`: each query composes its `endedAt: null` plus target-id filter directly into the `where` clause (never fetch-then-filter); each returns `null` unchanged for no match.
  - `createAssignment`: writes exactly the supplied fields, no more; returns the row via the existing `ASSIGNMENT_SELECT` shape.
  - `endAssignmentById`: sets `endedAt` to a real `Date`; never deletes the row.
  - `findLeadById`, `findClientById`, `findBookingById`, `findAssigneeCandidateById`: each selects only its documented narrow field set; each returns `null` unchanged for no match.
  - `insertAuditLog`: asserts `db.auditLog.create` is called with `data` containing a generated `id` (a string of the expected UUID shape — never asserted against a fixed, hardcoded UUID value, since `randomUUID()` produces a fresh value per call) plus exactly the supplied `actorId`, `action`, `entityType`, and `entityId`; a second assertion confirms that when the caller supplies `beforeState`/`afterState`, those exact values are preserved unchanged in the `data` object; a third assertion confirms that when the caller omits `beforeState`/`afterState`, both keys remain explicitly present in the `data` object with value `undefined` — matching the real implementation's own unconditional object-literal construction exactly, never replaced with `null`, `{}`, or any other fabricated value; a fourth assertion confirms the function's own return value resolves to `undefined` (matching its `Promise<void>` signature), fabricating no domain object of its own.

  **7. Login and proxy requirements — F-Cross, Category B remedy.** Affects `CROSS-01` and `CROSS-05`. Stage 2 implementation must add exactly:
  - `apps/web/src/app/login/page.test.tsx` (new, jsdom): a successful `signIn.email` call navigates via `router.push('/dashboard')` and calls `router.refresh()`; a controlled sign-in failure renders an accessible error and performs no navigation; the submit control is disabled while a request is in flight.
  - `apps/web/src/proxy.test.ts` (new): a request with no session cookie redirects to `/login`; a request with a session cookie present returns `NextResponse.next()`; neither case involves a database query.

  **8. Deterministic integration command — F-Flake, Category E mitigation.** Affects `COV-04`. Stage 2 implementation must add exactly this script to `apps/web/package.json`:

  `"test:integration": "vitest run --no-file-parallelism src/features/assignments/service.integration.test.ts src/features/bookings/service.integration.test.ts src/features/clients/service.integration.test.ts src/features/leads/service.integration.test.ts src/features/proposals/service.integration.test.ts src/features/staff/service.integration.test.ts"`

  `--no-file-parallelism` is Vitest's own documented option for suites that share an external resource such as a database, and is recorded here as an evidence-backed deterministic mitigation for the reproduced shared-database `TransactionWriteConflict` flakiness (Section 2's `COV-04` finding. Exact run accounting: three default-parallelism invocations ran all six real-PostgreSQL integration files together — one failed, two passed; one separate default-parallelism invocation ran only the Assignments and Proposals integration files together and failed in Proposals; five consecutive invocations of the complete six-file set using Vitest's documented `--no-file-parallelism` option all passed, during discovery. This supports `--no-file-parallelism` as a deterministic mitigation but does not establish the flakiness's root cause). It is explicitly **not** claimed, here or by any later stage, to be proof of that conflict's root cause, which remains undetermined.

  **9. Validation contract.** The later implementation stage cannot be accepted unless: the full `pnpm test` suite passes; `pnpm --filter web run test:integration` (Section 8's exact script) is run with a real, externally supplied `TEST_DATABASE_URL` that each of the six integration files independently re-validates against its own existing `validateTestDatabaseUrl` safety guard; all six integration files genuinely execute — an all-skipped run, produced by omitting `TEST_DATABASE_URL`, is a failure of this validation requirement, not a passing result; zero integration test files and zero integration tests are skipped in that run's own summary output; every integration test in that run passes; ESLint passes; TypeScript type-checking passes; `pnpm format:check` is run and its result reported accurately, without inventing or assuming a result; and the final implementation-stage diff contains exactly the six files authorized in Section 3 — no other file. Database credentials must never be embedded in the `test:integration` script itself or printed by any command run to satisfy this contract.

  **10. Explicit non-blocking deferrals.** The following are recorded as confirmed, real findings that this decision does not require Stage 2 to resolve, and does not itself resolve: numeric coverage thresholds and any permanent coverage-tooling configuration (`COV-02`); provisioning a real PostgreSQL service in GitHub Actions so CI itself executes the integration tier (`COV-03`) — CI absence of the integration tier remains a known, accepted gap, consistent with the unbroken local-real-PostgreSQL-evidence precedent every one of D-022 through D-031 already established as sufficient, and is not required by this decision; the `CLAUDE.md`/`.claude/rules/validation-deployment.md` documentation drift recorded as `DOC-01`/`DOC-02`, which remains open for its own future, separately approved documentation-correction pass; direct test coverage for `GET /api/me`, `GET /api/health`, and `/api/auth/[...all]` (`CROSS-07`/`08`/`09`); any broader Booking real-PostgreSQL parity beyond the eight assertion groups Section 4 authorizes; and any work toward the separately tracked "End-to-end verification of the full lead → client → proposal → booking flow in a real PostgreSQL-backed development/test environment" item (`E2E-01`), which this decision does not touch, complete, or partially complete in any way.

  **11. Closure rules.** This decision does not itself modify `docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md` — the "Automated test coverage for all of the above" checklist item remains unchecked until the Stage 2 implementation described above, and its required validation (Section 9), actually exist and pass. This decision does not claim that any Stage 2 implementation or closure-validation work has occurred as of its acceptance. Stage 0's governing discovery pass executed this codebase's existing mocked and real-PostgreSQL test suites solely to gather the evidence recorded in Section 2 above; no Stage 2 test implementation, package-script change, or closure validation has occurred under this decision, and no production, test, configuration, schema, migration, CI, Task Board, or other implementation-boundary file has yet been changed under Stage 2. D-032 acceptance authorizes Stage 2 implementation — it does not claim that implementation is complete. D-032 acceptance authorizes exactly the six-file implementation contract described in Sections 3 through 9; Task Board closure of this checklist item requires a later, separate implementation and verification stage, mirroring D-022 through D-031's identical, already-established precedent of never marking a Task Board item complete from within the decision that authorizes the underlying work.

- **Rationale:** Reusing the exact 45-entry classification the governing discovery pass and its three correction passes produced — rather than re-deriving or narrowing it at acceptance time — preserves the evidentiary chain those passes established, including the corrections that moved `LEAD-07` out of Category A, corrected `BOOKING-01` through `BOOKING-04`'s CI-matrix wording, corrected the exact 12-function/10-untested `assignments/repository.ts` count, and replaced an invalid JavaScript-side-pause concurrency design with the directly reused, already-proven `proposals/service.integration.test.ts` `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`/`pg_blocking_pids()` pattern for Booking. Scoping Stage 2 to exactly six files — extending the two files (`bookings/`, `leads/service.integration.test.ts`) whose real-database evidence is genuinely incomplete, extending `assignments/repository.test.ts` to close a concrete, currently-zero-coverage security-relevant gap, adding two small, currently-nonexistent test files for `login/page.tsx`/`proxy.ts`, and adding one deterministic test script — closes every Category B, C, and (via mitigation) E finding this checkpoint's own scope requires, without touching production code, schema, CI infrastructure, or coverage policy, each of which this decision's discovery found to be either unnecessary to honestly close this specific Task Board item or a materially larger, separate decision. Declining to require CI provisioning of the integration tier (`COV-03`) reflects the unbroken precedent already set by D-022 through D-031, none of which required it as their own acceptance bar; introducing a stricter bar for this checkpoint alone, silently redefining what "automated test coverage" has meant for this entire phase, would itself be an unapproved scope expansion. Requiring `--no-file-parallelism` as the deterministic integration-runner command, rather than attempting to diagnose or fix `COV-04`'s root cause, reflects this repository's `validation-deployment.md` discipline of never claiming a check passed, or a cause confirmed, that was not actually established — mirroring D-031 F-07's identical, already-established discipline for the same underlying flakiness. Requiring the `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`/captured-PID/`pg_blocking_pids()` mechanism for the Booking concurrency test, rather than a simpler but unproven pause-based design, ensures the genuine two-target race this checkpoint requires is actually, verifiably in flight before either outcome is observed — consistent with every other genuine-concurrency test already accepted in this codebase (D-027 §10, D-031 F-01's real-PostgreSQL races).
- **Constraint:** No `schema.prisma` change and no migration are authorized or required by this decision. No existing action name, error code, entity-type constant, route, or UI component is renamed or altered. No existing role allowlist, authorization rule, or business behavior is changed by this decision — every assertion Sections 4 through 7 authorize proves behavior the existing, already-accepted implementation already exhibits; none of them requires or authorizes a production-code change to pass. `docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md` is not modified by this entry. No numeric coverage threshold is introduced or implied by Section 8's or any other section's reference to coverage tooling. The six files authorized in Section 3 are the only files this decision authorizes changing under Stage 2; no production code, schema, migration, CI workflow, `apps/web/vitest.config.ts`, coverage configuration, or other documentation file may be modified under this decision.
- **Deferred:** Numeric coverage thresholds and permanent coverage-tooling configuration (`COV-02`); provisioning PostgreSQL in GitHub Actions so CI executes the integration tier (`COV-03`, accepted as a known, non-blocking gap under D-022 through D-031's existing local-evidence precedent); the `CLAUDE.md`/`validation-deployment.md` documentation-drift correction (`DOC-01`/`DOC-02`); direct test coverage for `GET /api/me`, `GET /api/health`, and `/api/auth/[...all]` (`CROSS-07`/`08`/`09`); any Booking real-PostgreSQL parity beyond Section 4's eight assertion groups; any determination of `COV-04`'s undetermined root cause; and the separately tracked "End-to-end verification of the full lead → client → proposal → booking flow in a real PostgreSQL-backed development/test environment" item (`E2E-01`), untouched in any way by this decision.
- **Effect:** Authorizes Stage 2 implementation of exactly the F-Booking, F-Lead, F-Repo, F-Cross, and F-Flake remedies described above, in exactly the six files listed in Section 3, toward closing the Phase 2 "Automated test coverage for all of the above" checklist item. Does not itself implement any code or test file, and does not mark that — or any other — Task Board item complete; that update remains a later, separate closure checkpoint, made only once the Stage 2 implementation described above, and its required validation (Section 9), actually exist and pass.

## D-033 — Phase 2 End-to-End Admin-Surface Flow Verification Contract

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Date accepted:** August 17, 2026
- **Decision:** The "End-to-end verification of the full lead → client → proposal → booking flow in a real PostgreSQL-backed development/test environment" checkpoint — Phase 2's sole remaining checklist item and the phase's own completion gate (`docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md`; blueprint Sections 5.2, 6.7, 8, 9, 9.1, 13.1–13.5, 14.2, 14.4, 14.5; D-022 through D-032) — is approved as follows, on the basis of an exhaustive, evidence-based discovery pass and its two subsequent correction passes (recorded in this session as "D-033 Stage 0 — Phase 2 End-to-End Flow Verification Discovery," Correction Passes 1 and 2, the sole governing discovery result for this entry). This entry is self-contained — it is the authoritative contract for implementation, not a pointer to a separate report.

  **1. Objective.** D-033 closes the sole remaining Phase 2 checklist item by proving that one real `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT` can execute the complete Lead → `QUALIFIED` Lead → converted Client → Proposal and first `ProposalVersion` → published/current client-visible `ProposalVersion` → externally recorded `ACCEPT` response under blueprint Section 9.1 → Booking → Booking visibility and one allowed Booking status transition, through the real rendered admin surfaces, using: a real Chromium browser controlled by Playwright; the real `/login` form; a real Better Auth credential and session cookie; real `proxy.ts`, admin layout, page, API, service, repository, and authorization execution; a real Next.js production build/server; the disposable local `heritage_v3_test` PostgreSQL database; and direct database assertions paired with the browser journey. No mocked authentication, actor, service, repository, route, persistence, or component is permitted anywhere in the canonical E2E journey.

  **2. Exact evidence boundary.** The browser journey proves: real login; real cookie/session handling; real middleware and layout authorization; real rendered admin pages; real navigation and form submission; real route/service integration; that a staff member can complete the journey through the implemented surfaces. The paired direct database assertions prove: cross-entity identity and linkage; assignment inheritance; Proposal publication/current-version state; Proposal acceptance; Booking linkage; Lead and Booking history rows; required `AuditLog` rows and actor/entity attribution. Neither evidence tier may be claimed to prove the other tier's facts without its paired evidence — this is the corrected position Correction Pass 1 established after the original Stage 0 discovery's real-PostgreSQL-service-chain-only recommendation was found insufficient to truthfully establish the literal completion-gate wording "through the implemented admin surfaces."

  **3. Canonical actor and journey.** Exactly one test-created `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT` is used throughout — no `ADMIN_MANAGER` participates in the canonical flow, since no implementation constraint requires one anywhere in this chain (D-024 §2 permits the assigned TC to convert; D-027 §3 permits the assigned TC to author/publish and to record the §9.1 response). The browser must, in order: (1) log in through `/login` with the test-created real credential; (2) create a Lead through `/admin/leads/new`; (3) confirm the Lead is atomically self-assigned to that TC; (4) transition the Lead from `NEW` to `QUALIFIED` through the Lead admin page; (5) convert it into a new Client through the conversion panel; (6) confirm navigation/access to the resulting Client; (7) create a Proposal and its first version through the Client admin page; (8) publish that version through the Proposal admin page; (9) record an external `ACCEPT` response under Section 9.1 through the Proposal admin page; (10) create a Booking from that accepted version; (11) navigate to/list/read the Booking; (12) transition the Booking from `DRAFT` to `PENDING_CONFIRMATION` through the Booking admin page. No second unauthorized-TC negative case is required: `StaffAssignment` carries no field distinguishing an inherited (conversion-created) row from a directly assigned one, so `findActiveAssignmentForClient`'s single, shared, already-tested query cannot behave differently based on a row's provenance — D-024, D-027, and D-031 already prove the constituent assignment-inheritance mechanics and downstream rejection boundaries in isolation; D-033 proves only their positive cross-feature composition, and a second-TC rejection case would prove no fact those three decisions do not already establish.

  **4. Real authentication fixture.** A Playwright test-scoped fixture creates exactly one `User` (`role: TRAVEL_CONSULTANT`) with one linked credential `Account`, using a `randomUUID()`-suffixed unique email and a generated test-only plaintext password, hashed via `better-auth/crypto`'s `hashPassword` — the same technique `apps/web/prisma/seed.ts` and `features/staff/service.ts`'s `createStaffAccount` already use (D-012). The `User` and `Account` rows are created atomically, in one dedicated E2E Prisma transaction, so no partial (`User`-without-`Account`) state can ever exist. The plaintext password is generated once in the fixture's own in-memory closure and is passed directly to the test as a fixture value; it necessarily also exists transiently — for the genuine login this contract requires — in the Playwright test process's own memory, in the browser's rendered password field, in the local HTTP authentication request Better Auth receives, and in the authentication server's own in-process memory while it verifies the credential. It is never intentionally persisted, logged, committed, placed in an environment variable, or included in any filename. No `storageState` or fabricated cookie may replace the real `/login` interaction — the canonical journey performs a genuine form submission every run.

  **5. Dedicated E2E database client and safety.** The E2E harness never imports or reuses `apps/web/src/lib/db.ts`'s production Prisma singleton or `getServerEnv()`. `apps/web/e2e/support/test-database.ts` is the single canonical implementation, used identically by `run-e2e.ts`, `playwright.config.ts`, `fixtures.ts`, and the spec's own direct-assertion code, that: reads `TEST_DATABASE_URL`; requires protocol `postgresql:` or `postgres:`; requires hostname `localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, or `::1`; rejects any `host` query parameter (case-insensitive, covering encoded and repeated forms) before returning the URL — the installed `pg-connection-string@2.14.0` parser, confirmed directly against its source, copies every query parameter onto its internal config object and derives the connection host from the URL's actual hostname only when no `host` parameter is already present, so an unrejected `host` query parameter would silently override an otherwise-valid `localhost`/`127.0.0.1`/`::1` hostname and redirect the driver to an arbitrary host (Stage 2 Correction Pass 4); requires database name exactly `heritage_v3_test`; requires a non-empty username; never logs or embeds the connection string or credentials in any error or success message; hard-fails rather than skipping when the variable is missing or invalid; returns the validated URL; and constructs an independent `PrismaClient`/`PrismaPg` adapter from that validated URL alone. The spawned Next.js server's child environment (`childEnv`, Section 6) explicitly sets: `TEST_DATABASE_URL` and `DATABASE_URL` = the validated `TEST_DATABASE_URL`; `BETTER_AUTH_URL` = `http://127.0.0.1:3100`; `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET` = a fresh, in-memory, randomly generated value of at least 32 characters, held only for the lifetime of the spawned process and never a production or developer secret; `NODE_ENV` = `production`. These explicit E2E values override any inherited development value of the same name; `TEST_DATABASE_URL` itself is not read by the Next.js application (only by this suite's own independent re-validation calls) but is present in `childEnv` alongside the four application-critical values. Playwright's own `webServer` launch additionally merges its non-secret default variables (`BROWSER`, `FORCE_COLOR`, `DEBUG_COLORS`) and redundantly restates the four application-critical values a second time via `webServer.env` (Section 7) — a defense-in-depth restatement of `childEnv`'s own values, not a second or different source of them (confirmed directly against the installed `playwright/lib/runner/index.js`, Stage 2 Correction Pass 1). This decision does not claim exactly four, or any other fixed total count of, environment variables reach the spawned server — only that no inherited development `DATABASE_URL`, Better Auth secret, or Better Auth URL can survive into it, since `spawn`'s `env` option fully replaces (never merges with) the true parent/host environment for the child (Section 6). The child-process environment otherwise preserves only the defined inherited variables genuinely needed for executable resolution/runtime (e.g. `PATH`) and then explicitly overrides exactly these five keys — no `undefined` value may be passed through as the literal string `"undefined"`. No credential may appear in a command-line argument or in any printed output; a successful safety message may state only that the local `heritage_v3_test` database was validated, never the URL, username, password, or any other credential.

  **5a. Real-Prisma RPC bridge (Stage 2 Correction Pass 1; causal explanation corrected, Stage 2 Correction Pass 2).** Directly loading the generated Prisma client (`apps/web/src/generated/prisma/client.ts`) from Playwright-run code fails reproducibly: Playwright 1.62.1's own installed loader (`playwright/lib/common/index.js`'s `requireOrImport`/`fileIsModule`, confirmed directly against the installed source) routes every `.ts` file in a package with no `"type": "module"` field — which includes every file in this Stage 2 boundary — through plain CJS `require()`, and that exact code path fails with `Cannot require() ES Module ... in a cycle` once it reaches the generated client, whose own module graph does contain an internal circular reference (`internal/class.ts`/`internal/prismaNamespace.ts`). This is not established as a universal rule, however: an isolated `tsx/cjs`-hooked `require()` of the identical generated-client file, run outside Playwright's own process, succeeds with no error, so the circular reference has not been proven the complete, sufficient cause on its own, and the exact mechanism distinguishing Playwright's own failing pipeline from that succeeding isolated case remains unresolved. What is directly, repeatedly confirmed, and is the sole basis for this authorization, is narrower: no file inside the Stage 2 boundary can load the generated Prisma client for runtime use through Playwright's own actual process (a type-only `import type` of the same file is unaffected, since type-only imports are erased entirely and never trigger a runtime module load). This decision explicitly authorizes, as the accepted practical workaround for that confirmed failure within the fixed `.ts` file-extension boundary — not as a claim that the underlying mechanism is fully understood — a real-Prisma RPC bridge with exactly these five properties: (1) exactly one real `PrismaClient` (built from the validated `TEST_DATABASE_URL` and `@prisma/adapter-pg`), owned exclusively by `run-e2e.ts` — a genuine `tsx`-run, ESM-capable process, confirmed directly to load the generated client without incident; (2) an HTTP bridge bound only to `127.0.0.1:3101`, existing only for the lifetime of the E2E run; (3) every file inside Playwright's own process (`fixtures.ts`, the spec) accesses the database exclusively through a transport-proxy RPC client — never a direct `PrismaClient` import or instance; (4) Prisma disconnection is owned exclusively by `run-e2e.ts`; the Playwright-side RPC client's own `$disconnect` is a documented no-op that closes no connection; (5) `run-e2e.ts` executes its child processes (Section 6) asynchronously (`spawn`, never `execFileSync` or another synchronous form) so the bridge's own HTTP event loop remains available to serve concurrent RPC calls throughout the run. The Playwright-side accessor is named `createE2EPrismaRpcClient()` and returns an explicit, narrow `E2EPrismaRpcClient` interface type describing only the exact `<model>.<method>` operations this suite actually uses plus its no-op `$disconnect` — never a `PrismaClient` type assertion; the object is a transport proxy, not a database connection, and must never be represented as one. Every method's transported result is typed `Promise<unknown>`, never a real Prisma delegate return type: JSON transport does not preserve genuine Prisma return-value semantics (e.g. a `Date` becomes a string), so no return-type assertion may imply otherwise; only input-argument shapes retain real Prisma argument types. Every unknown result is runtime-validated/narrowed by the calling code before any field access — never an unchecked whole-result type assertion (Stage 2 Correction Pass 2). The bridge is unauthenticated and accepts loopback connections only; this is a deliberate, bounded acceptance for this specific threat model — a disposable local `heritage_v3_test` database, a bridge that exists only for the duration of one local E2E run, and a host with no other untrusted local process expected to be listening — not a general pattern for any future networked or shared use.

  **5b. RPC bridge hardening (Stage 2 Correction Pass 1; sanitization and lifecycle corrected, Stage 2 Correction Pass 2; request-controlled logging and response-text trust corrected, Stage 2 Correction Pass 3; non-success HTTP status message fixed, Stage 2 Correction Pass 4).** The bridge allowlists only the exact Prisma model/method pairs the canonical journey and its fixture/cleanup code actually use — it must not dispatch to an arbitrary `model`/`method` pair supplied in a request. It accepts only the exact intended request path and the `POST` method, rejecting any other path or method. It imposes a conservative maximum request-body size and rejects any request exceeding it. The bridge's own wire error response is a completely fixed literal, independent of the caught error's class or the request's content; its local console log may additionally include a safe built-in error class name derived from the actual caught Error, and the allowlisted operation name — but only once the request's `model` and `method` have both been confirmed to be runtime strings and the exact pair has been confirmed present in the allowlist. For every malformed, unknown, or disallowed request, the local log names the operation only as a fixed literal, never any request-controlled value — no unvalidated request field (including a rejected or disallowed `model`/`method`) is ever interpolated into a log, error, or response. No raw Error object, message, stack, cause, Prisma or PostgreSQL error, connection string, credential, request URL, host, port, file path, response body, or parser error is ever printed or returned anywhere, including locally. On the Playwright side, every RPC transport-layer failure (a non-success HTTP response, a network-level rejection, a missing or malformed JSON response, an invalid response envelope) is a fixed, stable, local message — including a non-success HTTP response, whose message never interpolates the response's own status code or any other response-controlled value (Stage 2 Correction Pass 4). For a structurally valid `{ ok: false, error: string }` envelope specifically, the client validates only the envelope's shape — that `ok` is `false` and `error` is a string — and never trusts or surfaces that string's content in a thrown error: a wrong or locally spoofed service on the loopback port could return arbitrary text, so the thrown client-side error is always the same fixed message regardless of what the envelope's `error` field contains. Startup and shutdown are failure-safe: if `listen` fails after the real `PrismaClient` has already been constructed, the bridge disconnects that client before rejecting the startup promise, and the original startup failure still propagates to `run-e2e.ts`'s own existing sanitized top-level handler (Section 6) rather than hanging indefinitely or being logged raw at the point of failure. `close()` is idempotent and always attempts both the HTTP-server shutdown and the Prisma disconnection, regardless of whether either one fails, so a failure in one can never prevent or skip the other, and port 3101 is released whenever the server-close half succeeds independent of the Prisma-disconnect outcome; any resulting failure is surfaced only as a generic, sanitized rejection.

  **6. Cross-platform orchestration refinement.** `apps/web/e2e/support/run-e2e.ts` must not assume the bare executable name `pnpm` resolves identically through Node's child-process APIs on every platform; it resolves `const pnpmExecutable = process.platform === 'win32' ? 'pnpm.cmd' : 'pnpm';` and invokes both child processes — `pnpm --filter web build` and `pnpm --filter web exec playwright test` — through that resolved executable, using asynchronous `child_process.spawn` (never a synchronous form such as `execFileSync`, which would block this same process's own RPC bridge event loop, Section 5a, for as long as the child runs — directly confirmed as the cause of an earlier fixture-setup timeout). No dynamic, user-controlled, or credential-bearing value is ever concatenated into a command string; `command` and every element of `args` are static literals throughout, and every credential or secret travels exclusively through the `env` object passed to `spawn`, never through `command` or `args`. On Windows, launching the `pnpm.cmd` shim requires `shell: true` — `.cmd` files cannot be launched directly via `CreateProcess`, and this is Node's own documented mechanism for that case, not a departure from it; Playwright's own installed `webServer` launcher (`playwright/lib/runner/index.js`, confirmed directly against the installed source) uses the identical `shell: true` mechanism for the same reason. This decision does not claim no shell is ever involved on Windows — only that no dynamic or credential-bearing data is ever interpolated into the command that shell executes. If Node's `DEP0190` deprecation warning (passing both an `args` array and `shell: true` to `spawn`) proves unavoidable given this static-argument implementation, it is explicitly accepted rather than worked around. The orchestration order is fixed: (1) validate `TEST_DATABASE_URL`; (2) generate the ephemeral Better Auth secret; (3) construct the explicitly overridden child environment; (4) start the real-Prisma RPC bridge (Section 5a); (5) build `apps/web`; (6) invoke Playwright; (7) close the RPC bridge; (8) propagate a nonzero exit status from either the build or the test step. Top-level failure reporting (`main().catch`) logs only a fixed, stable failure message plus the error's safe class/constructor name — never the underlying error's raw message, stack trace, or any credential- or connection-bearing text; full diagnostic detail for a build or test failure remains available through the child processes' own inherited stdio output, which streams before this top-level handler ever runs.

  **7. Playwright server/configuration contract.** `playwright.config.ts` uses: `testDir: './e2e'`; exactly one Chromium project; `workers: 1`; `fullyParallel: false`; `retries: 0`; `baseURL: 'http://127.0.0.1:3100'`; an explicit journey timeout suitable for the complete twelve-step flow and an explicit `expect` timeout; a `webServer` whose `command` is exactly `next start --port 3100 --hostname 127.0.0.1`, whose readiness `url` is exactly `http://127.0.0.1:3100/api/health` (verified to perform zero database or Prisma calls, so it truthfully signals process readiness without requiring any fixture to exist first), `reuseExistingServer: false` unconditionally (never reused, never attached to an unrelated process or a server connected to another database), an explicit server-start timeout, and a `webServer.env` restating the four application-critical values from Section 5 — a defense-in-depth second assertion of `childEnv`'s own already-validated values (Sections 5 and 6), not the sole or first place those values are set. The existing development server on port 3000 is never touched. The E2E run always exercises a production Next.js build produced by `run-e2e.ts` immediately before Playwright starts — never `next dev`.

  **8. Reporter and generated artifacts.** Configuration uses exactly: reporter `'list'` only (no HTML reporter, no blob reporter, no automatic report opening); `outputDir: './test-results'`; `screenshot: 'only-on-failure'`; `trace: 'off'`; `video: 'retain-on-failure'`. Tracing is disabled entirely, not merely retained on failure: Playwright's trace format captures full network request bodies, including the `/login` form's own POST body, which necessarily contains the fixture's plaintext test password (Section 4) — no trace-retention policy can be made consistent with this contract's prohibition on ever persisting that password, so no trace artifact of any kind is generated by any run. Only `apps/web/test-results/` is added to `.gitignore` — `playwright-report/` and `blob-report/` are not added, since this configuration never creates either directory.

  **9. Failure-safe fixture discovery and teardown refinement.** Fixture cleanup does not depend solely on IDs the browser test successfully captured during its run: a browser action may commit a database transaction and then fail before the spec records the resulting URL or id, and cleanup must still discover and remove that already-committed chain. The unique test-created TC actor is the cleanup root. Before deleting `AuditLog` rows, teardown queries that actor's own `AuditLog` rows (`where: { actorId: tcUserId }`) to rediscover every committed entity id the test run produced — the Lead id from `LEAD_CREATED`; the Client id from `CLIENT_CREATED` or `LEAD_CONVERTED_TO_CLIENT`; the Proposal id from `PROPOSAL_CREATED`; the `ProposalVersion` id from `PROPOSAL_CREATED.afterState.firstVersionId` and any version-targeted audit row; the Booking id from `BOOKING_CREATED` — and may supplement this with relationship queries from those recovered ids. Locally tracked ids (recorded as the spec itself observes them) may be used as an additional source but must never be the sole cleanup mechanism. Because the fixture actor's id is unique to this test run, querying `AuditLog` by that exact `actorId` can never select another run's data. After discovery, cleanup deletes only the recovered test actor/entity ids, in this verified FK-safe order: (1) `AuditLog` rows for the exact test actor/entities; (2) `BookingStatusHistory`; (3) `StaffAssignment` rows connected to the recovered Lead, Client, Booking, or fixture actor; (4) `Booking`; (5) `ProposalAcceptance`; (6) `ProposalVersion`; (7) `Proposal`; (8) `LeadStatusHistory`; (9) `Lead`; (10) `Client`; (11) `User` last, allowing `Account` and `Session` to cascade-delete automatically (`onDelete: Cascade` on both, confirmed against `apps/web/prisma/schema.prisma`). Every delete uses an explicit id-list filter (`deleteMany({ where: { id: { in: [...] } } })` or the equivalent relationship filter) — idempotent by construction, and never a broad or unfiltered `deleteMany`. Cleanup executes after test success or failure alike (Playwright's own post-`use()` fixture teardown, which runs regardless of the test body's outcome) and disconnects the dedicated Prisma client in a `finally` block. If teardown itself fails, the E2E run fails and reports only the non-sensitive fixture/entity ids needed for manual remediation — never credentials or PII.

  **10. Exact database assertion contract.** Identity and linkage: `Lead.clientId === Client.id`; `Proposal.clientId === Client.id`; `ProposalVersion.proposalId === Proposal.id`; `ProposalVersion.clientVisibleAt` is non-null and `ProposalVersion.supersededAt` is null (the schema's actual derived "current" state — confirmed by reading the full `Proposal` model, `apps/web/prisma/schema.prisma:1413-1436`, that no `Proposal.currentVersionId` field exists; this decision does not assert one); `ProposalAcceptance.proposalVersionId === ProposalVersion.id` and `.responseType === 'ACCEPT'`; `Booking.clientId === Client.id`; `Booking.proposalVersionId === ProposalVersion.id`. Assignments: the initial active Lead `StaffAssignment` points to the canonical TC before conversion; after conversion, the active Client `StaffAssignment` points to that same TC; the original Lead assignment remains active and unaffected by conversion (D-024 §5/§6 — conversion never ends the Lead's own assignment); no Booking assignment is expected or asserted, since the canonical `CreateBookingButton` flow calls only `createBooking`, never `setBookingAssignment`. `LeadStatusHistory` contains exactly three rows — `null → NEW`, `NEW → QUALIFIED`, and `QUALIFIED → CONVERTED_TO_CLIENT` — all three with `changedByUserId` equal to the canonical TC; the third row is written by `convertLeadWithHistory`'s own nested `statusHistory.create` inside the same `lead.update` call that sets `status: CONVERTED_TO_CLIENT` (`apps/web/src/features/leads/repository.ts`, confirmed directly against the installed source; this corrects this entry's original assertion, made without that direct source check, that conversion writes no third row — Stage 2 Correction Pass 1). `BookingStatusHistory` contains exactly two rows — `null → DRAFT` and `DRAFT → PENDING_CONFIRMATION`, both `changedByUserId` equal to the canonical TC. `AuditLog` contains exactly these eleven rows for the fixture actor and test chain — confirmed against `features/leads/audit.ts`, `features/assignments/audit.ts`, `features/proposals/audit.ts`, and `features/bookings/audit.ts` directly, not assumed — each asserted for its exact `action`, `actorId` (the canonical TC), `entityType`, `entityId`, expected count of exactly one, and only the specific linkage/status fields named, never a full re-test of an already-accepted sanitizer's shape: `LEAD_CREATED` (`Lead`/Lead.id, `afterState.status: 'NEW'`); `LEAD_ASSIGNMENT_CREATED` (`Lead`/Lead.id, `afterState.assignedStaffId`/`leadId`); `LEAD_STATUS_CHANGED` (`Lead`/Lead.id, `afterState.status: 'QUALIFIED'`); `LEAD_CONVERTED_TO_CLIENT` (`Lead`/Lead.id, `afterState.clientId`/`clientCreated: true`); `CLIENT_CREATED` (`Client`/Client.id, `afterState.sourceLeadId`); `CLIENT_ASSIGNMENT_CREATED` (`Client`/Client.id, `afterState.assignedStaffId`/`clientId`); `PROPOSAL_CREATED` (`Proposal`/Proposal.id, `afterState.clientId`/`firstVersionId`); `PROPOSAL_VERSION_PUBLISHED` (`ProposalVersion`/ProposalVersion.id, `afterState.proposalId`); `PROPOSAL_RESPONSE_RECORDED` (`ProposalVersion`/ProposalVersion.id, `afterState.responseType: 'ACCEPT'`); `BOOKING_CREATED` (`Booking`/Booking.id, `afterState.clientId`/`proposalVersionId`); `BOOKING_STATUS_CHANGED` (`Booking`/Booking.id, `afterState.status: 'PENDING_CONFIRMATION'`). `PROPOSAL_VERSION_CREATED` is not expected — the canonical flow calls only `createProposal`, never `createProposalRevision`, and `createProposal`'s own first-version identity is folded into `PROPOSAL_CREATED`'s `afterState` rather than written as a second action. No total or partial `AuditLog` timestamp ordering is asserted: `AuditLog.id` is a random (non-sequential) UUID and `createdAt` carries no compound tie-breaker index (`apps/web/prisma/schema.prisma:727-743`), so exact ordering — particularly among rows written inside the same transaction, such as rows 4–6 above from `convertLead` — is not a property this schema deterministically supports.

  **11. Exact Stage 2 file boundary.** Stage 2 implementation is authorized in exactly these eight paths, and no other tracked file, unless a separately reviewed contract correction to this decision explicitly expands this boundary:
  1. `apps/web/package.json` — existing, modified
  2. `pnpm-lock.yaml` — existing, regenerated by the authorized `pnpm` dependency installation; never hand-edited
  3. `apps/web/playwright.config.ts` — new
  4. `apps/web/e2e/lead-to-booking-flow.spec.ts` — new
  5. `apps/web/e2e/support/test-database.ts` — new
  6. `apps/web/e2e/support/fixtures.ts` — new
  7. `apps/web/e2e/support/run-e2e.ts` — new
  8. `.gitignore` — existing, modified only to add `apps/web/test-results/`

  Stage 2 pins `"@playwright/test": "1.62.1"`, verified through the npm registry on August 17, 2026 using `npm view @playwright/test version` and `npm view @playwright/test engines`; the reported engine requirement is Node `>=20`, satisfied by this repository's Node `>=24.0.0` requirement. Installing `@playwright/test` does not install browser binaries; Chromium installation remains the separate, required environment prerequisite: `pnpm --filter web exec playwright install chromium`.

  **12. Validation contract.** Stage 2 cannot be accepted unless: the Chromium binary is installed using the authorized command above; `pnpm test` passes; `pnpm --filter web run test:integration` passes three consecutive genuine times against `heritage_v3_test`; `pnpm --filter web run test:e2e` passes at least three consecutive genuine times; no E2E test is skipped in any of those runs; Chromium genuinely launches (confirmed by the `list` reporter's own output naming the `chromium` project); every E2E run validates `heritage_v3_test` before build/server startup and performs the real login and the full twelve-step canonical journey; fixture teardown leaves no row from the test chain, confirmed by an independent post-run query; `pnpm lint` passes; `pnpm typecheck` passes; each E2E run performs its own successful production build (Section 6), so a redundant standalone `pnpm build` is not separately required; `pnpm format:check` is run and its result reported accurately; `git diff --check` passes; the implementation diff contains exactly the eight files authorized in Section 11 — no other file; tracing is confirmed disabled (`trace: 'off'`) and no trace artifact is generated by any run (Section 8) — never retained and then inspected for the password's absence, since no retention policy for a trace can be made consistent with never persisting it; and no credential, connection string, or plaintext password appears in any log, report, screenshot, video, generated filename, or the diff itself. GitHub Actions execution of `test:e2e` is not authorized under this decision — all E2E evidence is local real-PostgreSQL/real-browser evidence and must never be represented as CI evidence.

  **13. Explicit exclusions.** This decision does not authorize: any production-code change; any schema or migration change; any Vitest configuration change; any CI workflow change; any root `package.json` change; any environment-file change; any Phase 3 client-portal behavior; any Phase 5 staging verification; any second-role or additional negative-boundary sweep (Section 3); any revision-specific (`createProposalRevision`) Proposal behavior; any Booking-assignment behavior; any browser other than Chromium; any HTML or blob report infrastructure; and any unrelated refactor or test backfill.

  **14. Closure rules.** This decision records the implementation contract only. It does not claim that `@playwright/test` or Chromium is installed, that any of the eight files in Section 11 exist or are modified, that the E2E test has run, that any item in Section 12's validation contract has passed, that the Phase 2 Task Board item is complete, that Phase 2 itself is complete, or that Phase 3 may begin. `docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md` is not modified by this entry. Task Board closure of the "End-to-end verification..." checklist item, and any resulting Phase 2 completion-gate determination, remains a later, separate stage, made only once the Stage 2 implementation described above and its complete Section 12 validation contract actually exist and pass — mirroring D-022 through D-032's identical, already-established precedent of never marking a Task Board item complete from within the decision that authorizes the underlying work.

- **Rationale:** Requiring both a real browser journey and paired direct database assertions (Section 2), rather than either tier alone, reflects Correction Pass 1's own literal parsing of the Phase 2 completion gate's two independent clauses — "staff can manage... through the implemented admin surfaces" and "automated coverage passing" — neither of which this codebase's own established D-022–D-032 precedent (mocked-tier UI proof plus separately-scoped real-Postgres backend proof, never combined) had previously been asked to satisfy together for a single, phase-closing item. Reusing the already-accepted `prisma/seed.ts`/`createStaffAccount` credential technique (D-012) for fixture authentication, rather than a mocked session or fabricated cookie, and reusing the already-established `validateTestDatabaseUrl` allowlist pattern for the new dedicated E2E Prisma client, extend existing, already-reviewed mechanisms rather than inventing new ones. Removing the second unauthorized-TC case (Section 3) reflects a precise re-examination finding no schema-level distinction between an inherited and a directly created `StaffAssignment` row for the shared authorization query to behave differently against — duplicating, not extending, D-024/D-027/D-031's already-accepted evidence. Rediscovering committed entity ids via the fixture actor's own `AuditLog` rows (Section 9), rather than relying solely on ids the spec happens to capture during the browser journey, closes a genuine failure-mode gap a browser test — unlike a pure service-layer test — actually has: a UI action can commit and then fail before its result is ever read back into the spec. Disabling Playwright tracing entirely (Section 8), rather than retaining it only on failure, reflects that Playwright's trace format captures full network request bodies — including the `/login` form's POST body, which necessarily contains the fixture's plaintext test password (Section 4) — so no trace-retention policy can be made consistent with this contract's own prohibition on ever persisting that password; screenshot and video capture remain unaffected, since neither can reveal a masked password field's actual character content. Omitting `AuditLog` ordering assertions (Section 10) reflects direct schema verification that `AuditLog.id` is a non-sequential UUID with no compound `createdAt` tie-breaker, so an exact-order claim is not one this schema can be honestly asked to support. Declining CI execution (Section 12) mirrors D-032's own explicit, already-accepted non-blocking treatment of `COV-03`.
- **Constraint:** No `schema.prisma` change and no migration are authorized or required by this decision. No existing action name, error code, entity-type constant, route, service function, repository function, or UI component is renamed, altered, or otherwise changed — every assertion in Section 10 proves behavior the existing, already-accepted D-022–D-032 implementation already exhibits; none of it requires or authorizes a production-code change to pass. `docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md` is not modified by this entry. `apps/web/vitest.config.ts` is not modified — the new `apps/web/e2e/` tree is deliberately outside its `include: ['src/**/*.test.ts', 'src/**/*.test.tsx']` glob. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and the root `package.json` are not modified. The eight files authorized in Section 11 are the only files this decision authorizes changing under Stage 2; no ninth file, and no file outside that exact list, may be modified under this decision unless a separately reviewed contract correction expands it first.
- **Deferred:** Any CI provisioning of the E2E tier; any second-role or additional negative-boundary sweep beyond Section 3's reasoning; any revision-specific (`createProposalRevision`) Proposal coverage; any Booking-assignment (`setBookingAssignment`) coverage; any browser engine other than Chromium; any HTML/blob Playwright reporter infrastructure; any Phase 3 client-portal or Phase 5 staging work; and Task Board closure of the "End-to-end verification of the full lead → client → proposal → booking flow in a real PostgreSQL-backed development/test environment" item itself, which remains its own later, separate stage.
- **Effect:** Authorizes Stage 2 implementation of exactly the Playwright-based, database-paired end-to-end verification contract described above, in exactly the eight files listed in Section 11, toward closing Phase 2's sole remaining checklist item. Does not itself implement any file, install any dependency or browser, or run any test, and does not mark that — or any other — Task Board item, or Phase 2 itself, complete; that determination remains a later, separate closure checkpoint, made only once the Stage 2 implementation described above, and its required validation (Section 12), actually exist and pass.

## D-034 — Portal Invitation, Account Activation, and Client Home / Overview Contract

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Date accepted:** August 20, 2026
- **Context:** Phase 2 — Admin Dashboard Core is complete (D-033); Phase 3 — Client Dashboard Core is active (`docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md`). This decision is the authoritative implementation contract for Phase 3's first slice — the portal invitation flow, account activation, and the Client Home / Overview landing page — on the basis of an exhaustive, evidence-based Stage 0 discovery pass and its three subsequent correction passes (recorded in this session as "D-034 Stage 0 — Portal Invitation and Account Activation Discovery," Correction Passes 1 through 3, the sole governing discovery result for this entry). This entry is self-contained — it is the authoritative contract for implementation, not a pointer to a separate report. This entry records the contract only: no application code, schema, migration, dependency, or configuration has been implemented, installed, or applied under this decision. It does not itself update `docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md`.

  **1. Authoritative scope.** This decision authorizes, across the separately reviewed stages in Section 15: PortalInvitation prepare/send (automated and audited manual)/resend/reissue/revoke; the public token-validation and activation backend; the admin Client-detail invitation-management UI; CLIENT-role post-login routing; and a production-quality Client Home / Overview landing page at `/client` (Section 14). It authorizes an additive Prisma schema/migration direction (Section 13) and a case-insensitive `User.email` uniqueness direction (Section 8), neither implemented in this Stage 1. It does not authorize any other Phase 3 module (Section 16).

  **2. Delivery-channel contract.** D-034 supports three coordinated states:
  - **(a) Preparation.** Staff may prepare an invitation before sending it. `INVITATION_PREPARED` is a genuine, persisted state that never itself claims delivery. A prepared row may initially carry no token or expiry (Section 13). No client may activate from `INVITATION_PREPARED` under any circumstance — activation requires an auditable send confirmation first (Section 6).
  - **(b) Automated transactional email.** Resend is the selected automated transactional-email provider. Integration uses Resend's normal Node.js/Next.js API-key path and must not depend on the Vercel Marketplace integration, since the deployment target remains Hostinger (ADR-001). The intended sender identity is a Heritage Philippines sending-domain address; the exact sender address, Resend account, API key, DNS access, and domain verification are not yet confirmed and are never guessed at or written into documentation (Section 12). Automated sending must fail closed — refusing to attempt a send, never silently marking an invitation as sent — whenever required configuration or domain verification is unavailable. The audited manual-email channel (below) remains available independently of automated-send readiness. No secret value may be committed, logged, placed in documentation, or requested through a normal application field. Non-production environments must use Resend test addresses, sandbox behavior, or an explicit recipient allowlist, and must never send an invitation email to a real client address by accident. Invitation volume is unknown; no document may claim the Resend free tier is adequate or hard-code a plan assumption.
