Resend's provider-idempotency behavior must be reverified against its own official documentation during implementation — the currently verified contract is a 24-hour idempotency-key window, but this is not represented as permanent. Webhook signatures must be verified against the raw request body before any `delivered`, `bounced`, `complained`, or `suppressed` event is accepted.
  - **(c) Audited manual email.** An authorized staff member may generate an activation link and send it through their own normal email client. The system records the channel (manual email), the frozen destination address, the confirming staff actor, and the confirmation timestamp. Merely displaying or copying the link never marks it sent — staff must take an explicit, separate confirmation action. If the link is lost before that confirmation, it cannot be reconstructed from its stored digest; staff must rotate/regenerate it (Section 4). Manual delivery is a supported, audited fallback, never a hidden workaround. It supports `emailVerified: true` only under the exact conditions in Section 7. No SMS, chat, phone, or in-person verification channel is included in D-034.

  **3. State machine.** Valid transitions on `PortalInvitation.status`: `(no row) → INVITATION_PREPARED` (prepare); `INVITATION_PREPARED → INVITATION_SENT` (first send, automated-accepted or manual-confirmed); `INVITATION_SENT ⇄ INVITATION_OPENED` (an explicit Continue confirmation, Section 5 — never a bare GET); `INVITATION_SENT | INVITATION_OPENED → INVITATION_SENT` (explicit resend — a genuinely new lifecycle event, Section 4, never inferred from a retry); `INVITATION_PREPARED | INVITATION_SENT | INVITATION_OPENED → INVITATION_REVOKED` (revoke, reason required, Section 9); `INVITATION_SENT | INVITATION_OPENED → INVITATION_EXPIRED` (computed from `expiresAt`, persisted only per Section 4's conditional-write rule); `INVITATION_EXPIRED | INVITATION_REVOKED → INVITATION_SENT` (authorized reissue, in place, same row — Section 4); `INVITATION_SENT | INVITATION_OPENED → ACCOUNT_ACTIVATED` (activation, Section 6). `ACCOUNT_ACTIVATED` is terminal — no further transition of any kind is valid from it. No row is ever deleted and recreated during resend or reissue; every one of these transitions updates the same `PortalInvitation` row identified by its `clientId`.

  **4. Token, expiry, and reissue rules.** Raw invitation tokens are generated with `generateRandomString` (`better-auth/crypto`, confirmed present and reusable — the same primitive `features/staff/service.ts`'s `createStaffAccount` and `apps/web/e2e/support/fixtures.ts` already use), at length 24 over the alphabet `a-z`,`A-Z`,`0-9`,`-_` (64 symbols): 24 × log₂(64) = **144 bits of entropy**, confirmed uniform by direct inspection of the installed generator's rejection-sampling implementation (`@better-auth/utils@0.4.2`'s `random.mjs`), which for this exact 64-symbol alphabet accepts every possible byte value with zero rejection (256 is an exact multiple of 64). Only a **deterministic SHA-256 digest** of the raw token is ever persisted, for indexed lookup (`PortalInvitation.tokenHash @unique`). Better Auth's salted `hashPassword` must never be used for invitation-token storage — its per-call random salt makes an exact-match lookup structurally impossible, confirmed directly against the installed `better-auth/dist/crypto/password.mjs`. The raw token exists only transiently: in the sending/manual-display request's own memory, and in the client's activation request — never persisted in plaintext anywhere, never logged, never written to `AuditLog`. **Invitation validity is seven calendar days from the token's own issuance** — reset by every send/resend/reissue, never extended merely by the invitation being opened. Security-relevant decisions (activation eligibility, resend/reissue authorization) always compute effective expiry from `expiresAt` directly, regardless of what the persisted `status` column currently reads; a safe GET (Section 5) must never write `INVITATION_EXPIRED` merely because time has passed, but an activation POST that observes expiry may conditionally persist that transition, and an admin read may present an effective-expired state without itself performing a GET-time mutation. An explicit resend always rotates `tokenHash`/`expiresAt` and invalidates the prior link; a bare retry must never be silently treated as an explicit resend. Prepare and revoke retries are idempotent no-ops (returning current state unchanged, mirroring `changeStaffRole`'s and `deactivateStaffAccount`'s established already-in-requested-state precedent, `features/staff/service.ts:171-176,215-217`). First-send retries are reconciled using a stable send-operation identifier (Section 10), never by blindly rotating on every retry. No database transaction may ever remain open across a Resend API call or any other network call — the transaction that records the outcome opens only after that call has returned or definitively failed. Provider-timeout ambiguity is represented honestly: if the outcome cannot be confirmed, the system never claims delivery, and the row requires either reconciliation (a later webhook/status check) or an explicit resend that rotates the link.

  **5. Safe transport and OPENED semantics.** The token-bearing `GET` is safe per RFC 9110 §9.2.1 — it performs no lifecycle mutation of any kind, never marks the invitation `INVITATION_OPENED`, and never activates or consumes it. A separate, explicit **Continue** `POST` — requiring a genuine user action on the rendered landing page, never triggered by the initial GET — records the `INVITATION_SENT → INVITATION_OPENED` transition; repeating Continue is an idempotent no-op. `openedAt` is never treated as proof of human identity and is never used as a fraud signal. The activation password `POST` performs the final, single-use redemption and is the only request that may transition the row to `ACCOUNT_ACTIVATED`. D-034 uses the **direct activation-POST design** — no `ActivationSession` table and no stateless activation cookie/nonce are introduced. The activation URL uses a fixed route, preferring `/activate/[token]` unless implementation evidence demonstrates a safer, equally compatible alternative. Every activation-flow response sets `Cache-Control: no-store` and `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`. The activation page loads no third-party script, analytics, font, or beacon of any kind. No caller-controlled post-activation return URL is accepted anywhere in this flow. Application logs, `AuditLog`, error responses, test output, and the UI itself must never echo the raw token or a submitted password. Playwright tracing remains `off` (D-033 §8, unchanged); screenshots/video capture page content, not browser chrome — if any manual-link admin UI displays a raw token, the later test suite must produce direct evidence that failure artifacts (screenshots, video, retained console/failure text) do not retain it, never assume their absence.

  **6. Activation eligibility, identity, and atomicity contract.** The activation endpoint accepts only `status ∈ {INVITATION_SENT, INVITATION_OPENED}`; `INVITATION_PREPARED`, `INVITATION_EXPIRED`, `INVITATION_REVOKED`, and `ACCOUNT_ACTIVATED` are never redeemable, and every rejection uses a generic, anti-enumeration-safe public error indistinguishable across these cases (mirroring `features/clients/service.ts`'s established `notFoundOrForbidden` anti-enumeration discipline, extended to an unauthenticated context). The invitation must reference an existing `Client`. On success, in one atomic transaction (mirroring `prisma/seed.ts`'s and `features/staff/repository.ts`'s `createStaffUser`'s established `user.create` + nested `accounts.create` pattern): a new `User` is created with `name` from `Client.fullName`, `email` from the invitation's frozen destination email (Section 7), and `role: 'CLIENT'` explicit; a linked `Account` is created with `providerId: 'credential'` and `accountId` equal to the new `User.id`, following the existing reviewed account-creation pattern exactly; the submitted password is hashed with Better Auth's existing password hasher (`hashPassword` from `better-auth/crypto`) — never a new cryptographic primitive; password and confirmation must match, with a minimum length of **12 characters** and a reasonable implementation-defined maximum solely to prevent abuse; a `ClientProfile` is created linking the new `User.id` and the invitation's `Client.id`; and `PortalInvitation.status` is set to `ACCOUNT_ACTIVATED`. Every existing `User` email collision, of any role, is rejected — no existing account is ever automatically linked. Every existing `ClientProfile` collision or otherwise-inconsistent invitation/profile state (Section 6 of D-034 Stage 0 Correction Pass 1) is rejected and reported diagnostically only to server-side/staff-visible channels, never to the unauthenticated caller, whose response remains generic. No `Client` field, including `email`, is ever modified by activation. A `Client` must have a valid, present email before an email invitation (automated or manual) may be generated at all. The destination-email snapshot (Section 7) is normalized consistently and frozen at the moment its token is generated; a `Client.email` changed afterward never alters an already-issued invitation — staff must explicitly resend/reissue to the new address.

  **7. Email-verification evidence contract.** `emailVerified` on the activation-created `User` may be set `true` only when the redeeming token's invitation was: (a) sent by Resend to its recorded frozen destination email; or (b) sent via the audited manual-email channel, explicitly confirmed by an authorized staff actor, to that same recorded frozen destination email. Every other case — an unrecorded channel/destination, a non-email delivery method, or an activation-collected email differing from the recorded destination — leaves `emailVerified: false`; a collected email differing from the frozen destination is never silently accepted as verified, and requires either a separate verification step (not authorized by this decision) or outright rejection of the mismatch.

  **8. Case-insensitive `User.email` uniqueness.** A later implementation stage is authorized to: add a PostgreSQL database-enforced case-insensitive unique invariant for `User.email`, preferably a functional unique index on `lower(email)` (mirroring this schema's existing precedent of hand-written constraints beyond Prisma's own schema language, e.g. the `StaffAssignment`/`Document` CHECK constraints already applied); perform a read-only preflight for existing case-variant duplicates before generating or applying that migration, stopping and reporting rather than proceeding if any are found; never silently merge, rename, or delete an account; and preserve the existing `User.email` field and its existing plain unique constraint unless implementation evidence requires a separately reviewed adjustment. An application-level normalized-email lookup may be used for clean error handling, but the database invariant — not the application check — is what closes the concurrent-creation race: canonicalizing to lowercase before every trusted write, combined with the existing unique constraint, prevents duplication only among application code paths that actually obey that canonicalization; the functional database index additionally protects the invariant when casing conventions are bypassed and makes the rule explicit and enforced at the database layer itself, closing the race a pre-check alone cannot.

  **9. Authorization and audit contract.** Invitation prepare/generate/send/resend/reissue/revoke: `ADMIN_MANAGER` is authorized unconditionally; `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT` is authorized only while holding a current active `StaffAssignment` for that Client, re-verified transaction-locally as the first operation inside the mutation (mirroring D-031 F-01's established `runSerializableWithRetry` + fresh in-transaction recheck pattern) — a lost-access retry receives the same controlled forbidden result the pre-transaction check would have; every other role, including `SYSTEM_ADMINISTRATOR`, is rejected. Revoke requires a `reason`; the reason is recorded only in the minimized `AuditLog.afterState` (mirroring `deactivateStaffAccount`'s exact `afterState: { isActive: false, reason }` precedent) — no dedicated `PortalInvitation.revocationReason` column is required. Every invitation-lifecycle `AuditLog` entry uses entity type `'PortalInvitation'` and `PortalInvitation.id` as `entityId`, including the `ACCOUNT_ACTIVATED` entry. Explicit audit actions are recorded for: successful prepare (`PORTAL_INVITATION_PREPARED`); automated send accepted by the provider (`PORTAL_INVITATION_SENT_AUTOMATED`); manual-send confirmation (`PORTAL_INVITATION_SENT_MANUAL_CONFIRMED`); resend and reissue, sharing one action since both rotate the token identically (`PORTAL_INVITATION_RESENT`); revoke (`PORTAL_INVITATION_REVOKED`); the Continue-confirmed opened transition (`PORTAL_INVITATION_OPENED`); and activation (`PORTAL_INVITATION_ACTIVATED`) — these names are the authorized naming direction for the later implementation stage, not yet defined in any source file. The raw token, the token digest, any password, a complete raw provider payload, and any Client PII beyond what each snapshot minimally requires are forbidden from every audit snapshot. The newly created `CLIENT` `User` is the recorded actor for `PORTAL_INVITATION_ACTIVATED`, valid only because that `User` row already exists, in the same transaction, by the time the audit insert runs — confirmed technically supportable by `AuditLog.actorId`'s plain `User` foreign key, which imposes no role restriction.

  **10. Delivery evidence and provider-state model.** A later schema-design stage is authorized to add minimized fields/enums sufficient to distinguish exactly: not attempted; automated-provider-accepted; manually-confirmed-sent; provider-delivered; failed; bounced; complained; and suppressed — at minimum a frozen destination-email field, a delivery-method field, a delivery-state field, a stable send-operation identifier, a provider message identifier, a delivery-confirmation timestamp, and a confirming-staff-identity field for manual email. Provider acceptance is never represented as confirmed recipient delivery anywhere — in UI copy, audit text, or documentation — and every one of the eight states above must be distinguished accurately wherever displayed.

  **11. Rate-limiting requirement.** The public activation flow cannot be declared staging- or production-ready without rate limiting, applied at minimum per request source and per token/invitation. It must use a production-stable backing mechanism, never process-local memory — exact infrastructure is selected during the public-activation implementation stage (Section 15, Stage 5) after reconciling Hostinger deployment constraints; if no trusted edge/provider limiter is available, a PostgreSQL-backed, privacy-minimized implementation is used instead. No raw IP address is stored unless separately justified at that time. Every rate-limit rejection remains generic and never discloses token validity.

  **12. Resend/manual-delivery configuration boundary.** A later implementation stage may introduce documented, server-only environment variables such as `RESEND_API_KEY`, `RESEND_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, `EMAIL_FROM`, optionally `EMAIL_REPLY_TO`, and an explicit email-delivery enablement/mode variable if needed to fail closed safely — none of these values may ever be placed in documentation. Automated delivery remains operationally disabled until: a Resend account exists; DNS control is confirmed; the Heritage Philippines sending domain is verified; the exact sender address is approved; and the required secrets are configured through the deployment environment, never committed. The audited manual-email channel remains fully functional without any of these prerequisites.

  **13. Prepared-token schema direction.** A later additive Prisma migration is authorized to: permit `PortalInvitation.tokenHash` and `expiresAt` to be null while the invitation is genuinely `INVITATION_PREPARED` and no link has ever been generated; require both for every redeemable state (`INVITATION_SENT`, `INVITATION_OPENED`, `ACCOUNT_ACTIVATED`); permit a revoked, never-sent prepared invitation to retain null token fields; enforce this with a database `CHECK` constraint plus service-layer validation, mirroring this schema's own established hand-written-constraint discipline; retain `tokenHash`'s uniqueness when non-null (PostgreSQL's standard unique-index semantics already tolerate multiple `NULL` values without conflict); and never store the raw token under any circumstance. The previously considered discarded-placeholder-token design — generating and hashing a token at prepare time purely to satisfy a non-null constraint, then discarding it unused before send generates a real one — is **rejected**: a `PREPARED` row must never contain a meaningless digest merely to satisfy an old constraint. This migration is not implemented in this Stage 1.

  **14. Activation handoff and full Client Overview scope.** Activation does not automatically sign the client in; successful activation redirects to a fixed login URL carrying a non-sensitive success indicator only. After a normal, subsequent login, `/dashboard` redirects `CLIENT`-role users to `/client` (extending D-029 §2's existing role-aware `dashboard/page.tsx` fork, which today has no `CLIENT` destination beyond the Phase-1 verification content). `/client` is a **production-quality Client Home / Overview landing page** — not the Phase-1 verification page, and not a temporary minimal placeholder. It must: enforce `CLIENT` role and `ClientProfile` ownership server-side, on every request; never accept a client-supplied Client id as authority for which record it reads (mirroring `canAccessClient`'s existing `findClientProfileOwnership`-based ownership check, `features/assignments/authorization.ts:84-109`, `repository.ts:144-153`); show the authenticated client's real Client/profile information; show useful summaries of their current proposals, bookings, and journey state using only already-authorized, already-existing data reads; provide deterministic, helpful empty states; show assigned-consultant/contact information only where the existing data model safely supports it; use the canonical Client Portal navigation terminology from blueprint Section 2.3 verbatim; be responsive and accessible per `.claude/rules/frontend.md`; avoid dead or misleading links; clearly mark any later-phase destination it references as not yet available, rather than pretending it works; and preserve every existing V2 public-site route, including the public My Journey planner (blueprint Section 2.3's explicit distinction from the portal's own "My Journey" area). "Full overview" does not pull any later module into D-034's scope — Section 16 lists exactly what remains excluded.

  **15. Implementation stages.** Each stage below requires its own new guarded checkpoint and its own separately authorized file scope; this Stage 1 authorizes documentation only and implements none of them:
  1. Stage 1 — contract and authoritative documentation synchronization (this entry).
  2. Stage 2 — additive schema/migration and environment/provider foundations.
  3. Stage 3 — invitation repository/service, authorization, audit, Resend adapter, verified webhooks, and manual-email fallback.
  4. Stage 4 — Admin Client-detail invitation-management UI.
  5. Stage 5 — public validation/Continue/activation backend and activation UI.
  6. Stage 6 — CLIENT login routing and the production-quality `/client` overview.
  7. Stage 7 — unit, route, component, real-PostgreSQL integration, security, and browser-level E2E verification.
  8. Stage 8 — later Task Board closure, only once the accepted D-034 scope is genuinely complete.

  **16. Non-goals.** Explicitly excluded from D-034: generic/public self-signup; automatic account linking or repair of any inconsistent identity state; SMS/chat/phone/in-person invitation-verification channels; marketing/broadcast email of any kind; real-client email delivery from any local/test environment; storing a plaintext token or password anywhere; automatic Booking/Proposal/PaymentPlan/VisaCase creation as a side effect of invitation or activation (blueprint Section 5.1); every client-portal module beyond the Client Home / Overview landing page — client proposal response, full My Journey detail, full Bookings detail, Payments & Receipts, Documents, Visa Center, Support & Messages, Profile/Settings workflows, and Regional Tours/tours content management; production DNS/account/API-key setup within this documentation stage; purchasing or assuming any particular Resend billing plan; and Phase 3 Task Board closure of any kind.

  **17. Validation contract for later stages.** No item below is claimed to have passed as of this entry's acceptance — this Stage 1 performs no implementation and no validation run. Later stages' acceptance requires, at minimum: unit coverage for the state machine (Section 3), token generation/digest handling (Section 4), and every idempotency/retry distinction named in Section 4; route/service coverage for the full authorization matrix (Section 9), including the transaction-local assignment recheck and its lost-access-versus-exhausted-retry split, mirroring D-031 F-01's established test shape; component coverage for the admin invitation panel and the activation UI's loading/empty/error/permission-denied/success states (`.claude/rules/frontend.md`); real-PostgreSQL integration coverage for prepare→send→resend→activate and prepare→revoke, existing-email collision (both `CLIENT` and staff roles), expired/revoked-token rejection, and concurrent activation/resend/revoke races; targeted security probes proving the raw token and password never reach a log, `AuditLog` row, error response, or (where applicable) a retained Playwright failure artifact — produced as direct evidence, never assumed; webhook-signature verification tests against the raw request body; rate-limiting behavior tests; a passing `pnpm test`, `pnpm --filter web run test:integration`, `pnpm --filter web run test:e2e`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm format:check`, `pnpm build`, and `git diff --check`; and a final diff review confirming each stage's changes remain within that stage's own separately authorized file boundary.

- **Rationale:** Splitting Phase 3's first slice into eight separately reviewable stages, rather than authorizing implementation in this same entry, mirrors this repository's own unbroken D-022 through D-033 precedent of separating contract acceptance from implementation acceptance, and is especially warranted here given the security sensitivity of an unauthenticated public endpoint, raw-token handling, and credential creation — surfaces this codebase has never previously exposed to an unauthenticated caller. Selecting Resend with a Hostinger-compatible (non-Vercel-Marketplace) integration path, and requiring automated sending to fail closed pending real domain/account configuration, avoids guessing at operational details (sender address, DNS, secrets) this repository has no authority to invent, consistent with `.claude/rules/backend.md`'s "no secret in source" discipline and this repository's established practice of never inventing an unconfirmed value. Requiring the audited manual-email fallback to remain independently functional, regardless of automated-send readiness, ensures Phase 3's actual client-facing capability does not silently depend on an external account this repository cannot provision for itself. Rejecting the discarded-placeholder-token design and instead authorizing nullable prepared-token columns (Section 13) avoids persisting a meaningless digest merely to satisfy an old constraint, at the honest cost of a later additive migration — a trade-off made explicit rather than hidden, per D-034 Stage 0 Correction Pass 3 §3. Requiring the direct activation-POST design (Section 5), rather than a server-mediated cookie/nonce exchange, accepts the residual browser-history/access-log token exposure that design shares with every link-based single-use-token system, in exchange for materially lower implementation complexity and no new session-adjacent state this codebase has never needed before — a deliberate, recorded trade-off, not an oversight. Conditioning `emailVerified` on the specific recorded evidence for each individual invitation (Section 7), rather than a blanket rule tied to which delivery mechanism exists, reflects that the logical proof genuinely differs case by case, not by whether automation was involved. Requiring a database-enforced case-insensitive `User.email` uniqueness mechanism (Section 8), rather than relying on an application-level convention alone, reflects this codebase's own repeated, already-accepted precedent (D-025 §7, D-031 F-01) that only a database-level check closes a genuine concurrent-write race.
- **Constraint:** No `schema.prisma` change and no migration are authorized or implemented by this Stage 1 entry — Sections 8, 10, and 13 authorize only the *direction* a later, separately reviewed migration must take. No dependency is installed. No environment variable is set or documented with a real value. No existing action name, error code, entity-type constant, route, service function, repository function, or UI component belonging to any prior decision (D-022 through D-033) is renamed, altered, or otherwise changed by this entry. `docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md` is not modified by this entry. Automated Resend delivery must never be represented as available or configured by any later stage's documentation until the operational prerequisites in Section 12 are genuinely met.
- **Deferred:** Every item named in Section 16; the exact naming/shape of the delivery-evidence schema fields (Section 10) beyond their required distinctions; the exact rate-limiting backing mechanism (Section 11); the exact activation-route path if `/activate/[token]` proves unsuitable (Section 5); and Task Board closure of any Phase 3 checklist item, which remains Stage 8's own later, separate responsibility.
- **Effect:** Authorizes Stage 2 through Stage 8 implementation of exactly the contract described above, each under its own separately reviewed guarded checkpoint and file-scope authorization. Does not itself implement any file, install any dependency, apply any migration, or run any test, and does not mark any Task Board item, Phase 3 itself, or any part of this contract, complete.

## D-035 — Portal Invitation Service, Delivery, and Manual-Workflow Implementation (D-034 Stage 3)

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Date accepted:** August 26, 2026
- **Context:** D-034 remains the historical foundation decision for the whole Portal Invitation / Account Activation contract and is not amended or superseded by this entry. This decision records what D-034 Stage 3 ("invitation repository/service, authorization, audit, Resend adapter, verified webhooks, and manual-email fallback") actually implemented — first under a user-authorized 8-point implementation contract, then corrected and hardened under a follow-up "D-034 Stage 3 Correction and Security Review Pass 1" that found and fixed several real defects (Sections 5–9 below) before any staging or commit. `docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md`'s Phase 3 completion gate ("An invited client can activate an account, view their proposal, and respond with Accept, Decline, or Request Changes end-to-end in staging") remains unmet and is not marked complete by this entry — Stage 4 (admin invitation-management UI) and Stage 5 (public activation) remain unimplemented.

  **1. Schema correction (two-file implementation of one logical migration).** Migrations `20260826090000_add_automated_unconfirmed_delivery_state` and `20260826090001_portal_invitation_unconfirmed_constraint_and_message_id_uniqueness` together implement one authorized logical correction to D-034's Section 10 delivery-state model: a ninth `PortalInvitationDeliveryState` value, `AUTOMATED_UNCONFIRMED` ("an automated send operation was reserved/attempted, but provider acceptance has not yet been confirmed" — a transport interruption or timeout whose actual provider outcome is unknown, never conflated with `PROVIDER_FAILED` or any accepted/delivered state), plus a `providerMessageId` unique index (`portal_invitation_providerMessageId_key`) so a webhook can correlate to at most one row. The two-file split is a PostgreSQL requirement, not a scope change: `ALTER TYPE ... ADD VALUE` cannot be referenced in a CHECK constraint within the same transaction that adds it ("unsafe use of new value ... must be committed before they can be used"), and `prisma migrate deploy` applies each migration file as one transaction. Only one existing constraint required a change — `portal_invitation_provider_states_require_automated`, extended to include `AUTOMATED_UNCONFIRMED` in its `NOT IN (...)` list — every other existing constraint (`delivery_method_presence`, `send_operation_id_presence`, `provider_message_id_automated_only`, `provider_confirmed_requires_message_id`) already generalized correctly to the new value by construction, confirmed by a rolled-back temp-table/real-row truth table on the local `heritage_v3_dev` database (6/6 cases correct) before this entry was written. Both migrations were applied only to the guarded local `heritage_v3_dev` and disposable `heritage_v3_test` databases; neither has been applied to staging or production.

  **2. Invitation module and API surface.** `apps/web/src/features/invitations/` (`repository.ts`, `service.ts`, `schemas.ts`, `audit.ts`, `errors.ts`, `token.ts`, `resend-adapter.ts`, `http.ts`) implements the full staff-facing invitation lifecycle, mirroring this codebase's established per-feature module shape (`features/staff/*`, `features/clients/*`). Routes: `GET/POST /api/clients/[id]/invitation` (read current state / prepare), `POST .../send`, `POST .../resend`, `POST .../revoke`, `POST .../confirm-manual-sent`, and the public `POST /api/webhooks/resend`. Stage 3 owns these routes outright — Stage 4 is UI-only, consuming them unmodified, mirroring the D-028 Booking-UI precedent (an already-built backend consumed by a later UI stage) rather than D-027's combined-decision pattern.

  **3. Token generation and hashing.** Raw invitation tokens are generated via `generateRandomString(24, 'a-z','A-Z','0-9','-_')` (`better-auth/crypto`, the same primitive `features/staff/service.ts` already uses for generated passwords — 144 bits of entropy). Only a SHA-256 digest (`node:crypto`'s `createHash('sha256')`, hex-encoded) is ever persisted as `tokenHash` — never Better Auth's salted `hashPassword`, which cannot support the exact-match lookup a redemption/activation request needs. The raw token exists only in the memory of the single request/response cycle that generates it; it is never logged, never written to `AuditLog`, and never persisted in any form other than its digest.

  **4. Automated delivery and provider error classification.** The `resend` npm package is pinned to an exact version, `6.22.1` (no caret range, matching this repository's dependency-pinning convention), confirmed at implementation time to include `resend.webhooks.verify()` — no separate `svix` dependency was required. `sendInvitation`/`resendInvitation` pass the idempotency key via the SDK's second argument (`resend.emails.send(payload, { idempotencyKey })`), confirmed against the installed package's own type declarations — never inside the payload's `headers`. The SDK's `{ data, error }` response contract is classified, not assumed to throw on failure: a positive `data.id` is `'accepted'`; an explicit `error` in the response is a definite `'definite-failure'` (→ `PROVIDER_FAILED`); a thrown transport/network exception is `'ambiguous'` (→ the row is left `AUTOMATED_UNCONFIRMED`, never guessed as failed). Automated delivery remains fail-closed by default (`EMAIL_DELIVERY_ENABLED` still defaults to `"false"`; the service layer additionally requires `RESEND_API_KEY` and `EMAIL_FROM` to both be present before ever attempting a send) — no real Resend account, API key, domain, or webhook secret has been configured in any environment by this decision.

  **5. Manual-email workflow, corrected.** Prepare (`INVITATION_PREPARED`) creates no token. `sendInvitation`/`resendInvitation` with `deliveryMethod: 'MANUAL_EMAIL'` generate and rotate the token exactly as the automated channel does, but — per the schema's `portal_invitation_delivery_method_presence`/`send_operation_id_presence` constraints, which force `deliveryMethod`/`sendOperationId` to stay null while `deliveryState` remains `NOT_ATTEMPTED` — deliberately leave `deliveryMethod`/`deliveryState`/`sendOperationId` untouched at `NOT_ATTEMPTED` until the separate `confirm-manual-sent` action; no provider call is ever made on this channel. **Correction:** the original Stage 3 implementation generated the raw token inside this reservation but never returned it to the caller — an authorized staff action could not actually retrieve the one-time manual link. `SendResult` now carries an optional `manualInvitationUrl` (the full activation URL, built via `buildActivationUrl(rawToken)`), populated only for a freshly reserved `MANUAL_EMAIL` send/resend — never for the automated channel, never for a cross-request idempotent retry (the raw token is never persisted and cannot be safely reconstructed), and never returned by a later `GET`. Merely generating/returning this URL never marks the invitation delivered — only `confirm-manual-sent` does, and only via its own explicit action. **Correction:** `confirm-manual-sent` now requires a client-supplied, validated-UUID `Idempotency-Key` header (identical discipline to send/resend), stored as this confirmation's own `sendOperationId` — the original implementation server-generated this value, accepting no caller-supplied idempotency key at all. Confirmation remains an unconditional idempotent no-op once already `MANUALLY_CONFIRMED`, regardless of which key a retry supplies, since it never rotates a token and has nothing further to protect against.

  **6. Stale-resend optimistic-concurrency contract.** `GET /api/clients/[id]/invitation` exposes the invitation's current `sendOperationId`/`updatedAt`. A genuine new `resendInvitation` call must supply `expectedCurrentSendOperationId` (nullable UUID) and `expectedUpdatedAt` (ISO datetime, mirroring `features/clients/schemas.ts`'s already-established `expectedUpdatedAt` pattern exactly) in its body, alongside a fresh `Idempotency-Key`. If the incoming key already equals the invitation's current `sendOperationId`, the current result is returned idempotently, with no precondition check. Otherwise the service compares `expectedCurrentSendOperationId`/`expectedUpdatedAt` against the invitation's actual current values — first explicitly (clear error attribution), then a second time atomically in the write's own `WHERE id AND sendOperationId AND updatedAt` clause (`recordSendReservationIfUnstale`, mirroring `features/clients/repository.ts`'s `updateClientFieldsIfUnstale` exactly) — rejecting a mismatch with a stable `INVITATION_SEND_OPERATION_STALE` (409) and performing no mutation or provider call. `sendInvitation` (first send only, from `INVITATION_PREPARED`) needs no equivalent precondition: a later retry with a different idempotency key already fails its status check instead of silently rotating anything.

  **7. Webhook correlation, ordering, and transactional recheck, corrected.** All 11 Resend webhook event types are handled: `email.sent`/`email.delivery_delayed` reconcile `AUTOMATED_UNCONFIRMED` → `AUTOMATED_ACCEPTED` (capturing `providerMessageId`), guarded to transition only *from* `AUTOMATED_UNCONFIRMED` (never regressing a later state); `email.delivered`/`bounced`/`complained`/`failed`/`suppressed` map to their matching terminal `deliveryState` unconditionally (a complaint may legitimately supersede an already-delivered state); `email.opened`/`clicked`/`received`/`scheduled`, and any unrecognized event, are acknowledged (200) with no lifecycle mutation — `email.opened`/`clicked` never produce `PORTAL_INVITATION_OPENED`, which remains exclusively Stage 5's token-bearing transition. Correlation is by `providerMessageId` (`@unique`) and by the signed `sendOperationId` tag; **corrected** to attempt both lookups whenever both identifiers are present rather than treating the tag as a fallback consulted only when the message-id lookup fails — the original design could never detect two independently-successful lookups resolving to different rows, which is now rejected as ambiguous (no mutation) alongside the original fallback-row-disagrees case. **Corrected:** every fresh token reservation (`recordSendReservation`/`recordSendReservationIfUnstale`) now unconditionally clears `providerMessageId` to null, closing the path by which a late webhook event for an attempt already superseded by a resend could otherwise still correlate to the row via its stale message id and mutate delivery evidence that no longer belongs to the current operation. **Corrected:** correlation now runs *inside* the same `runSerializableWithRetry` transaction as the mutation it gates (previously a plain `prisma.$transaction` around an already-decided row id, with correlation read via the un-transacted module singleton) — a concurrent resend/revoke racing the same row is serialized before this read or forces a genuine PostgreSQL conflict-and-retry, mirroring the D-031 F-01 transaction-local-recheck discipline already established for authorization, applied here to a read-then-conditionally-write webhook mutation instead.

  **8. tokenHash never leaves the server.** **Correction:** every route response previously serialized the full persisted invitation record, including `tokenHash` — a one-way SHA-256 digest, not itself replayable, but excluded from every response regardless as defense-in-depth (it serves no purpose to a staff caller). `features/invitations/http.ts`'s `toInvitationResponse`/`toNullableInvitationResponse`/`toSendResultResponse` — explicit allow-lists, not destructure-and-omit, mirroring `sanitizeInvitationSnapshot`/`sanitizeAccountSnapshot`'s established pattern — now gate every response; `manualInvitationUrl` (Section 5) remains the one and only response field that ever carries a complete invitation URL.

  **9. Authorization, audit, and email normalization (unchanged from the original Stage 3 pass, reconfirmed under review).** Every mutation — prepare, send, resend, revoke, confirm-manual-sent — gates access via `canAccessClient` (ADMIN_MANAGER unconditional; TRAVEL_CONSULTANT via active assignment; every other role, including SYSTEM_ADMINISTRATOR, rejected) before opening a `runSerializableWithRetry` transaction, then re-verifies a TRAVEL_CONSULTANT actor's assignment transaction-locally as the first operation inside that transaction (D-031 F-01's exact pattern). `notFoundOrForbidden` gives ADMIN_MANAGER a genuine `CLIENT_NOT_FOUND` (404) and every other role an indistinguishable `CLIENT_FORBIDDEN` (403) for the identical unauthorized outcome — including, per a defect this review's own real-database integration test surfaced and fixed, `prepareInvitation`'s insert path, which previously let ADMIN_MANAGER's unconditional `canAccessClient` result reach a foreign-key-violating database write against a nonexistent `clientId` instead of a clean `CLIENT_NOT_FOUND`. `AuditLog` entries (`PORTAL_INVITATION_PREPARED`/`SENT_AUTOMATED`/`SENT_MANUAL_CONFIRMED`/`RESENT`/`REVOKED`) use an explicit allow-list snapshot (`sanitizeInvitationSnapshot`) that never includes the raw token, `tokenHash`, or `destinationEmail`. `createStaffUser` and `prisma/seed.ts` now call the existing `normalizeEmail` (`lib/contact-normalization.ts`, D-022 §4) as defense-in-depth before every trusted `User.email` write, per D-034 Section 8 and PR #60's follow-up gate — `Client.email` reaching `sendInvitation`/`resendInvitation` is separately normalized at the point `destinationEmail` is frozen.

- **Rationale:** Recording this as a new decision, rather than editing D-034 in place, preserves D-034 as the accurate historical record of what was originally accepted while giving the corrections found during implementation (Sections 5–9) their own durable, evidence-backed home — mirroring this repository's established pattern (D-030/D-032/D-033 recording verification/closure against an earlier design decision, never rewriting it). Each correction in Sections 5–9 was found by a specific, named verification method — a real-database integration test (Section 9's `prepareInvitation` fix), direct inspection of the installed `resend` package's compiled source and type declarations (Sections 4, 7's correlation and clearing fixes), and literal re-reading of this codebase's own established optimistic-concurrency precedent (Section 6) — never by assumption. Reusing `features/clients/repository.ts`'s `updateClientFieldsIfUnstale`/`expectedUpdatedAt` pattern for Section 6, and D-031 F-01's transaction-local-recheck pattern for Section 7's webhook fix, extends already-reviewed mechanisms rather than inventing new ones for problems this codebase has already solved once.
- **Constraint:** No further schema or migration change is authorized or implemented by this entry beyond the two files named in Section 1, both already applied only to `heritage_v3_dev`/`heritage_v3_test`. No dependency other than `resend@6.22.1` (already installed under the original Stage 3 authorization) is added. Stage 4's admin invitation-management UI and Stage 5's public activation backend/UI remain entirely unimplemented and unauthorized by this entry. `docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md`'s Phase 3 completion gate is not marked met. No real Resend account, API key, domain, or webhook secret is configured, claimed configured, or required to exist by this entry.
- **Deferred:** D-034 Stage 4 (admin Client-detail invitation-management UI); Stage 5 (public token-validation/Continue/activation backend and activation UI); Stage 6 (CLIENT login routing and `/client` overview); Stage 7 (full cross-tier verification); Stage 8 (Task Board closure). The residual manual-channel retry-friendliness gap noted during this review — a genuine same-key network-level retry of a manual resend cannot be silently absorbed as an idempotent no-op the way the automated channel's `sendOperationId` match allows, because the schema has no legal place to persist a manual operation's idempotency ticket while `deliveryState` remains `NOT_ATTEMPTED`; the `expectedUpdatedAt`/`expectedCurrentSendOperationId` precondition guarantees the retry is rejected rather than silently double-rotating, but surfaces as a 409 rather than a silent no-op — is recorded as a known limitation for a future stage to resolve, not silently worked around with an unauthorized schema change.
- **Effect:** Records, as the durable governance entry, the D-034 Stage 3 implementation actually present on `feat/phase-3-portal-invitation-service-delivery` as of this entry — the invitation repository/service/API/webhook module, the `AUTOMATED_UNCONFIRMED` schema correction, the manual-workflow and stale-resend fixes, and the webhook/response-shaping security corrections described above. Does not mark any Task Board item, Phase 3 itself, or D-034 as a whole, complete. Does not itself stage, commit, or push any file.

---

## D-036 — Portal Invitation Admin UI Implementation (D-034 Stage 4)

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Date accepted:** August 26, 2026
- **Context:** D-034 remains the historical foundation decision for the Portal Invitation / Account Activation contract; D-035 recorded D-034 Stage 3's service/delivery implementation. This entry records what D-034 Stage 4 ("admin invitation-management UI") actually implemented on `feat/phase-3-portal-invitation-admin-ui`, following a discovery-only pass and an explicit user-approved implementation contract that also authorized correcting one verified Stage 3 gap (Section 6 below) as part of this stage. `docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md`'s Phase 3 completion gate remains unmet and is not marked complete by this entry — Stage 5 (public activation) and every later stage remain unimplemented.

  **1. Component and wiring.** `apps/web/src/app/admin/clients/[id]/_components/PortalInvitationPanel.tsx` (new) renders the invitation lifecycle for a single client, alongside the existing `ClientAssignmentPanel` and `EditClientForm` on `admin/clients/[id]/page.tsx` (modified). `page.tsx` calls the same `getInvitationForClient` service function `GET /api/clients/[id]/invitation` itself calls — never a repository, never an internal fetch — and passes the result through the same `toNullableInvitationResponse` allow-list that route already uses (D-035 §8), converting every `Date`-typed field to an ISO string via a new `toInvitationViewProp` helper before handing it to the panel. The raw `InvitationRecord` — which still carries `tokenHash` — is never passed to this Client Component: Next.js serializes Client Component props into the page's own RSC payload, so doing so would leak `tokenHash` into the browser exactly as D-035 §8 closed at the API layer. Styling additions (`.invitationPanel`, `.manualLinkBox`) were added to the existing shared `apps/web/src/app/admin/clients/clients.module.css`, mirroring `.assignmentPanel`'s existing wrapper convention.

  **2. Status and delivery presentation.** The panel renders blueprint §7.1's canonical invitation-status labels (Not Invited / Invitation Prepared / Invitation Sent / Invitation Opened / Account Activated / Invitation Expired / Invitation Revoked) and separately labels delivery evidence (including `AUTOMATED_UNCONFIRMED`, D-035 §1) preserving §7.1's explicit "Sent ≠ Delivered" distinction — sent and delivered are never collapsed into one indicator. Expiry is annotated client-side against the current time for display only; the server's own status remains authoritative and is never overridden by this client-side check.

  **3. Role and authorization boundary.** Per blueprint §7.3, `ADMIN_MANAGER` and the assigned `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT` hold identical prepare/send/resend/revoke invitation authority — unlike `ClientAssignmentPanel` (D-025 §2, `ADMIN_MANAGER`-only), this panel accepts no `role` prop at all and renders identically for both allowed roles, mirroring `BookingStatusTransitionPanel`'s already-established precedent for an identically-shared-authority action. The panel performs no authorization decision of its own — every action still re-authorizes server-side via `canAccessClient` and, for `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT`, a transaction-local assignment recheck (D-031 F-01), exactly as D-035 §9 already established; the UI boundary is presentation only.

  **4. Secure one-time manualInvitationUrl handling.** A `manualInvitationUrl` returned by prepare/send/resend/confirm-manual-sent (D-035 §5) is held only in this component's transient React state — never written to `localStorage`, `sessionStorage`, or any persistent client storage, never logged to the console, and never included in any request other than the one response that produced it. The link is displayed in a read-only input with an explicit "Copy invitation link" control (`navigator.clipboard.writeText`); the on-screen copy states plainly that the link is shown only once and that a lost link requires reissuing (a fresh resend/revoke-then-reissue), matching D-035 §5's actual server behavior — prepare alone never generates or returns a token. State is cleared whenever a new send/resend attempt starts, on a successful manual-sent confirmation, and whenever the panel's `clientId` prop changes (mirroring `ClientAssignmentPanel`'s existing clientId-reset guard, extended here to also clear the manual link and copy status) — a link belonging to one client or one operation is never left displayed against a different one.

  **5. Concurrency and stale-refresh behavior.** Resend/reissue requests include `expectedCurrentSendOperationId`/`expectedUpdatedAt` (D-035 §6) read from the panel's current displayed state. A rejected `INVITATION_SEND_OPERATION_STALE` (409) locks the relevant form, shows the server's message, and offers a "Refresh and try again" control that only calls `router.refresh()` — mirroring `EditClientForm`'s existing `CLIENT_CONFLICT` recovery pattern exactly. The stale-conflict state clears itself, at render time (not inside an effect), only once the user has clicked that control *and* the parent's next `initialInvitation` prop is demonstrably different from the one that was rejected — never optimistically, and never merely because time has passed. A synchronous `pendingActionRef` (mirroring `BookingStatusTransitionPanel`'s established pattern) blocks a second submission before React commits the corresponding `useState` update, preventing an accidental double-send race the server-side idempotency key alone would otherwise have to absorb.

  **6. Corrected revoked-reissue transition (Stage 3 gap, closed here).** D-034 §3 authorizes `INVITATION_EXPIRED | INVITATION_REVOKED → INVITATION_SENT` as a legitimate reissue transition, but D-034 Stage 3 (D-035) implemented only the `INVITATION_EXPIRED` half — `resendInvitation`'s `allowedStatuses` list omitted `INVITATION_REVOKED`, found by direct inspection of `features/invitations/service.ts` during Stage 4 discovery, not by assumption. This entry authorizes and implements the correction: `resendInvitation`'s `allowedStatuses` now includes `INVITATION_REVOKED`, and only that legitimate resend/reissue transition — `prepareInvitation`'s existing duplicate-prepare protection is unchanged. The existing optimistic-concurrency `WHERE`-clause and stale-operation rejection (Section 5 above; D-035 §6) apply identically regardless of which allowed source status is being reissued from, including `INVITATION_REVOKED`. `features/invitations/repository.ts`'s `sendReservationData()` — which already unconditionally reset delivery evidence on every fresh reservation (D-035's Correction Pass) — now also resets `revokedAt` to `null` in that same atomic write: a row can only legitimately carry a non-null `revokedAt` while `status = INVITATION_REVOKED`, and a reissue reservation is, in the same write, moving `status` away from that value, so the stale revocation timestamp is cleared on the current row only; the historical `PORTAL_INVITATION_REVOKED` `AuditLog` entry is untouched, since audit rows are append-only and this clears only the current row's own field, never a past record of what happened. Token and expiration are rotated exactly as every other reissue already rotates them (D-035 §3, §6). A second gap found in the same pass — `resendInvitation` over `MANUAL_EMAIL` wrote no `AuditLog` entry at all, because the original Stage 3 audit-write condition only fired for `AUTOMATED_EMAIL` — is also closed here: `reserveSendOperation` gains a seventh parameter, `alwaysAudit` (default `false`), and `resendInvitation` now passes `true`, so every resend/reissue is audited regardless of channel, consistent with D-034 §9's channel-agnostic audit language; `sendInvitation`'s first-send call site is unchanged and still does not audit an unconfirmed first manual send, preserving D-035's original, deliberate rationale for that one case. Authorization, anti-enumeration (`notFoundOrForbidden`), the transaction-local assignment recheck, idempotency, and the audit allow-list (`sanitizeInvitationSnapshot` — never `tokenHash` or the raw token) are all unchanged and apply identically to this transition. New unit tests (`features/invitations/service.test.ts`) cover the allowed transition and its audit call, and stale-precondition rejection when reissuing from `INVITATION_REVOKED`; a pre-existing, misleadingly named unit test that never actually asserted its own claimed audit behavior was also corrected. New real-`heritage_v3_test`-database integration tests (`features/invitations/service.integration.test.ts`, using a mocked `./resend-adapter` module boundary so no real Resend account is ever contacted) cover: manual reissue from `INVITATION_REVOKED` (fresh token, cleared `revokedAt`, correct three-entry `AuditLog` sequence); automated reissue from `INVITATION_REVOKED` through the mocked provider adapter boundary; stale-precondition rejection on a delayed reissue attempt with no mutation; and authorization for both an assigned and an unassigned `TRAVEL_CONSULTANT` attempting the same reissue.

  **7. `DELIVERY_DISABLED` presentation.** When automated delivery is not configured (D-035 §4's fail-closed default), the panel surfaces the server's own error message where provided, falling back to a fixed, non-fabricated explanatory string otherwise — it never claims automated delivery is available, configured, or attempted, and never invents delivery-status detail the server did not report.

  **8. Required UI states and accessibility.** The panel handles loading (initial server-rendered prop, no client fetch on mount), empty (`initialInvitation === null`, rendered as "Not Invited" with a Prepare action), error (each action's own failure banner, distinguishing `INVITATION_SEND_OPERATION_STALE`, `CLIENT_FORBIDDEN`/`CLIENT_NOT_FOUND` — rendered as a fixed "you no longer have access" message rather than leaking a raw server error — `DELIVERY_DISABLED`, and every other error verbatim from the server), and success (per-action confirmation banners) per `.claude/rules/frontend.md`. All controls are native `button`/`input`/`select`/`textarea`/`label` elements, reachable and operable by keyboard alone; the manual link's copy confirmation and the stale-conflict message are exposed via `role="status"` for assistive-technology announcement.

  **9. Test evidence.** `PortalInvitationPanel.test.tsx` (new, 26 tests) covers every workflow (prepare, send, resend/reissue including stale-conflict recovery, confirm-manual-sent, revoke), error classification, `manualInvitationUrl` security properties (never persisted, never logged, cleared appropriately), and accessibility. `page.test.tsx` gained 5 tests covering the wiring itself, including a direct proof that a client's raw `tokenHash` never appears in the rendered page's HTML. All were run against the real `heritage_v3_test` PostgreSQL database (never mocked at the database layer) for the integration suite, and the full existing automated suite continues to pass alongside them (Section 11).

- **Rationale:** Recording this as a new decision, rather than editing D-034 or D-035 in place, preserves both as accurate historical records while giving Stage 4's own implementation, and the Stage 3 correction authorized alongside it (Section 6), their own durable, evidence-backed home — mirroring D-035's own stated rationale for the identical choice. Authorizing the `INVITATION_REVOKED` reissue correction inside this stage, rather than deferring it, closes a real, verified gap against D-034 §3's own already-accepted state machine using this stage's already-open review cycle, rather than leaving a known contract violation live in `main` for an unspecified further stage. Reusing `EditClientForm`'s `CLIENT_CONFLICT` recovery pattern, `BookingStatusTransitionPanel`'s `submittingRef`/no-`role`-prop patterns, and `ClientAssignmentPanel`'s clientId-reset guard, rather than inventing new UI patterns for the same problems, keeps this panel consistent with the admin dashboard's own established conventions (`.claude/rules/architecture.md`'s "avoid premature abstraction," applied here as "avoid premature novelty").
- **Constraint:** No `schema.prisma` change and no migration are authorized or implemented by this entry. `docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md` is not modified by this entry. No dependency is added. No existing action name, error code, entity-type constant, route, or exported function signature belonging to D-034/D-035 is renamed or removed — `reserveSendOperation`'s new `alwaysAudit` parameter is additive (defaulted, appended last) and every existing call site not named in Section 6 is unchanged. The implementation diff for this entry is exactly eleven paths: two new (`PortalInvitationPanel.tsx`, `PortalInvitationPanel.test.tsx`) and nine modified (`page.tsx`, `page.test.tsx`, `clients.module.css`, `features/invitations/service.ts`, `features/invitations/service.test.ts`, `features/invitations/service.integration.test.ts`, this entry itself, `features/invitations/repository.ts`, and `app/api/clients/[id]/invitation/resend/route.ts`). The first seven of those nine modified paths, together with the two new paths, are the nine paths of the originally approved Stage 4 inventory (this entry, `docs/HERITAGE_V3_DECISIONS_LOG.md`, was itself one of those nine, explicitly required by the approval). `features/invitations/repository.ts` was a separately approved tenth path, not part of that original nine, confirmed and approved before being made. `app/api/clients/[id]/invitation/resend/route.ts` is a separately approved eleventh path, comment-only: a Correction and Security Review Pass found its own doc comment still listed only `INVITATION_SENT`/`INVITATION_OPENED`/`INVITATION_EXPIRED` as resend's allowed source statuses, stale against this entry's own Section 6 correction; the comment was corrected to also name `INVITATION_REVOKED`, with no change to executable code, imports, or the file's existing line-ending style. Both the tenth and eleventh paths were added only after being separately confirmed and approved, per this repository's standing scope-discipline practice of stopping to confirm a genuinely required file outside an explicitly enumerated list rather than silently expanding scope.
- **Deferred:** D-034 Stage 5 (public token-validation/Continue/activation backend and activation UI); Stage 6 (CLIENT login routing and `/client` overview); Stage 7 (full cross-tier verification); Stage 8 (Task Board closure). `docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md`'s Phase 3 completion gate is not marked met by this entry.
- **Effect:** Records, as the durable governance entry, the D-034 Stage 4 implementation actually present on `feat/phase-3-portal-invitation-admin-ui` as of this entry — `PortalInvitationPanel` and its wiring into the Client detail page, and the corrected `INVITATION_REVOKED` reissue transition (including its audit fix) in `features/invitations/service.ts`/`repository.ts`. Does not mark any Task Board item, Phase 3 itself, or D-034/D-035 as a whole, complete. Does not itself stage, commit, or push any file.

---

## D-037 — Portal Invitation Public Activation Backend and Activation UI Implementation Contract (D-034 Stage 5)

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Date accepted:** August 27, 2026
- **Context:** D-034 remains the historical foundation decision for the whole Portal Invitation / Account Activation contract; D-035 recorded D-034 Stage 3's service/delivery implementation; D-036 recorded D-034 Stage 4's admin invitation-management UI implementation. This decision is the authoritative implementation contract for D-034 Stage 5 — the public token-validation GET, the explicit Continue transition, the activation POST, and the activation UI — on the basis of the Stage 5 repository discovery and security review recorded by this decision. This entry is self-contained: it is the authoritative contract for implementation, not a pointer to any external report, and every requirement and design decision it depends on is stated in full within Sections 1 through 17 below. This entry records the contract only — no application code, schema, migration, dependency, or configuration has been implemented, installed, or applied under this decision, and no Stage 5 migration has been authored or executed. `docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md`'s Phase 3 completion gate ("An invited client can activate an account, view their proposal, and respond with Accept, Decline, or Request Changes end-to-end in staging") remains unmet and is not marked complete by this entry.

  **1. Scope and status.** This entry authorizes Stage 5 of D-034 §15's eight-stage plan: the public, unauthenticated token-validation GET at `/activate/[token]`; the explicit Continue transition (`POST /api/activation/continue`); the activation transition (`POST /api/activation/activate`); and the activation UI these two routes and this one page together compose. This entry does **not** authorize D-034 Stage 6 (CLIENT-role post-login routing and the production-quality `/client` overview), Stage 7 (full cross-tier verification beyond what Section 15 of this entry requires as Stage 5's own evidence), or Stage 8 (Task Board closure of any kind). This entry accepts the **implementation contract only** — a Stage 5a, documentation-only checkpoint, mirroring this repository's own established D-034/D-035/D-036 precedent of separating contract acceptance from implementation acceptance. As of this entry's acceptance: no Stage 5 file has been created or modified, no Stage 5 migration has been authored or applied to any database, no dependency has been installed, and no test has been run under this authorization.

  **2. Governing requirements.** This contract is traced to, and implements, the following D-034 sections without reinterpreting any of them: §5 (the token-bearing GET is safe and performs no mutation; the Continue POST is the only request that may record `INVITATION_SENT → INVITATION_OPENED`; the activation POST is the only request that may reach `ACCOUNT_ACTIVATED`; every activation-flow response requires `Cache-Control: no-store` and `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`; the activation page loads no third-party script, analytics, font, or beacon; no caller-controlled post-activation return URL is accepted); §6 (activation eligibility is restricted to `status ∈ {INVITATION_SENT, INVITATION_OPENED}`; every other status is rejected with a generic, anti-enumeration-safe error indistinguishable across cases; the activation transaction's exact atomic write shape — `User`, `Account`, `ClientProfile`, `PortalInvitation` — and its collision-handling rules); §7 (the exact `emailVerified` evidence contract); §9 (the authorization/audit-action-naming contract this entry extends with a constrained anonymous-actor representation, Section 7 below); §11 (the rate-limiting requirement for the public activation flow, including its requirements that the mechanism never be process-local memory, that no raw IP address be stored unless separately justified, and that every rate-limit rejection remain generic and never disclose token validity); §14 (activation does not automatically sign the client in; a successful activation redirects to a fixed login URL carrying a non-sensitive success indicator only); and §17 (the required unit/route/component/real-PostgreSQL-integration/security/webhook/rate-limiting test evidence, and the `pnpm test`/`test:integration`/`test:e2e`/`lint`/`typecheck`/`format:check`/`build`/`git diff --check` validation contract, deferred to this entry's own later implementation sub-stages per Section 17 below).

  **3. Exact routes and responses.**

  `GET /activate/[token]` — unauthenticated. Performs a SOURCE-dimension rate-limit check (Section 11) as operational bookkeeping only, before any other work. If that check rejects, the response is HTTP 200 rendering one fixed, generic throttled-state page ("Too many attempts. Please try again later.") — never HTTP 429 (a Server Component page render has no supported mechanism to emit an arbitrary non-2xx/3xx status without introducing middleware or a proxy solely to manufacture one, which this entry declines to do for a passive read), and the request never reads or mutates `PortalInvitation`, `AuditLog`, `User`, `Account`, or `ClientProfile` once the SOURCE check has rejected it. If the SOURCE check passes, the request performs no TOKEN-dimension check at all (Section 11) and looks up the invitation by the SHA-256 digest of the presented token (Section 4). An eligible invitation (`status ∈ {INVITATION_SENT, INVITATION_OPENED}`, effective expiry not passed) renders HTTP 200 with the eligible-form UI (Section 14). Every other invitation-state cause — not found, malformed token shape, `INVITATION_PREPARED`, `INVITATION_EXPIRED` (persisted or effective-only), `INVITATION_REVOKED`, `ACCOUNT_ACTIVATED` — renders the identical HTTP 200 generic invalid-link UI ("This invitation link is no longer valid."), with a fixed link to `/login` always shown alongside it. No response from this route ever includes anything that distinguishes one ineligible cause from another. No separate public GET API route exists; the Server Component at `/activate/[token]` calls the activation service directly, server-side.

  `POST /api/activation/continue` — unauthenticated, but requires a strict same-origin request (Section 10). A successful transition, and the already-`INVITATION_OPENED` idempotent no-op alike, return HTTP 200 with exactly `{ "opened": true }` and no other field. Every ineligible invitation state returns HTTP 409 with exactly:
  ```json
  { "error": { "code": "ACTIVATION_NOT_POSSIBLE", "message": "This invitation link is no longer valid." } }
  ```
  A structurally malformed request (invalid JSON, wrong field shape, malformed token shape, an oversized body) returns HTTP 400 with the project-standard `{ "error": { "code": "VALIDATION_ERROR", "message": "The request did not pass validation.", "details": [{ "path": "token", "message": "token must be exactly 24 characters." }] } }` envelope — one `{ "path", "message" }` object per violated field, the exact shape mirroring `features/invitations/http.ts`'s existing `validationErrorResponse` — never leaking database state. An Origin-policy rejection (Section 10) returns HTTP 403 with exactly:
  ```json
  { "error": { "code": "FORBIDDEN", "message": "Request rejected." } }
  ```
  A rate-limit rejection (Section 11) returns HTTP 429 with exactly:
  ```json
  { "error": { "code": "RATE_LIMITED", "message": "Too many attempts. Please try again later." } }
  ```

  `POST /api/activation/activate` — unauthenticated, same strict same-origin requirement. A successful activation returns HTTP 200 with exactly `{ "activated": true }` and no other field — never the new `User.id`, `Account.id`, `ClientProfile.id`, `PortalInvitation.id`, the raw token, the token digest, the password, or the password hash. Every eligibility failure, every `User`-email collision (any role), and every `ClientProfile` collision or otherwise-inconsistent invitation/profile state returns the identical `ACTIVATION_NOT_POSSIBLE` 409 envelope shown above — a caller cannot distinguish any of these seven causes from one another by response shape, status code, or message text. Password length/confirmation validation failures return HTTP 400 with the same `VALIDATION_ERROR` envelope, field-level — a `details` array of `{ path: 'password', message }` objects, matching the project's existing validation-error convention, where `message` is exactly one of `'Password must be at least 12 characters.'`, `'Password must not exceed 200 characters.'`, or `'Passwords do not match.'`, whichever rule the submitted value violated — preserving whatever the caller submitted for re-display exactly as `.claude/rules/frontend.md` requires. Origin-policy and rate-limit rejections use the identical 403/429 envelopes shown above. Activation never signs the resulting `User` in automatically; success is a pure data-mutation response, and the client-side redirect to `/login?activated=1` (Section 4) is a UI action, not a server-issued session.

  **4. Token lifecycle and transport.** The raw invitation token is 24 characters over the alphabet `a-z`, `A-Z`, `0-9`, `-`, `_` (144 bits of entropy), generated by `generateInvitationToken()` in `apps/web/src/features/invitations/token.ts` (`better-auth/crypto`'s `generateRandomString`, already implemented and unchanged by this entry) and validated against the shape `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{24}$` before any lookup is attempted. The only persisted form is `hashInvitationToken(rawToken)`, the same file's existing unsalted SHA-256 hex digest, matched against `PortalInvitation.tokenHash` by equality — no new hashing primitive is introduced. `GET /activate/[token]`'s Server Component performs this lookup server-side, directly, to decide which UI state to render; it never mutates `PortalInvitation` or any other business-domain row (Section 3). The raw token is never passed to the Client Component `ActivationForm` as a serialized prop, a hidden form input, a `data-*` attribute, rendered visible text, a cookie, or any persistent browser-storage value. Instead, `ActivationForm` derives the token transiently, at the moment of each Continue/Activate action, by reading `window.location.pathname`, matching it against the exact expected shape `^/activate/([A-Za-z0-9_-]{24})$`, and capturing only that group — held in a function-local variable inside the action handler, never assigned to component state, never logged, never included in any request other than the one same-origin HTTPS JSON `POST` body it belongs to. On a successful activation, the client navigates to `/login?activated=1` using replace semantics (`window.location.replace`/the router's own `replace`, not `push`), so the token-bearing history entry is overwritten wherever the browser's own history API honors that call; no query parameter, header, or body field supplied by the caller is ever read back as a redirect target anywhere in this flow. The absence of the raw token from the rendered HTML/RSC payload is treated as an **empirical, live-response gate**, not an assumption: before this sub-stage can be considered complete, a test issuing an actual HTTP request against a running server must confirm the raw token substring does not appear anywhere in `/activate/[token]`'s response body (Section 15). If that evidence instead shows Next.js itself serializing the token into the response through some mechanism this design did not anticipate, implementation stops and that evidence is reported rather than the page shipping with the exposure.

  **5. Complete invitation state machine.** Eligibility for both Continue and Activate is `status ∈ {INVITATION_SENT, INVITATION_OPENED}` **and** `expiresAt >= now()`, evaluated fresh at the moment of each request — never from a cached prior read. Every other reachable condition is ineligible: **not found** (the presented digest matches no row — covering both a token that never existed and a token rotated out by a later resend/reissue, "superseded," which is structurally indistinguishable from never-existed since `tokenHash` is overwritten in place on resend); **malformed** (the URL segment does not match the token's shape, rejected before any lookup); `INVITATION_PREPARED` (no token has ever been issued for redemption); `INVITATION_SENT`/`INVITATION_OPENED` **with effective expiry already passed** — checked independently of the persisted `status` column, since `status` can lag `expiresAt` exactly as D-034 §4 requires; `INVITATION_EXPIRED` (persisted); `INVITATION_REVOKED`; and `ACCOUNT_ACTIVATED` (terminal). `GET /activate/[token]` never persists an `INVITATION_EXPIRED` transition merely because effective expiry has passed while rendering the page — it computes effective expiry for display purposes only. Neither `POST /api/activation/continue` nor `POST /api/activation/activate` opportunistically persists `INVITATION_EXPIRED` on a rejected request in this entry's implementation: D-034 §5 permits, but does not require, an activation POST that observes expiry to conditionally persist that transition ("may conditionally persist"), and this entry deliberately does not exercise that permitted-but-optional behavior absent a separate, explicit governing requirement compelling it — a rejected Continue or Activate transaction rolls back with **zero business-domain mutation**, full stop. Effective expiry is always calculated from `expiresAt` directly, at the moment of the check, regardless of what `status` currently reads. Resend and reissue (D-034 §4, D-035 §6, D-036 §6) are unaffected by this entry and continue to compute effective expiry from `expiresAt` independently of the stored `status` column, exactly as already implemented.

  **6. Exactly-once Continue and auditing.** This is a core correctness invariant of the Continue transition, not merely a test property. Inside one `runSerializableWithRetry` transaction, the invitation is re-fetched fresh by token digest as the first operation. If ineligible, the request is rejected per Section 5 with no update and no audit row. If `status = INVITATION_SENT`, the row is updated atomically — `status → INVITATION_OPENED`, `openedAt → now()` — and exactly one `AuditLog` row is inserted for action `PORTAL_INVITATION_OPENED`, using `actorKind: 'ANONYMOUS'` and `actorId: null` (Section 7). If `status` already reads `INVITATION_OPENED`, the request returns the identical `{ "opened": true }` success response with **no row update and no additional audit insert** — an idempotent no-op. Under concurrent Continue requests against the same invitation, whichever transaction commits first performs the transition and the single audit insert; every other concurrent request either serializes after it (its own fresh re-fetch already observes `INVITATION_OPENED` and takes the no-op branch) or hits a genuine PostgreSQL serialization conflict and retries into that same no-op branch — the result, verified directly by a real-database concurrency test (Section 15), is **exactly one** `PORTAL_INVITATION_OPENED` row ever recorded per invitation, never zero for an eligible request and never more than one regardless of how many concurrent Continue requests are issued.

  **7. Complete AuditLog actor invariant.** `AuditLog` gains a new required enum column, `actorKind AuditLogActorKind @default(USER)`, with two values: `USER` and `ANONYMOUS`. `AuditLog.actorId` changes from a required `String` to a nullable `String?`, and its relation field changes from `User` to `User?` to match (`onDelete: Restrict` unchanged). A database `CHECK` constraint enforces the relationship in both directions: `actorKind = 'USER'` requires `actorId IS NOT NULL`; `actorKind = 'ANONYMOUS'` requires `actorId IS NULL`. Every one of this repository's seven existing `insertAuditLog` implementations (`features/staff/repository.ts`, `features/proposals/repository.ts`, `features/leads/repository.ts`, `features/assignments/repository.ts`, `features/bookings/repository.ts`, `features/clients/repository.ts`, `features/invitations/repository.ts`) already passes a required, non-null `actorId` on every call; adding a `NOT NULL` column carrying a `DEFAULT 'USER'` clause to `audit_log` in PostgreSQL 11+ is a metadata-only operation that requires no table rewrite and no data backfill — every existing row becomes `actorKind = 'USER'` by the column default, which trivially satisfies the new `CHECK` constraint together with that row's own pre-existing non-null `actorId` (the column was `NOT NULL` before this change, so a read-only preflight `SELECT count(*) FROM audit_log WHERE actor_id IS NULL` is expected to return `0` before this migration is authored, mirroring this repository's established preflight-verification discipline for the case-insensitive-email migration). No production read path in this repository joins or `include`s `AuditLog.actor` anywhere — every existing read selects `actorId` as a plain scalar — so the relation becoming optional has no effect on any existing query. Six of the seven `insertAuditLog` implementations require **no code change at all**: `actorKind` is simply omitted from their `data: {}` object and defaults to `'USER'` at the database layer, and their `actorId: string` parameter remains required and unaffected. Only `features/invitations/repository.ts`'s `insertAuditLog` changes — its parameter becomes a discriminated union, `{ actorKind: 'USER'; actorId: string; action: string; entityType: string; entityId: string; beforeState?: Prisma.InputJsonValue; afterState?: Prisma.InputJsonValue } | { actorKind: 'ANONYMOUS'; action: string; entityType: string; entityId: string; beforeState?: Prisma.InputJsonValue; afterState?: Prisma.InputJsonValue }`, so that passing `actorKind: 'ANONYMOUS'` together with a defined `actorId`, or `actorKind: 'USER'` without one, is a compile-time error in addition to the database-level `CHECK` constraint — and `features/invitations/service.ts`'s four existing call sites (`prepareInvitation`, `reserveSendOperation` — shared by send/resend, `confirmManualSend`, `revokeInvitation`) each gain one additional field, `actorKind: 'USER'`, alongside their existing `actorId: actor.id` — a mechanical, non-behavioral addition with no change to any existing runtime outcome. `PORTAL_INVITATION_OPENED` (Section 6) is the sole new usage of `actorKind: 'ANONYMOUS'`, `actorId: null` in this repository. `PORTAL_INVITATION_ACTIVATED` (Section 8) uses `actorKind: 'USER'` with the newly created `CLIENT`-role `User`'s id as `actorId`, valid because that `User` row already exists, in the same transaction, before the audit insert executes — no fabricated or system-level `User` account is introduced anywhere by this entry. No `AuditLog` entry written by this entry's implementation, for either action, ever includes the raw token, the token digest, the submitted password, a raw IP address, or any other bearer credential — `PORTAL_INVITATION_OPENED`'s audit row carries no `beforeState`/`afterState` payload beyond the invitation's own already-established sanitized-snapshot shape, and `PORTAL_INVITATION_ACTIVATED`'s likewise never includes password material.

  Illustrative migration shape (not authored or applied by this entry — Section 16):
  ```sql
  CREATE TYPE "AuditLogActorKind" AS ENUM ('USER', 'ANONYMOUS');

  ALTER TABLE "audit_log"
    ADD COLUMN "actorKind" "AuditLogActorKind" NOT NULL DEFAULT 'USER';

  ALTER TABLE "audit_log"
    ALTER COLUMN "actorId" DROP NOT NULL;

  ALTER TABLE "audit_log" ADD CONSTRAINT "audit_log_actor_kind_actor_id_consistency"
    CHECK (
      ("actorKind" = 'USER' AND "actorId" IS NOT NULL)
      OR
      ("actorKind" = 'ANONYMOUS' AND "actorId" IS NULL)
    );
  ```
  A brand-new `CREATE TYPE` is safe to reference within the same migration transaction that creates it (unlike `ALTER TYPE` adding a new value to an *existing* enum, which this repository's own `20260826090000`/`20260826090001` migration pair already established requires a two-file split) — this change is authored as one file.

  **8. Complete activation transaction.** In exact order: (a) the Origin/media-type/body-size/rate-limit gates of Sections 10–11 run first, all before any database access beyond the rate-limit table itself; (b) request and password fields are schema-validated; (c) the password is hashed via `hashPassword` from `better-auth/crypto` — the same primitive `features/staff/repository.ts`'s `createStaffUser` already uses — **outside** the `runSerializableWithRetry` transaction, so this deliberately slow, CPU-bound step does not extend the transaction's wall-clock duration or increase serialization-conflict/retry pressure; (d) one `runSerializableWithRetry` transaction opens, and as its first operation re-fetches the invitation fresh by token digest and re-evaluates the full eligibility check of Section 5 against that fresh read — never against any state observed before step (c);
