(e) a normalized-email collision check (`prisma.user.findUnique({ where: { email: invitation.destinationEmail } })`, `destinationEmail` already normalized at freeze time per D-034 §6/§8) runs inside the same transaction against **any** existing `User` row regardless of role — a match is rejected with the generic `ACTIVATION_NOT_POSSIBLE` envelope, reported diagnostically only through server-side channels never reaching the unauthenticated caller, per D-034 §6; (f) a `ClientProfile` consistency check confirms no existing or otherwise-inconsistent `ClientProfile` already exists for this invitation's `Client`, rejected the same way on conflict; (g) a new `User` is created with `role: 'CLIENT'`, `name` from the `Client.fullName`, and `email` from the invitation's own frozen `destinationEmail` — never from any client-supplied input, since the activation request accepts no email field at all; `emailVerified` is set per the exact matrix in Section 9; (h) a new `Account` is created with `providerId: 'credential'` and the password hash from step (c), mirroring `createStaffUser`'s established `user.create` + nested `accounts.create` pattern exactly; (i) a new `ClientProfile` is created linking the new `User.id` and the invitation's `Client.id`; (j) no `Client` field, including `Client.email`, is ever modified by this transaction; (k) `PortalInvitation` transitions `status → ACCOUNT_ACTIVATED` with `activatedAt → now()`; (l) an `AuditLog` row for `PORTAL_INVITATION_ACTIVATED` is inserted **after** step (g), since its `actorId` foreign key requires the new `User` row to already exist in the same transaction (Section 7). Any failure at (d) through (f) rolls the entire transaction back atomically with no partial write of any kind, and the password hash computed in step (c) is simply discarded — harmless, since it was never logged or persisted outside this attempt. If two concurrent activation requests race past step (d)'s pre-checks (a narrow window a `SERIALIZABLE` transaction closes for same-row races, but not necessarily for a same-email-different-invitation edge case), the database's own unique constraints (`user_email_key`, `user_email_lower_key`, `client_profile_userId_key`, `client_profile_clientId_key`) remain the final, authoritative backstop — a caught unique-violation at insert time is translated to the identical generic `ACTIVATION_NOT_POSSIBLE` response, never a raw database error reaching the caller. Every failure mode in this section — ineligibility, expiry, revocation, prior activation, email collision, profile collision — produces the same response the caller cannot distinguish from any other (Section 3).

  **9. Complete emailVerified matrix.** `emailVerified` on the activation-created `User` is computed as a pure function of `PortalInvitation.deliveryMethod` and `PortalInvitation.deliveryState`, read fresh inside the same transaction as Section 8's eligibility check, per this exact, exhaustive table:

  | `deliveryMethod` | `deliveryState` | `emailVerified` | Evidence |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | `AUTOMATED_EMAIL` | `AUTOMATED_ACCEPTED` | **true** | Resend positively accepted the send to the frozen `destinationEmail` (D-034 §2(b), D-035 §4) |
  | `AUTOMATED_EMAIL` | `PROVIDER_DELIVERED` | **true** | Provider confirmed delivery — stronger evidence than acceptance alone |
  | `AUTOMATED_EMAIL` | `AUTOMATED_UNCONFIRMED` | false | Provider outcome genuinely unknown (D-035 §1/§4) — never treated as proof of anything |
  | `AUTOMATED_EMAIL` | `PROVIDER_FAILED` | false | Definite provider rejection |
  | `AUTOMATED_EMAIL` | `PROVIDER_BOUNCED` | false | Message did not reach the mailbox |
  | `AUTOMATED_EMAIL` | `PROVIDER_COMPLAINED` | false | Recipient marked as spam — no proof of legitimate receipt |
  | `AUTOMATED_EMAIL` | `PROVIDER_SUPPRESSED` | false | Provider suppressed the send |
  | `MANUAL_EMAIL` | `MANUALLY_CONFIRMED` | **true** | Staff explicitly confirmed sending; the existing `portal_invitation_manual_confirmation_evidence` `CHECK` constraint already guarantees `deliveryConfirmedAt`/`deliveryConfirmedByStaffId` are both non-null whenever this state holds |
  | `MANUAL_EMAIL` | `NOT_ATTEMPTED` | false | A genuinely reachable state, not defensive-only: a staff member can reserve a manual link — which already sets `status = INVITATION_SENT` per D-035 §5 — without yet confirming it was sent. A client activating from that link before staff confirms it is eligibility-permitted (Section 5) but has no delivery evidence at all, correctly `false`; this is an intentional, independent interaction between eligibility (status-gated) and verification evidence (delivery-state-gated), not a defect |
  | `null` | `NOT_ATTEMPTED` | false | No send attempt recorded at all |

  Because activation collects no email field from the caller at all — `User.email` is always the invitation's own frozen `destinationEmail` (Section 8, step (g)) — D-034 §7's "activation-collected email differing from the recorded destination" disqualifying clause is structurally unreachable under this implementation and requires no separate handling: the matrix above is a complete function of the invitation row's own stored delivery evidence, nothing else.

  **10. Origin, media type, and bounded-body processing.** Both POST routes process a request in this exact order, with no step reordered: (1) require an `Origin` header and normalize the application's expected value via `new URL(getServerEnv().BETTER_AUTH_URL).origin`; compare it against the incoming header, itself parsed the same way (`new URL(originHeader).origin`); (2) a missing, malformed (fails to parse as a URL), or mismatched `Origin` returns the identical generic 403 envelope from Section 3, before any database or rate-limit table access of any kind; (3) `Content-Type` is accepted case-insensitively as `application/json`, with either no parameter or exactly one `charset` parameter (e.g. `application/json; charset=utf-8`); any other media type, or any other/malformed parameter, is rejected with the `VALIDATION_ERROR` 400 envelope; (4) a declared `Content-Length` that is malformed, negative, internally conflicting, non-numeric, or greater than 4096 bytes is rejected with the same 400 envelope, without reading any body bytes; (5) the request source is resolved and the SOURCE-dimension rate-limit check/increment (Section 11) runs **before the body is read at all**; (6) the body is then read via a manual byte-counting stream reader enforcing an actual 4096-byte cap, effective regardless of whether `Content-Length` was present, absent, understated, or the request used chunked transfer encoding — the read is aborted and the request rejected (400) the instant cumulative bytes exceed the cap; (7) the body is parsed as JSON and structurally validated (Zod); (8) the token digest is computed from the parsed token field and the TOKEN-dimension rate-limit check/increment (Section 11) runs; (9) password length/confirmation is validated (Activate only); (10) the password is hashed outside the business transaction (Section 8, step (c)); (11) the `SERIALIZABLE` business transaction begins and re-checks all authoritative state fresh (Section 8, step (d) onward). The 4096-byte cap is sized for one 24-character token plus two passwords capped at 200 characters each, with ample headroom for JSON structural overhead. Every rate-limit read/write in this contract uses Prisma's own parameterized query builder (Section 11) — no `$queryRawUnsafe` call, and no string-concatenated SQL of any kind, is authorized anywhere in this implementation.

  **11. PostgreSQL rate limiting.** Implemented as a feature-local module, `apps/web/src/features/activation/rate-limit.ts` — not placed in `apps/web/src/lib/` for this stage, per `.claude/rules/architecture.md`'s rule against introducing a shared abstraction until at least two concrete features need it; a later login-rate-limiting task may extract this mechanism into shared infrastructure once a second concrete feature genuinely needs it. Schema:
  ```prisma
  enum RateLimitDimension { SOURCE  TOKEN }

  model RateLimitBucket {
    id          String              @id
    dimension   RateLimitDimension
    bucketKey   String
    windowStart DateTime
    count       Int                 @default(0)
    createdAt   DateTime            @default(now())
    updatedAt   DateTime            @updatedAt

    @@unique([dimension, bucketKey, windowStart])
    @@index([windowStart])
    @@map("rate_limit_bucket")
  }
  ```
  Two dimensions plus one shared fallback bucket: **SOURCE**, keyed by `HMAC-SHA256(ACTIVATION_RATE_LIMIT_HMAC_SECRET, resolvedSourceIdentifier)` — a new, required, server-only, purpose-specific environment secret (Section 16), at least 32 characters, never `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET` reused for this or any other second purpose, per `docs/HERITAGE_V3_ENVIRONMENT_CONFIGURATION.md` §7.1's "a secret is never reused across two different purposes"; **TOKEN**, keyed by the same SHA-256 token digest already computed for the invitation lookup (Section 4) — no separate hash, and computed whether or not the presented token resolves to a real invitation; and the literal shared bucket key `"unknown-source"`, used whenever the request's source cannot be resolved as trusted (Section 12). No raw IP address is ever stored, and no unkeyed digest of an IP address is ever computed — every source identifier is HMAC-keyed with the dedicated secret before it is used as a `bucketKey`.

  Atomic increment uses Prisma's `upsert()` against the table's own compound-unique index:
  ```ts
  const updated = await prisma.rateLimitBucket.upsert({
    where: { dimension_bucketKey_windowStart: { dimension, bucketKey, windowStart } },
    create: { id: randomUUID(), dimension, bucketKey, windowStart, count: 1 },
    update: { count: { increment: 1 } },
    select: { count: true },
  });
  const limited = updated.count > limit;
  ```
  A request is rejected exactly when the **post-increment** count exceeds the configured limit — the request that pushes the count from `limit` to `limit + 1` is itself the first one rejected. This is documented as the intended atomic mechanism, but Prisma's `upsert()` compiling to a single atomic database-level statement using PostgreSQL's `INSERT` with an `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` clause is conditional on Prisma's own documented per-version, per-database-provider behavior for a matching compound-unique constraint — it is **not treated as a settled fact of this contract**. Before this mechanism may be relied upon, implementation must produce direct evidence, against the actually-installed Prisma version, that this exact compound-unique shape produces one atomic upsert, together with a concurrent-increment test proving no lost updates under simultaneous requests to the same bucket key. If that evidence fails, implementation stops for separate review rather than proceeding on an unverified assumption about Prisma's compiled behavior; a possible reviewed fallback is a parameterized tagged-template `$queryRaw` statement issuing PostgreSQL's `INSERT` with an `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` clause and a `RETURNING count` clause, since Prisma's `$executeRaw` returns only the number of affected rows and cannot return the incremented `count` value a `RETURNING` clause produces — `$queryRawUnsafe`, `$executeRawUnsafe`, string concatenation, and interpolating identifiers or request-controlled values into any raw SQL statement remain forbidden regardless of which mechanism is ultimately selected, and naming this fallback here does not itself pre-authorize its implementation.

  Ordering: the SOURCE-dimension check always runs, and is always resolved, before any TOKEN-dimension check or row is created (Section 10, steps 5 and 8) — this bounds how many TOKEN-bucket rows one abusive source can ever create, since every further request from an already-limited source is rejected at the SOURCE step before a TOKEN row is ever touched. A distributed attacker spread across many distinct sources, each individually under its own SOURCE limit, presenting one distinct random token each, will still create one TOKEN-bucket row per distinct token — an acknowledged, bounded-not-eliminated residual, closed only by the retention policy below, not by the ordering itself.

  Initial, configurable limits: **SOURCE — 30 requests per 15-minute fixed window**; **TOKEN — 10 requests per 60-minute fixed window**; **unknown-source shared bucket — 30 requests per 15-minute fixed window**, numerically identical to SOURCE but tracked as one bucket shared across all traffic whose source cannot be resolved as trusted. A fixed-window design (rather than a sliding-window log) is chosen deliberately for its bounded row growth; the accepted limitation is that a burst straddling a window boundary can admit up to roughly twice the nominal limit in a short span — recorded here as an accepted trade-off, not an oversight. Rotating `ACTIVATION_RATE_LIMIT_HMAC_SECRET` changes every future `bucketKey` derivation, so every pre-rotation SOURCE bucket becomes permanently orphaned (harmless — it simply ages out via cleanup) and every source's effective history resets to zero — an intentional, expected side effect of rotation.

  The TOKEN-dimension limit is described accurately: it limits **repeated consumption attempts, automated replay, malformed-password churn against one token, abusive repeated state probes, and resource consumption tied to a single presented credential** — it does **not** protect a disclosed, valid, unredeemed bearer token from succeeding on its first attempt. Activation sets a brand-new password; it never verifies an existing one, so possession of a valid token is sufficient for activation regardless of any request-count limit applied to it. Token throttling is a resource/abuse control, not a compensating control for bearer-token disclosure — the actual mitigations for that risk are the token's own 7-day validity window, single-use redemption, and delivery-channel security (D-034 §2), not this limiter.

  Retention: rows are eligible for deletion once `windowStart` is more than 24 hours in the past — far beyond either window's own length, so a row is never deleted while it could still be receiving increments. Cleanup is attempted probabilistically on a small fraction of requests via `shouldRunCleanup(random: () => number = Math.random): boolean { return random() < CLEANUP_PROBABILITY }`, a function tests exercise deterministically by injecting a fixed `random` implementation rather than depending on true randomness. A cleanup attempt runs in its own `try`/`catch`, strictly **after** the request's own rate-limit decision has already been computed — a cleanup failure is caught and never allowed to alter, delay, or roll back that decision. On failure, the catch block logs a single fixed, sanitized string (e.g. `'[rate-limit] cleanup failed'`) and nothing else — it never serializes the caught Prisma error object and never logs a `bucketKey`, since a `bucketKey` is HMAC or token-digest output that this contract minimizes exposure of even though it is not itself raw secret material.

  **12. Source trust and deployment gates.** The request-source resolver is default-deny:
  ```ts
  export type ResolvedSource = { trusted: true; identifier: string } | { trusted: false };
  export type SourceTrustConfig = { trustedHeaderName?: string; trustedProxyHopCount?: number };
  export function resolveRequestSource(request: Request, trustConfig: SourceTrustConfig): ResolvedSource;
  ```
  `X-Forwarded-For`, `X-Real-IP`, and every other proxy-supplied header are ignored unconditionally unless `trustConfig.trustedHeaderName` is explicitly set — which it is not, in any environment, under this entry's authorization. With no trusted header configured, `resolveRequestSource` returns `{ trusted: false }` for every request, and callers fall back to the shared `"unknown-source"` bucket (Section 11). `trustConfig` is caller-supplied, never derived from `process.env.NODE_ENV` or any value the incoming request itself controls, so test code exercises the trusted branch by passing an explicit permissive configuration directly — a simulated attacker's request headers can never flip this trust in either test or production. Once a verified trusted-header/hop configuration exists (not authorized by this entry), the resolved identifier is normalized before HMAC derivation: lowercased; an IPv6 zone identifier is stripped (`fe80::1%eth0 → fe80::1`); an IPv4-mapped IPv6 form is collapsed to its IPv4 equivalent (`::ffff:203.0.113.7 → 203.0.113.7`). Before that future trust configuration is enabled in production, the operator must also explicitly consider IPv6 address rotation and prefix aggregation (a single client can present many distinct IPv6 addresses over time, and many distinct clients can share a provider-aggregated prefix) as a factor in choosing the granularity of the trusted identifier — not resolved by this entry, named here so it is not silently assumed away later.

  Two items are recorded as **deployment gates on enabling activation in staging or production**, not as unresolved application-design decisions this entry leaves open: (a) verified Hostinger reverse-proxy behavior — which header, if any, reliably and non-spoofably carries the true client source, how many proxy hops precede the application, and whether a request can bypass the proxy and reach the origin directly with a forged header; and (b) verified Hostinger access-log behavior for the `/activate/*` path — whether the full request URL (including the raw token) is captured, its retention period, who can access it, and whether path-specific redaction is available. Until both are verified and documented, `trustConfig` remains unset in every environment, meaning **all activation traffic shares the single 30-per-15-minute unknown-source bucket** — an explicit, acknowledged, application-wide throttling exposure if real traffic volume ever approaches that shared budget — and the raw token's exposure to infrastructure-layer logging outside this application's control remains unverified. This implementation may be merged with both gates still open; activation must not be exposed in staging or production until both are resolved.

  **13. Headers, caching, and inherited resources.** `apps/web/src/app/activate/[token]/page.tsx` declares `export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic';`, the Next.js App Router route-segment-config export that prevents this route from being statically prerendered or cached at build/ISR time, on the installed Next.js `16.2.10`. `apps/web/next.config.ts` gains a scoped `async headers()` entry matching `/activate/:token*`, setting `Cache-Control: no-store` and `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` — the mechanism this route requires, since `next/headers`'s `headers()` function is read-only for an incoming request and cannot set outgoing headers from within a Server Component; a page has no other framework-native way to set response headers short of introducing `middleware.ts`, which this entry does not authorize for this single-route need. `POST /api/activation/continue` and `POST /api/activation/activate`, being Route Handlers, set the identical two headers directly on their returned `NextResponse`. Because a dynamically-rendered Next.js response can carry a composite `Cache-Control` value rather than the single literal string `"no-store"`, the required live-response test (Section 15) parses the header's comma-separated directives case-insensitively and asserts: `no-store` is present; `public` is absent; no directive named `s-maxage` is present; no directive assigns `max-age` a positive numeric value; and `Referrer-Policy` equals exactly `no-referrer`. `/activate/[token]` is a top-level route, structurally unreachable from `apps/web/src/app/admin/layout.tsx` or any `dashboard`-scoped layout, and inherits only `apps/web/src/app/layout.tsx` and its imported `globals.css` — both confirmed, by direct inspection, to contain no script tag, no `next/font` usage, no analytics snippet, no third-party embed, and (in `globals.css`) no `@import` or remote font URL, only a `system-ui` font stack. No `instrumentation.ts`/`instrumentation-client.ts` exists in this repository. This confirms the complete inherited resource chain for this route is free of third-party scripts, fonts, analytics, and beacons, without requiring any new restriction beyond what already exists.

  **14. Complete UI and accessibility contract.** The activation surface handles every state required by `.claude/rules/frontend.md`: **passive generic invalid** (any of Section 5's ineligible causes reached via GET or a failed Continue submission, or — reachable only through a failed Activate submission, since Continue never creates an identity and cannot surface either collision — Section 8's two collision causes; one fixed message, "This invitation link is no longer valid.," with a fixed `/login` link, never distinguishing cause); **throttled** (Section 3's GET-side rate-limited state — one fixed message, "Too many attempts. Please try again later.," rendered with no structural difference from the invalid state beyond copy text, so neither state leaks which condition occurred); **eligible, not yet continued** (a native `<button>` for Continue — never auto-fired on page load, per D-034 §5); **eligible, continued** (a native `<form>` with `password`/`confirmPassword` `<input type="password" autoComplete="new-password">` fields, each with an associated `<label>`); **submitting** (a synchronous client-side pending-ref guard, mirroring `PortalInvitationPanel`'s established `pendingActionRef` pattern, disabling the submit control — backstopped, not replaced, by the server-side transactional recheck of Section 8); **password validation failure** (field-level messages per `.claude/rules/frontend.md`, submitted values preserved and never cleared); **terminal generic failure** (a 409 from Continue, always for one of Section 5's ineligible causes, or from Activate, for one of those same causes or one of Section 8's two collision causes — every case rendering the identical fixed message the passive state uses, so no additional detail leaks through a failed submission either); and **success** (redirect to `/login?activated=1` using replace semantics, Section 4, never an automatic sign-in). Every control is a native, keyboard-operable HTML element (`button`/`input`/`label`/`form`) with a visible focus state; asynchronous confirmations and the terminal/throttled states use `role="status"`/`role="alert"` as appropriate, mirroring `PortalInvitationPanel`'s existing precedent for exactly this purpose.

  **15. Complete tests and artifact controls.** **Unit:** the eligibility predicate across every status/expiry combination in Section 5; the `emailVerified` function against every row of Section 9's matrix; `resolveRequestSource`'s default-deny and injected-trust-config paths; the IPv4/IPv6 normalization function's edge cases; `shouldRunCleanup` with injected determinism; the Cache-Control composite-directive parser of Section 13; the `AuditLogActorKind` discriminated-union parameter, asserting `actorId` is `null` if and only if `actorKind` is `'ANONYMOUS'`. **Route:** every status code of Section 3 with the service layer mocked, including Origin present/absent/malformed/mismatched, every accepted and rejected `Content-Type` variant, oversized declared and actual body sizes. **Component:** every UI state of Section 14, including a direct assertion that the raw token never appears in `ActivationForm`'s rendered output or component state. **Real-`heritage_v3_test`-PostgreSQL integration:** the full prepare→send→continue→activate path over both `AUTOMATED_EMAIL` and `MANUAL_EMAIL`; every ineligible status rejected identically for both Continue and Activate; both of Activate's own collision causes (any-role `User`-email collision, `ClientProfile` collision — never reachable through Continue, which creates no identity); every row of Section 9's `emailVerified` matrix; expired-by-time-not-status rejection; activation racing a concurrent resend and a concurrent revoke on the same invitation, proving the transaction-local recheck of Section 8 rejects cleanly in both cases; the exact one-row `PORTAL_INVITATION_OPENED` audit-count invariant of Section 6 under both a single and a concurrent-request Continue call; a direct query proving `GET` performs no `User`/`ClientProfile` read once the SOURCE rate limiter has rejected it. **Concurrency:** N simultaneous Continue calls against one invitation yield exactly one transition and one audit row; N simultaneous Activate calls with the same token yield exactly one success and no duplicate `User`/`Account`/`ClientProfile`; simultaneous rate-limit increments against the same bucket key produce no lost updates (the concurrent-increment evidence Section 11 requires before Prisma's `upsert()` may be relied upon). **Rate-limit:** SOURCE-before-TOKEN ordering (Section 10); unknown-source shared-bucket saturation; a cleanup failure (forced via a mocked failing delete) leaves the request's own 200/409/429 outcome unchanged; cleanup never removes a row inside its own live window. **Security:** the raw token and the submitted password are absent from every response body, log call, thrown error message, and `AuditLog` row, across every surface. **Live E2E (real HTTP, not jsdom):** the Cache-Control/Referrer-Policy assertions of Section 13, issued against a running server for all three surfaces; a direct fetch of `/activate/[token]`'s rendered HTML/RSC response asserting the raw token substring is absent (Section 4's empirical gate); the full browser-driven happy path.

  Artifact retention for token-bearing tests: trace, screenshot, and video capture are disabled specifically for the activation E2E spec (`test.use({ trace: 'off', screenshot: 'off', video: 'off' })`), stricter than this repository's existing global Playwright configuration, mirroring the same reasoning D-033 §8 already applied to disabling trace project-wide — no retention policy for these artifacts can be made consistent with never persisting a captured secret, so none are retained for this spec unless a verified redaction process is introduced first, which this entry does not authorize. Assertions in this spec never construct an equality check whose default failure output would print the raw token (e.g., never `expect(url).toBe(fullUrlWithToken)`); they assert via a boolean predicate instead, so a failure's default diff output contains only `true`/`false`.

  A dedicated, deliberate-failure verification is required to prove this policy holds, but it is **not** a committed, ordinarily-failing Playwright test — this repository's own `pnpm --filter web run test:e2e` must finish successfully on every normal run. Instead, a separate, controlled verification harness (its own script, invoked by its own `pnpm` command, distinct from `test:e2e`) performs the proof: it spawns a dedicated, tag-isolated "expected-failure probe" test as a child process (excluded from the normal `test:e2e` run via a Playwright grep-tag exclusion), expects and validates that the child process exits **non-zero** — treating a zero exit as the harness's own failure, since that would mean the probe did not actually fail as designed and the proof is invalid — captures the child's stdout and stderr, scans every artifact directory the run produced, confirms the raw token substring used by the probe is absent from all of it, and finally deletes or isolates the probe's own temporary output so this verification run leaves nothing sensitive behind. The harness reports success only when both conditions hold: the child failed as expected, and no token was found anywhere scanned. If Playwright's own failure message or reporter output is found to embed the navigation URL in a way this harness cannot sanitize or avoid retaining, implementation stops and that evidence is reported rather than this requirement being weakened to accommodate it.

  **16. Complete file, migration, environment, and validation inventory.** New: `apps/web/src/features/activation/{schemas,errors,repository,service,http,rate-limit,source}.ts` and matching `*.test.ts`/`*.integration.test.ts`; `apps/web/src/app/activate/[token]/page.tsx` and `page.test.tsx`; `apps/web/src/app/activate/[token]/_components/ActivationForm.tsx` and its test; `apps/web/src/app/api/activation/continue/route.ts` and `route.test.ts`; `apps/web/src/app/api/activation/activate/route.ts` and `route.test.ts`; `apps/web/e2e/activation.spec.ts`; the tag-isolated expected-failure probe spec and its verification-harness script (Section 15); two new migration directories under `apps/web/prisma/migrations/`, timestamped at authoring time in a later sub-stage — one for the `AuditLogActorKind`/`actorKind`/nullable-`actorId` change (Section 7), one for the `RateLimitDimension`/`RateLimitBucket` table (Section 11) — two separately justified, separately reviewed schema areas, never bundled into one file. Modified: `apps/web/prisma/schema.prisma` (both new enums/models, `AuditLog`'s two field changes); `apps/web/src/features/invitations/repository.ts` (`findInvitationByTokenHash`, `insertAuditLog`'s discriminated-union signature); `apps/web/src/features/invitations/service.ts` (four call sites gain `actorKind: 'USER'`); `apps/web/src/lib/env.ts` (`ACTIVATION_RATE_LIMIT_HMAC_SECRET: z.string().min(32, 'ACTIVATION_RATE_LIMIT_HMAC_SECRET must be at least 32 characters')`, required, no default); `apps/web/.env.example` (the new variable documented, no real value, generation instruction, an explicit comment that it must never reuse `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET`); `apps/web/next.config.ts` (the `headers()` matcher of Section 13); `apps/web/src/app/login/page.tsx` and its test (a non-sensitive `?activated=1` success banner, D-034 §14); `apps/web/e2e/support/run-e2e.ts` and `apps/web/playwright.config.ts` (threading and validating the new secret for the isolated E2E server, mirroring `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET`'s existing handling exactly); `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (the `Build` step's `env:` block gains one new, textually distinct, non-secret placeholder line for the new variable); `docs/HERITAGE_V3_ENVIRONMENT_CONFIGURATION.md` (the new variable's purpose and required-in-every-environment status documented, per `.claude/rules/validation-deployment.md`'s "Environment Variables" rule); this entry, `docs/HERITAGE_V3_DECISIONS_LOG.md`. No new dependency is required anywhere in this contract — HMAC derivation uses `node:crypto`'s built-in `createHmac`, atomic increments use Prisma's existing query builder, password hashing reuses the already-installed `better-auth/crypto`. Migration authoring and execution are explicitly **not** part of this entry (Stage 5a) — they belong to a later sub-stage (Section 17). `docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md` is not modified by this entry and remains unchanged until Stage 8, per D-034 §15's own established stage boundary.

  **17. Guarded substages and branch.** Stage 5 proceeds through its own separately reviewed sub-stages: **5a** — this entry, the documentation-only implementation contract (complete as of this entry's acceptance); **5b** — authoring and applying the two migrations of Section 7/Section 11, reviewed against current `heritage_v3_dev`/`heritage_v3_test` data shape, applied only to those two disposable/local databases, never staging or production; **5c** — the core activation implementation (the `features/activation/` module minus rate limiting, both routes, the page and form, the `login/page.tsx` banner, the two `features/invitations/` adjustments); **5d** — rate limiting (`rate-limit.ts`, `source.ts`, wired into both POST routes and the GET page's throttled state, plus the Origin/media-type/body-cap policy in `http.ts`); **5e** — the required live-HTTP evidence gates that cannot be satisfied by assumption: Section 13's real Cache-Control/Referrer-Policy assertions, Section 4's live token-absence gate, Section 11's Prisma-`upsert()`-atomicity evidence, and Section 15's artifact-safety verification harness; **5f** — a Correction and Security Review Pass, mirroring this repository's established practice for D-034 Stages 3 and 4, before any staging, commit, or pull request. Independently of progress through 5a–5f, activation is not exposed in staging or production until both deployment gates of Section 12 are verified and documented. Branch: `feat/phase-3-portal-invitation-account-activation`, matching this repository's existing naming convention (`feat/phase-3-portal-invitation-service-delivery`, `feat/phase-3-portal-invitation-admin-ui`).

- **Rationale:** Recording this as a new decision, rather than editing D-034, D-035, or D-036 in place, preserves each as an accurate historical record while giving Stage 5's own contract its own durable, evidence-backed home — mirroring D-035's and D-036's own stated rationale for the identical choice. Introducing a constrained, database-enforced anonymous-actor representation for `AuditLog` (Section 7), rather than omitting the `PORTAL_INVITATION_OPENED` audit event, fabricating a system `User`, or merely making `actorId` nullable without a replacement invariant, closes a genuine gap between D-034 §9's audit-action list and `AuditLog.actorId`'s prior non-nullable shape without weakening the audit trail's own integrity — the new `CHECK` constraint makes the `USER`/`ANONYMOUS` distinction structurally enforced, not merely a convention. Keeping the passive `GET`'s invalid-link and throttled states fully generic, and confining collision detection to the activation transaction alone (Section 8), directly implements D-034 §6's anti-enumeration requirement without inventing a check the governing contract never asked the passive read to perform. Choosing a feature-local rate limiter, a required and purpose-specific HMAC secret, and a PostgreSQL-backed, source-before-token-ordered, retention-bounded design fulfills D-034 §11 without introducing a new infrastructure dependency this repository's Hostinger-VPS deployment target does not already have, while treating Prisma's own atomicity behavior as something to verify rather than assume, consistent with this repository's "never assume, verify" practice already established across D-030 through D-036. Deferring both the Hostinger source-trust and access-log questions to explicit deployment gates, rather than resolving them by assumption, keeps this contract honest about what it can and cannot certify from repository inspection alone.
- **Constraint:** No `schema.prisma` change, no migration, no dependency installation, and no database mutation of any kind is authorized or performed by this entry — Sections 7 and 11 describe the *direction* two later, separately reviewed migrations must take, not migrations this entry creates or applies. `docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md` is not modified by this entry. No existing action name, error code, entity-type constant, route, or exported function signature belonging to D-034, D-035, or D-036 is renamed or removed by this entry — every change named in Section 16 is additive to the existing invitation module, and the six unaffected `insertAuditLog` implementations named in Section 7 require no code change at all. The implementation diff for this entry is exactly one path: `docs/HERITAGE_V3_DECISIONS_LOG.md` itself.
- **Deferred:** Every file, migration, and test named in Sections 7, 11, and 15 (Stage 5b through 5f, Section 17); D-034 Stage 6 (CLIENT login routing and the production-quality `/client` overview); Stage 7 (full cross-tier verification beyond Stage 5's own evidence); Stage 8 (Task Board closure of any kind). The two deployment gates of Section 12 remain open and are not resolved by this entry.
- **Effect:** Authorizes Stage 5b through Stage 5f implementation of exactly the contract described above, each under its own separately reviewed guarded checkpoint and file-scope authorization, per Section 17. Does not itself implement any file, install any dependency, apply any migration, or run any test, and does not mark any Task Board item, Phase 3 itself, or D-034 as a whole, complete.

## D-038 — Fragment-Based Portal Activation Token Transport Security Remediation

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Date accepted:** September 1, 2026
- **Context:** D-034 Stage 5's own required live-HTTP evidence gate (D-037 Section 17, Stage 5e) — a real HTTP request against a running server, asserting the raw invitation token is absent from `/activate/[token]`'s actual response body — was implemented and run for the first time during Stage 5e (`apps/web/e2e/activation.spec.ts`, on branch `feat/phase-3-portal-activation-live-evidence`, merged through PR #64/#65/#66 up to `main`@`cb2f420`). That test failed, reproducibly, against the already-merged D-034 Stage 5c page implementation. This entry is the authoritative security-remediation decision responding to that finding. It is self-contained: every requirement it depends on is stated in full below. This entry records the remediation contract only — no application code, route, component, test, or configuration is implemented, moved, deleted, or modified by this entry, and no migration is authored or applied. D-034 Stage 5e's own genuine, currently-failing token-absence assertion remains exactly as committed, unweakened, as this remediation's own before/after security evidence. D-034 Stage 5 as a whole remains **FAIL** until this remediation is implemented (D-038b/c) and that assertion passes for real.

  **1. Context and verified defect.** D-034 Stage 5e's live-response test issued a real, unauthenticated `GET` request against a running server for a genuine, disposable `heritage_v3_test` invitation's real `/activate/[token]` URL and inspected the actual HTTP response body. The raw token was found present, verbatim, inside an inline `self.__next_f.push([...])` script — Next.js App Router's own RSC/Flight hydration payload. Direct inspection of the installed `next@16.2.10` server source (`node_modules/next/dist/server/app-render/app-render.js` and `create-flight-router-state-from-loader-tree.js`, `node_modules/next/dist/shared/lib/segment.js`) confirmed the exact, unconditional mechanism: `prepareInitialCanonicalUrl(url)` seeds the client router's initial state from `(url.pathname + url.search)`; `createFlightRouterStateFromLoaderTreeImpl` resolves a dynamic route segment's *actual current value* via `getDynamicParamFromSegment` and embeds it directly into the serialized `FlightRouterState` tree; `addSearchParamsIfPageSegment` separately `JSON.stringify`s and embeds any non-empty query string into the page's own segment key. All three run as core App Router routing/rendering infrastructure, before the page component itself is invoked, keyed off the route's file-system-defined segment structure and the resolved request URL — never gated by any page-level export or documented configuration. Direct evidence, not speculation, therefore established: converting `page.tsx` to a Client Component does not avoid this (the mechanism operates on the loader tree and resolved param above and independent of the leaf component's client/server nature); `export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic'`, `Cache-Control: no-store`, `Suspense` boundaries, dynamic `import()`, rewrites, and route groups do not affect it (none alter the route's own segment definition or the resolved request URL this mechanism keys off); a *query-string* token on a tokenless route has the identical defect, confirmed by the same `addSearchParamsIfPageSegment` source, not by analogy. No documented, supported page-level mechanism to suppress this serialization was found. A **page.tsx-only correction is therefore rejected** as structurally impossible for any design that places the raw token in the request path or query string of a Next.js App Router page. D-034 Stage 5e's own failing assertion is preserved, unweakened, as the direct security evidence this entry responds to and the regression proof its own remediation must flip to passing. The token briefly, incompletely redacted during this investigation's own diagnostic process belonged to a `heritage_v3_test`-only, already-cleaned-up disposable fixture invitation; its underlying row was independently confirmed deleted before this entry was authored, making that specific value permanently inert (resolves to no invitation, in a database never reachable from `heritage_v3_dev`, staging, or production); no tracked file contains it.

  **2. Authoritative replacement route.** The only accepted portal-activation link shape, superseding D-034 §5's `/activate/[token]` preference and exercising the contingency D-034 §5 itself already authorized ("preferring `/activate/[token]` unless implementation evidence demonstrates a safer, equally compatible alternative"), is:
  ```
  /activate#token=<24-character-token>
  ```
  The token itself is unchanged in every respect except its transport position: still exactly 24 characters over the alphabet `A-Z`, `a-z`, `0-9`, `-`, `_` (D-034 §4's existing 144-bit `generateRandomString`/`hashInvitationToken` design, entirely untouched by this entry). `features/invitations/resend-adapter.ts`'s `buildActivationUrl(rawToken)` must construct exactly this fragment shape (`${base}/activate#token=${encodeURIComponent(rawToken)}`, replacing its current `${base}/activate/${encodeURIComponent(rawToken)}` template) — the sole authorized change to that function's output shape; its existing `BETTER_AUTH_URL`-trailing-slash handling and `encodeURIComponent` use are otherwise unchanged. The old `/activate/[token]` App Router route is **removed**, not redirected: a server-side redirect necessarily requires the path-embedded token to reach the server as a normal request first — the exact origin/access-log exposure this entry closes — before it could ever be reissued into a fragment, so a redirect would reopen precisely the gate this entry closes. Any invitation link already issued in the pre-remediation path format is **not compatible** with the new route once it is removed; every such outstanding invitation must be explicitly revoked or reissued (D-034 §3/§4's already-existing revoke/reissue transitions, unchanged) before or as part of deploying this remediation — this entry does not claim, and implementation must not represent, that an old-format link continues to work.

  **3. GET /activate contract.** `GET /activate` is a fixed, static, tokenless activation shell superseding D-034 §5's/D-037 §3's described GET behavior in full:
  - It receives no token under any circumstance — a URL fragment is a client-side-only construct (RFC 3986 §3.5) that browsers never include in the HTTP request line, headers, or body of any request they send, for any request method, to any server, including this one.
  - It contains no dynamic route segment and no token query parameter.
  - It performs **no** `PortalInvitation` lookup, no call to `getActivationPageState` or any other activation service function, and no other database read of any kind.
  - It performs **no** `RateLimitBucket` write — it does not call `headers()`, `resolveRequestSource()`, or `checkSourceRateLimit()`. The passive GET **throttled UI state is removed entirely**, superseding every D-037 clause that required it (§3's throttled-response description, §11's GET-side SOURCE check, §14's throttled UI state). Ordinary public-page request-volume protection for this now-static, non-mutating, zero-database-read shell belongs to deployment/infrastructure-layer controls, not this application's database-backed limiter — that limiter remains reserved for the two POST operations that actually process credentials and mutate state (Section 5).
  - It does not, and structurally cannot, determine whether any invitation is valid, opened, expired, revoked, or already activated — every one of those determinations moves entirely to the POST routes, which already re-check eligibility fresh, transaction-locally, on every request (D-037 §5/§8, unchanged).
  - It always renders the identical, fixed, non-enumerating Continue state — never a passive "not-found"/generic-invalid branch driven by a server-side lookup, since no lookup occurs.
  - It remains free of third-party scripts, analytics, fonts, and beacons of any kind (D-034 §5, unchanged).
  - It retains `Cache-Control: no-store` and `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` (D-034 §5, unchanged) — required and verified exactly as today, via `next.config.ts`'s scoped `headers()` matcher, corrected from `/activate/:token*` to the exact static path `/activate` (D-037 §13, narrowed).
  - Because it performs no request-specific server work, it **may** be statically rendered — a genuine, structural relaxation from today's `force-dynamic` requirement — **provided** implementation produces the same class of live-response evidence D-034 Stage 5e already established: a real HTTP request against a running server proving the required headers are present and no token substring appears anywhere in the response, for whichever rendering mode is actually selected. This entry does not itself select `force-dynamic` vs. static rendering; that choice, and its own live evidence, belongs to D-038b/c.

  **4. Client token handling.** `ActivationForm` (or its replacement under the new route) must:
  - Read the token transiently from `window.location.hash` only at the moment Continue or Activate is actually invoked — never on initial render, never automatically.
  - Accept only the exact fragment syntax `#token=<24-character-token>` matching the existing token-shape alphabet; anything else is treated identically to a missing fragment.
  - Never place the token in component props, React state, a ref's persisted value, a hidden input, a `data-*` attribute, a cookie, `localStorage`, `sessionStorage`, rendered visible text, an error message, a log call, or a filename — the identical discipline D-037 §4 already established for `window.location.pathname`, now applied to `window.location.hash`.
  - Transmit it only inside the same-origin JSON body of `POST /api/activation/continue` and `POST /api/activation/activate` — no other request, header, or query parameter ever carries it.
  - Preserve the existing synchronous pending-action guard (blocking a second submission before a `useState` update commits) and the existing preservation of submitted password values on a field-validation failure (`.claude/rules/frontend.md`) — both unchanged in mechanism, merely re-verified against the new token source.
  - Continue to use `window.location.replace('/login?activated=1')` (never `push`) after a successful activation — unchanged.
  - Render the identical generic invalid state for a missing, malformed, expired, revoked, already-activated, or otherwise unusable token — collapsing every one of those causes indistinguishably, exactly as D-034 §6's anti-enumeration requirement already establishes for every other layer of this flow.
  - Preserve the token across a recoverable password-validation failure and across a SOURCE- or TOKEN-dimension rate-limit rejection from either POST route, so the user can retry without needing to re-open the original link — the token is re-read fresh from `window.location.hash` on each retry, never cached in state, so this is a property of the read-fresh-each-time design, not new state to introduce.
  - Never automatically invoke Continue on page load (D-034 §5, unchanged).
  - Repeat visits to an already-`INVITATION_OPENED` invitation may now show the Continue button again rather than a server-determined skip straight to the password form — the pre-remediation server-side "eligible-opened" initial state is removed (Section 3), superseding D-037 §14's description of that state. This is safe and accepted: Continue's own idempotent no-op behavior against an already-opened invitation (D-037 §6, unchanged) makes a redundant click harmless — no additional transition, no additional audit row.

  **5. POST and rate-limit contract.** Both activation POST routes (`/api/activation/continue`, `/api/activation/activate`) are **unchanged in every principle** — Origin validation; case-insensitive `Content-Type: application/json` enforcement; the 4096-byte body-size cap; SOURCE-dimension-before-TOKEN-dimension rate-limit ordering; `activationTokenSchema`'s existing 24-character shape validation against the POST body's `token` field; every anti-enumeration behavior (D-034 §6); `Cache-Control: no-store`/`Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`; the atomic activation transaction (D-034 §6, D-037 §8); and every existing audit rule (D-037 §7). The token still arrives via POST body exactly as today — this entry changes nothing about how either POST route reads, validates, hashes, rate-limits, or acts on it. The **only** change to the rate-limiting design is the removal of the GET-side `checkSourceRateLimit()` call named in Section 3, superseding D-037 §10's ordering description only insofar as it referenced the GET page's own SOURCE check, and D-037 §11's SOURCE-limiting requirement only insofar as it applied to GET. Both POST-side rate-limit dimensions (SOURCE and TOKEN), and their existing ordering *within each POST route's own request-processing sequence*, are entirely unaffected and remain fully enforced.

  **6. Deployment gates.** Recording precisely, correcting this entry's own earlier draft discovery report (which described GET-side SOURCE rate limiting as still necessary while also describing the Hostinger trusted-source gate as POST-only — an internal contradiction this entry resolves by removing the GET-side check entirely, per Section 3):

  **Closed by design, no further verification required:**
  - Raw-token exposure in the GET request's path.
  - Raw-token exposure in the GET request's query string.
  - Raw-token exposure in Next.js's HTML, RSC/Flight hydration payload, or any App Router router-state serialization — structurally impossible once the token is never part of any request the server receives (Section 1).
  - Raw-token exposure in normal proxy/origin GET access logs — nothing sensitive ever appears in any part of the GET request Hostinger's reverse proxy, or any infrastructure layer, observes.
  - Raw-token Referer leakage from the activation document — already independently closed by the existing `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` header, which governs every outgoing request a browser initiates from that document (including the Continue/Activate `fetch()` calls), fragment or not; unaffected by, and not dependent on, this entry's route change.

  **Still open, not resolved by this entry:**
  - D-037 §12's Hostinger trusted-source/reverse-proxy header-trust verification — narrowed by this entry to apply exclusively to SOURCE-dimension rate limiting on the two activation POST routes (Section 5), since GET no longer performs any rate-limit check at all.
  - Confirmation that Hostinger/proxy/origin **request-body** logging is disabled, or does not capture, `/api/activation/continue` and `/api/activation/activate` specifically. This entry does **not** claim, and no later stage may claim, that infrastructure can never observe the raw token: both POST requests necessarily transmit it in their JSON bodies (Section 5) — this was already true before this entry and remains true after it; this entry closes the *GET/path/query/RSC/access-log* exposure class only, never the fact that the token must reach the server at all at redemption time. Application-level logging of the token remains prohibited everywhere (D-034 §5, unchanged); staging/production verification must independently confirm request-body logging is off for these two specific endpoints before either is exposed outside a local/disposable environment.
  - Whether Resend's automated-delivery product preserves a URL fragment through any click-tracking, link-rewriting, or redirect behavior it may apply to outbound email links. This is **not verified** by this entry — automated delivery remains disabled in every environment today (D-034 §12, unchanged, independently confirmed via `isAutomatedDeliveryEnabled()`'s existing fail-closed design), so this is a must-verify-before-enabling condition, not a currently live exposure. If direct verification against Resend's own current, official documentation or dashboard configuration cannot establish that fragments survive intact, then either click-tracking/link-rewriting must be explicitly disabled for activation emails specifically, or automated delivery must remain unavailable — this entry authorizes neither guessing at nor assuming Resend's behavior. The audited manual-email channel (the only channel in active use today) is unaffected by this question, since staff copy the raw link directly with no wrapping.

  **7. Narrow supersession map.** This entry supersedes or narrows exactly the following D-037 clauses, and no others; D-034 through D-037 are preserved unedited as historical record:
  - **§3** — the GET route's described behavior (SOURCE-check → invitation lookup → eligible-form-or-generic-invalid render, and the GET-side 200 throttled-state response) is replaced by Section 3 above (no check, no lookup, always the fixed Continue state).
  - **§4** — token transport via `window.location.pathname`/a path-matching regex is replaced by `window.location.hash` (Section 4 above); D-037 §4's own "empirical, live-response gate" requirement is preserved and strengthened — its evidence is what this entry responds to, and D-038b/c's own live-response proof is what closes it.
  - **§5** — the GET's "safe transport" description is narrowed: it remains safe (still performs no mutation), but no longer performs a lookup at all rather than a safe read-only lookup.
  - **§10** — the described request-processing order for the POST routes is unaffected; only the sentence describing a GET-side SOURCE check preceding body-read logic (which never applied to GET's own processing order to begin with, since GET has no body) is clarified as not applicable to GET under this entry.
  - **§11** — the SOURCE-dimension rate-limiting requirement is narrowed to apply to the two POST routes only; the GET-side application of it is removed (Section 3/5 above).
  - **§12** — the two deployment gates are re-recorded exactly as Section 6 above states, closing the GET/path/query/RSC/access-log exposure class and narrowing the trusted-source gate to POST-only.
  - **§13** — the `next.config.ts` header matcher is corrected from `/activate/:token*` to the exact static path `/activate`.
  - **§14** — the UI-state contract is narrowed: no server-determined "eligible-not-opened" vs. "eligible-opened" vs. "not-found" initial GET render; always the fixed Continue state, with the throttled state removed.
  - **§15** — the required live-E2E evidence is extended: in addition to the items already named, evidence must now also prove the GET request reaches the server carrying no token, that no dynamic token path or query route exists, and that the new fragment-based flow's every state (missing/malformed fragment, repeat Continue, password-validation recovery, both POST rate-limit dimensions) behaves per Sections 4–5 above.
  - **§16** — the file inventory is replaced by Section 8 below.
  - **§17** — Stage 5's remaining guarded sub-stages (5e's own live-evidence completion, 5f) are superseded by this entry's own guarded stages (Section 10 below); Stage 5 as a whole remains blocked on this entry's own implementation and validation.

  Everything else in D-034 through D-037 is **preserved, unedited, and unaffected**, including without limitation: D-034 §5's direct activation-POST architecture (no `ActivationSession` table, no stateless activation cookie/nonce — this entry does not reopen that decision, and achieves its own security goal without introducing any new session-adjacent state); D-034 §4's token generation, hashing, and seven-calendar-day expiry; D-034 §3/§4's resend/reissue rotation semantics; D-034 §6's atomic activation transaction and every collision/anti-enumeration rule; D-034 §9's audit-action contract; D-037 §7's `AuditLogActorKind` invariant; D-037 §11's TOKEN-dimension rate-limiting design and its already-obtained Prisma-`upsert()`-atomicity evidence; and D-037 §15's artifact-retention protections for token-bearing tests (trace/screenshot/video `off`, the tag-isolated expected-failure probe and its harness).

  **8. Exact implementation inventory.** Verified by a fresh, read-only repository search performed while authoring this entry — not estimated:

  *Production route relocation/removal:*
  - `apps/web/src/app/activate/[token]/page.tsx` — removed; replaced by a new `apps/web/src/app/activate/page.tsx` implementing Section 3.
  - `apps/web/src/app/activate/[token]/_components/ActivationForm.tsx` — removed; replaced by a new `apps/web/src/app/activate/_components/ActivationForm.tsx` implementing Section 4.
  - `apps/web/src/app/activate/[token]/page.test.tsx` — removed; replaced by a new `apps/web/src/app/activate/page.test.tsx`.
  - `apps/web/src/app/activate/[token]/_components/ActivationForm.test.tsx` — removed; replaced by a new `apps/web/src/app/activate/_components/ActivationForm.test.tsx`.

  *Invitation URL generation:*
  - `apps/web/src/features/invitations/resend-adapter.ts` — `buildActivationUrl`'s template (Section 2).
  - `apps/web/src/features/invitations/resend-adapter.test.ts` — exactly one assertion, `expect(buildActivationUrl('abc-123')).toBe('http://localhost:3000/activate/abc-123')` (line 87 as of this entry), updated to the fragment shape. No other assertion in this file references the URL shape.
  - `apps/web/src/features/invitations/service.test.ts` — its `buildActivationUrl` mock (`vi.fn(() => 'http://localhost:3000/activate/mock-token')`) is an independently-mocked test double for the invitation service's own business logic; not required to change for correctness, recommended for shape consistency.
  - `apps/web/src/features/invitations/service.integration.test.ts` — its `buildActivationUrl` mock implementation (two call sites) is likewise an independent test double; not required to change for correctness, recommended for shape consistency.
  - `apps/web/src/features/activation/service.integration.test.ts` — its `buildActivationUrl` mock and its own `extractToken` helper (currently `manualInvitationUrl.split('/').pop()!`) parse a path-shaped mock URL; not required to change for the mock's own internal consistency (it can remain path-shaped, since it is independent of the real function), but its `extractToken` helper must be reconciled with whatever shape D-038c's own E2E evidence needs it to match if this file is exercised alongside real `buildActivationUrl` output anywhere.
  - `apps/web/src/app/admin/clients/[id]/_components/PortalInvitationPanel.test.tsx` — verified: none of its assertions depend on the URL's internal shape (they assert only that the opaque `manualInvitationUrl` string the component receives is never persisted to storage, never logged, and is cleared on a new send/resend attempt — `describe('PortalInvitationPanel — manualInvitationUrl security')`, and the earlier fixture-only usages). No assertion requires a change; its literal fixture strings (e.g. `'http://localhost:3000/activate/secret-raw-token'`) are path-shaped today and should be updated to the fragment shape for realism, not because any assertion requires it.

  *Activation dead-code removal:*
  - `apps/web/src/features/activation/service.ts` — `getActivationPageState` and the `ActivationPageState` type, no longer called by anything once `page.tsx` no longer performs a server-side lookup.
  - `apps/web/src/features/activation/service.test.ts` — every test of `getActivationPageState` removed together with the function.
  - Confirmed by search: `getActivationPageState`/`ActivationPageState` appear in exactly these four files and no others (`page.tsx`, `page.test.tsx`, `service.ts`, `service.test.ts`).

  *Configuration:*
  - `apps/web/next.config.ts` — the `headers()` matcher's `source: '/activate/:token*'` corrected to `source: '/activate'`.

  *Stage 5e adaptation (the seven paths authorized under D-037 Stage 5e remain otherwise unchanged until D-038c):*
  - `apps/web/e2e/activation.spec.ts` — token extraction changes from path-parsing to fragment-parsing; the Cache-Control/Referrer-Policy GET sub-check targets plain `/activate`; the eligible-page-fetch/token-absence sub-check's semantics shift from "prove this specific invitation's token is absent" to "prove no regression ever reintroduces a token-bearing GET" (structurally guaranteed once Section 3 is implemented, retained as defense-in-depth); the currently-failing token-absence assertion itself is preserved, unweakened, and is expected to flip to passing.
  - `apps/web/e2e/support/artifact-safety-probe.spec.ts` — the probe's navigation target changes to the fragment shape; because the server can no longer distinguish "no token" from "invalid token" on GET, the probe must add one real Continue-click step (a harmless POST using the non-persisted, still-never-redeemable canary) before it reaches the same generic-invalid state its deliberate failure already asserts against.
  - `apps/web/e2e/support/verify-artifact-safety.ts` — confirmed to require **no change**: it already computes and cleans up a TOKEN-dimension `RateLimitBucket` row keyed by the canary's own digest (`canaryTokenHash`), which is unused-but-harmless under the current GET-only probe design and becomes load-bearing, without any code change, once the probe's added Continue-click causes a real TOKEN-dimension write.
  - `apps/web/e2e/support/run-e2e.ts` — confirmed to require **no change**: its `--grep-invert @expected-failure-probe` exclusion and `ACTIVATION_RATE_LIMIT_HMAC_SECRET` threading are independent of the activation page's URL shape.
  - `apps/web/playwright.config.ts` — confirmed to require **no change**: its secret validation/threading and absence of any config-level `grep`/`grepInvert` are independent of the activation page's URL shape.
  - `apps/web/e2e/support/test-database.ts` — confirmed to require **no change**: its RPC allowlist additions (`portalInvitation`, `clientProfile`, `rateLimitBucket`) are keyed by `clientId`/bucket key, not by URL shape.
  - `apps/web/package.json` — confirmed to require **no change**: the `test:e2e:verify-artifact-safety` script name and command are independent of the activation page's URL shape.

  No `schema.prisma` change, and no migration, is authorized or expected by this entry or by any stage it guards.

  **9. Validation contract.** D-038b/c's own validation must run and honestly report: touched-file Prettier verification against the exact authorized file set; `pnpm test`; `pnpm typecheck`; `pnpm lint`; `pnpm --filter web run test:integration`; `pnpm --filter web run test:e2e`; `pnpm --filter web run test:e2e:verify-artifact-safety`; `pnpm build`; repository-wide `pnpm format:check`, reported honestly (never relabeled as a scoped pass, and any newly-implicated file distinguished from the pre-existing baseline); `git diff --check`; and the exact `git status`. Required new or updated evidence, each produced directly rather than assumed: `buildActivationUrl` produces the fragment format; a real GET request to `/activate` reaches the server carrying no token in any part of the request; the real HTML/RSC response contains no raw token substring, verified via the same live-response technique D-034 Stage 5e already established; no dynamic token path segment or token query route exists anywhere in the route tree; a missing or malformed fragment produces the generic invalid UI state without ever issuing a Continue/Activate request; Continue transmits the token only in its POST body; Activate transmits the token only in its POST body; repeat Continue against an already-opened invitation remains idempotent (no additional transition, no additional audit row); password-validation field-level behavior remains correct and preserves submitted values; both POST-side rate-limit dimensions remain enforced, in their existing order; GET creates no `RateLimitBucket` row of any kind; the fragment token is absent from rendered DOM, component state, application logs, error output, the RSC payload, the Playwright JSON report, screenshots, video, traces, and every retained artifact; the full, genuine browser-driven activation happy path succeeds end to end; D-034 Stage 5e's own existing token-absence assertion flips from its currently reproducible failure to a genuine pass, without the assertion itself having been weakened, skipped, inverted, or marked expected at any point in this process; and the expected-failure artifact-safety harness continues to prove its probe genuinely ran, genuinely failed at its own deliberate assertion, and retained no canary substring anywhere.

  **10. Guarded stages.** This entry authorizes only the first of four:
  - **D-038a** — this entry: the documentation-only proposed decision (complete as of acceptance).
  - **D-038b** — route/link/client-transport implementation (Section 8's production and dead-code-removal paths) and their own lower-tier (unit/component) test coverage.
  - **D-038c** — Stage 5e's live-E2E and artifact-safety-harness adaptation (Section 8's Stage-5e-file paths) and the full validation contract of Section 9.
  - **D-038d** — a Correction and Security Review Pass, mirroring this repository's established two-pass practice for prior stages, before any staging, commit, or pull request.

  Only once D-038d passes may the project return to D-034 Stage 5's own remaining sub-stage, **Stage 5f**. D-034 Stage 6 (CLIENT-role login routing and the production-quality `/client` overview) remains blocked until Stage 5f itself completes — this entry does not advance, shorten, or reorder that existing dependency.

  **11. Closure and constraints.** This entry does not implement any code, route, component, test, or configuration change. It does not claim the security defect identified in Section 1 is fixed — it remains open, with D-034 Stage 5e's own assertion left genuinely failing, until D-038b/c's implementation and validation prove otherwise. It does not mark D-034 Stage 5, Stage 5e, or any part of Phase 3 complete. It does not modify `docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md`. It does not close either remaining D-037 §12 deployment gate named in Section 6 above, nor the new Resend fragment-preservation question this entry itself names. It does not enable automated email delivery. It does not authorize exposing activation, in any form, to staging or production.

- **Rationale:** Recording this as a new decision, rather than editing D-034 or D-037 in place, preserves both as accurate historical records of what was authorized and known at the time, while giving this security-driven, evidence-triggered correction its own durable, evidence-backed home — mirroring every prior D-034-lineage entry's identical stated rationale for the same choice, and especially warranted here given that this entry exists specifically because a later stage's own required live evidence contradicted an earlier stage's implementation. Selecting the URL-fragment transport (Section 2) over every other option considered — a Route Handler in place of the React page, a query-string token, a cookie/token-exchange design, or any form of response-body filtering or undocumented framework flag — reflects that only the fragment design is backed by a structural, universal guarantee (a URL fragment is never transmitted to any server, by the HTTP/URL specification itself) rather than a mitigation that must be independently re-verified against every future framework version or infrastructure change. Rejecting the cookie/token-exchange family without a positive requirement to do so honors D-034 §5's own explicit, deliberate, previously-recorded rejection of that architecture rather than silently reopening a settled decision merely because a different problem needed solving. Removing the GET-side database rate limiter entirely, rather than retaining it alongside a tokenless GET, resolves a genuine internal contradiction this entry's own drafting process surfaced — a check that reads no per-request-distinguishing data and gates no lookup or mutation provides no security benefit proportionate to the trusted-source-header dependency it would otherwise inherit, and ordinary public-page volume protection is a deployment-layer concern this repository's own Hostinger target is better positioned to provide than an application-level database write on every page view. Explicitly declining to claim infrastructure can never observe the token (Section 6) keeps this entry honest about what a transport-layer fix can and cannot certify — the two POST requests still carry it, exactly as D-034 §5's own original rationale already accepted as a residual, deliberate trade-off of the link-based single-use-token design as a whole.
- **Constraint:** No `schema.prisma` change, no migration, no dependency installation, and no database mutation of any kind is authorized or performed by this entry. No production file, test, configuration, Task Board entry, environment file, or the seven paths authorized under D-037 Stage 5e are modified by this entry — Section 8 describes the *inventory* a later, separately authorized implementation stage (D-038b/c) must affect, not a change this entry itself makes. `docs/HERITAGE_V3_TASK_BOARD.md` is not modified by this entry. D-034 and D-037 are not edited — Section 7 records this entry's supersession of specific clauses without altering either entry's own text. The implementation diff for this entry is exactly one path: `docs/HERITAGE_V3_DECISIONS_LOG.md` itself.
- **Deferred:** Every file named in Section 8 (D-038b/c); the Correction and Security Review Pass (D-038d); D-034 Stage 5f and Stage 6; D-034 Stage 7 and Stage 8; both deployment gates and the Resend fragment-preservation question of Section 6; and any staging/production exposure decision, which remains independently gated on all of the above regardless of this entry's own acceptance.
- **Effect:** Authorizes D-038a only — the documentation record itself. Does not implement, move, delete, or modify any application file. Does not mark D-034 Stage 5, Stage 5e, Phase 3, or any Task Board item complete or corrected. D-034 Stage 5 remains **FAIL** pending D-038b through D-038d. **Superseded on the "Stage 5 remains FAIL" status only** by accepted D-039 (§11), effective once D-039 is committed and merged into `main`.
